# ASN-0094: Typed Relation Shapes

*Restricting the relational primitive into a predicate language.*

ASN-0086 establishes typed relations `L_K` with the three operations Emit, Observe, Nullify, governed by the lemma family R0…R7a (concretely: R0, R0a, R0a-Cor1, R0a-Cor2, R1, R2, R3, R4, R5, R5-Cor, R6a, R6b, R6c, R6c-Corollary, R7a, together with the auxiliary lemma LinkStoreInvarianceUnderArrangement). The relational structure as defined there is too permissive to support a typed predicate vocabulary: F and G can be any finite endsets whose coverage lies anywhere in `T`, so a predicate over `L_K` has no fixed signature. Every predicate would have to take a pattern in `℘(T) × ℘(T)` and return Bool, "two relations of the same shape" would not be expressible, and slot accessors `from(τ)`, `to(τ)` would be partial in a way that defeats type-checking.

We are looking for the minimum additional layer that closes this gap. The answer is a single structural decision per type: each `K ∈ T_cat` is assigned a *shape* — a tuple of constraints on cardinality, target domain, and idempotency — and the substrate enforces shape-conformance at Emit time. From the shape, a predicate template family is mechanically organized (though not mechanically derived; see Sh5). The pipeline is:

> R0…R7a (typed relations + operations) → **shape restrictions** (this document) → predicate template families → composed predicates

Shapes are not derivable from R0…R7a. They are an additional design decision the substrate makes about which relations it admits and what `(F, G)` pairs each admits. We justify each constraint by what predicate forms it makes possible, and by what the substrate cannot express without it.


## Scope and Substrate Scaffolding

*Arity scope.* This framework restricts the standard-triple slice `L^Σ` of `dom(Σ.L)` — the arity-3 links collected by ASN-0086's `L^Σ` definition. Higher-arity links admitted by L3 (ASN-0043) are outside its scope: the cardinality and target-domain shape components are defined over two slots only (F and G), and the slot-accessor and template machinery presupposes the arity-3 structure. Extending the framework to higher arities would require additional shape components per extra slot, which we do not pursue here.

*Emit_K routing commitment.* The framework is a discipline imposed by a relational layer atop ASN-0086. Every class-(iii) emission of a type `K ∈ T_cat` is committed to route through `Emit_K`; non-`Emit_K` class-(iii) invocations of these types are outside the framework's scope. Sh-conf below binds `Emit_K` (the relational-layer operation), not K.λ (the substrate primitive — K.λ remains permissive at the substrate level: any `F, G ∈ Endset` with `K ∈ T_admissible` is admissible to K.λ). The inductive arguments for Sh0–Sh3 invoke the *Emit_K routing commitment* to conclude that every new tuple in `L_K^Σ` for `K ∈ T_cat` arrived via an `Emit_K` call subject to Sh-conf.

*Naming convention for distinct framework commitments.* The framework operates under several distinct, non-interchangeable commitments. Each carries an explicit name to prevent conflation:
- *Emit_K routing commitment* (this paragraph): every class-(iii) `K ∈ T_cat` emission routes through `Emit_K`. Distinct from any conformance content.
- *Sh4 idempotency contract* (defined in the Sh4 section as the *Layer-discipline contract*): the layer's Observe-then-Emit protocol clauses (i)–(iii) for `shape(K).idem = ⊤` types.
- *FDD functional-dependency contract* (defined in the FunctionalDependencyDiscipline section): the layer's at-most-one-tuple-per-from-slot Observe-then-Emit protocol, strictly stronger than Sh4.
- *Single-home commitment* (defined in the SingleHomeCoverageDiscipline section): a per-K registration constraint that every K-emission uses one fixed home `d_K`.
- *Unit-depth retraction discipline* (ASN-0086): every `L_R^Σ` tuple's G is a single unit-depth span; this framework derives the discipline as a consequence of the Retraction shape rather than re-imposing it.

Subsequent text uses these names consistently. Where ambiguity could arise (e.g., a step in a preservation proof invokes more than one commitment), each commitment is cited by its explicit name at the citation site.

*Substrate-conforming-layer scaffolding.* The framework operates atop a *substrate-conforming layer* (ASN-0086, Definition). Where the framework's proofs and definitions require properties of `dom(Σ.C)`, `dom(Σ.L)`, and `dom(Σ.M)` not derivable from ASN-0034/ASN-0043/ASN-0086 alone, they consume them through the substrate-conforming-layer interface. ASN-0086's `SubstrateConformingLayer` Definition enumerates the full substrate invariant and chain-discipline catalog that a conforming layer must preserve; the following scaffolding clauses surface the specific properties this ASN cites by name without re-attributing them to the layer's internal numbered invariants. The label *substrate-conforming-layer scaffolding* (or just *the scaffolding clauses*) is the framework's name for the union; earlier drafts called this *content-side scaffolding*, but the union spans both link-side, content-side, and document-side properties — five of the ten clauses below are not strictly content-side — so the name is retired in favor of the substrate-conforming-layer reading.

- *Element-level content addresses.* Every `a ∈ dom(Σ.C)` is T4-valid (in the sense of T4, ASN-0034) with `zeros(a) = 3` and `#E(a) ≥ 2`. (Content-side analog of L1, L1b, and L1c from ASN-0043; T4-validity is the content-side counterpart to L1c's derivation via T10a.4 on the link side, and is required to apply T4's last-position non-zero clause `t_{#t} ≠ 0` at content addresses — e.g., in AllocatedAddressAntichain's Step 3.2.)
- *Content subspace partition.* There is a fixed subspace identifier `s_C ∈ ℕ` with `s_C > 0` and `s_C ≠ s_L` such that `E(a).1 = s_C` for every `a ∈ dom(Σ.C)`. (Symmetric to L0 from ASN-0043, with `s_C ≠ s_L` distinct. The positivity `s_C > 0` is forced by the element-level content-address clause's `zeros(a) = 3`: a constant `s_C = 0` would make every content address carry a fourth zero at the first E-field position, contradicting `zeros(a) = 3`.)
- *Link subspace partition.* There is a fixed subspace identifier `s_L ∈ ℕ` with `s_L > 0` such that `E(a).1 = s_L` for every `a ∈ dom(Σ.L)`. (This scaffolding clause is a local commitment of the substrate-conforming layer that is consistent with L0 from ASN-0043 — L0 introduces an abstract identifier function `subspace_I(·)` and states `subspace_I(a) = s_L`; the scaffolding fixes the layer-local identification `subspace_I(·) = E(·).1` on element-level addresses, making the link-side and content-side subspace partitions symmetric and directly comparable at the first element-field component. The identification is introduced here, not imported from upstream. The positivity `s_L > 0` is forced by L1's `zeros(a) = 3` for every `a ∈ dom(Σ.L)`, by the same argument as for `s_C > 0`. *Layer-commitment status of the identification.* The identification `subspace_I(·) = E(·).1` on element-level addresses is *not derivable* from L0 alone — L0 treats `subspace_I(·)` as an uninterpreted projection. Any layer instantiating the framework therefore commits to this identification at its interface, and any consumer reasoning at L0's abstract level must verify the framework's claims against the layer-local identification before consuming them. The framework's preservation theorems and the AllocatedAddressAntichain lemma rest on this identification — concretely, AllocatedAddressAntichain's Case 3 reads `E(x).1 = s_L` and `E(a).1 = s_C` directly from link-side and content-side membership at Steps 3.3a/3.3b, a step that has no derivation from L0's abstract `subspace_I(·) = s_L` without the layer-local identification. A substrate that surfaces `subspace_I` via a different physical projection (e.g., a non-`E(·).1` accessor) lies outside the framework's scope; the framework's preservation theorems and the AllocatedAddressAntichain lemma make no claims at such a substrate, and a port would need to re-derive both against the alternative identification.)
- *Content-store antichain.* `dom(Σ.C)` is a tumbler-prefix antichain at every reachable state: `(A a, a' ∈ dom(Σ.C) :: a ≼ a' ⟹ a = a')`. (Content-side symmetric to R0a from ASN-0086.)
- *Content-store monotonicity.* `dom(Σ.C) ⊆ dom(Σ'.C)` for every transition `Σ ↦ Σ'`. (Content-side symmetric to L12a from ASN-0043; content addresses are never deallocated.)
- *Content-store finiteness.* `dom(Σ.C)` is finite at every reachable state. (Content-side symmetric to L-fin from ASN-0043 — the link-side finiteness fact — supplied by the substrate-conforming layer.)
- *Document address structure.* Every `d ∈ dom(Σ.M)` is T4-valid with `zeros(d) = 2` — i.e., a document-level tumbler with two field-separator zeros (after node and user fields), no element-field zero. This is the property of document addresses surfaced by the substrate-conforming layer; the framework cites it directly without unpacking the chain through any specific numbered substrate invariant.
- *Per-document link sub-allocator chains.* For each `d ∈ dom(Σ.M)` the substrate-conforming layer supplies a link sub-allocator whose output chain enumerates `{ℓ : home(ℓ) = d}` under T9 (ForwardAllocation, ASN-0034); this is the same chain enumeration referenced by ASN-0086's R0a-Cor1 and FreshEmissionAddress.
- *Uniform link sub-allocator chain length.* All outputs of a single document's link sub-allocator share the same tumbler length: for every `d ∈ dom(Σ.M)` and every pair `ℓ_1, ℓ_2` in the chain at `d`, `#ℓ_1 = #ℓ_2`. (Property of the chain discipline supplied by the substrate-conforming layer; the framework cites this scaffolding name in proofs that need to compare two chain elements' lengths.)
- *Link sub-allocator chain-index function.* For each `d ∈ dom(Σ.M)` and each `ℓ` in the chain at `d`, the substrate-conforming layer supplies a total function `chain_index(ℓ, d) ∈ ℕ` such that `ℓ = inc^{chain_index(ℓ, d)}(d.0.s_L.1, 0)` — the unique non-negative integer indexing `ℓ`'s position in the link sub-allocator's enumeration. Well-defined and single-valued by T10a.7 (EnumerationInjectivity, ASN-0034) applied to the chain at `d`; total because the lemma's hypothesis "`ℓ` in the chain at `d`" supplies a chain index by construction. The framework cites this scaffolding name when downstream templates and disciplines (e.g., `emission_order` in the Coverage walkthrough) need a named accessor onto the chain-index machinery rather than appealing to implicit chain enumeration.

We refer to these collectively as *the scaffolding clauses* (equivalently, *the substrate-conforming-layer scaffolding*) and cite them by name in proofs below.

*Framework-wide layer commitment to the `subspace_I(·) = E(·).1` identification.* The `subspace_I(·) = E(·).1` identification surfaced by the *Link subspace partition* and *Content subspace partition* scaffolding clauses above is adopted as a *framework-wide invariant*: every framework theorem, lemma, definition, and template — including AllocatedAddressAntichain, Sh0–Sh4 (and their consequences, e.g., the totality of `slot_addrs(·)` on `A^Σ`-resident addresses), the per-K disciplines (*Sh4 idempotency contract*, *FDD functional-dependency contract*, *single-home commitment*), Sh5's template families and their per-row audit, the layer composites, and every worked example below — operates under this identification. Earlier drafts surfaced the identification as a per-lemma qualifier at AllocatedAddressAntichain alone, leaving downstream consumers (Sh4 idempotency contract clause (i.a), single-home contract preservation theorems, the *Codomain convention for partial templates*, and the per-shape template walkthroughs) to inherit the qualifier implicitly when invoking AllocatedAddressAntichain or any of its consequences (notably "every address in `A^Σ` is element-level"). The present draft promotes the identification to framework-wide commitment scope: any layer instantiating the framework commits to `subspace_I(·) = E(·).1` on element-level addresses *once*, at the framework's interface, and every downstream framework result holds under this commitment without per-citation re-qualification. Consumers reasoning at L0's abstract `subspace_I(·)` level must verify the layer-local identification before invoking any framework result; the framework makes no claims at substrates surfacing `subspace_I` via a different physical projection (e.g., a non-`E(·).1` accessor on element-level addresses), and any port to an alternative identification would need to re-derive every framework result against the alternative reading. The per-lemma qualifier paragraphs that appear at AllocatedAddressAntichain below are retained as exposition (they surface the identification's role in the lemma's specific proof) but are no longer load-bearing for the lemma's admission — the lemma's preconditions reduce to the bare `x ∈ A^Σ` hypothesis under the framework-wide commitment, with the qualifier paragraphs serving only to point a reader at the named invariant rather than declaring a separate conditional.

*The framework, defined.* Throughout this document, "the framework" denotes the shape discipline atop ASN-0086 introduced by this ASN. It comprises five concrete components: (1) the conformance axiom **Sh-conf** (a layer-level precondition added to ASN-0086's `Emit_K`), (2) the preservation lemmas **Sh0, Sh1, Sh2, Sh3** (cardinality and target-domain invariants over `L_K^Σ`, each proved by induction on `↦*`), (3) the idempotency theorem **Sh4** (proved under the *Sh4 idempotency contract*) together with its complement the auxiliary lemma **LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit** and the corollary **EffectiveWpSimplification**, (4) the META catalog **Sh5** (the per-shape template families and the citation-audit discipline that gates new catalog rows), and (5) the four layer-discipline contracts named in *Naming convention for distinct framework commitments* above (the *Emit_K routing commitment*, *Sh4 idempotency contract*, *FDD functional-dependency contract*, and *single-home commitment*) together with the **substrate-conforming-layer scaffolding** interface this section enumerates. Subsequent prose referring to "the framework" cites the union of these components; when only one is in scope (e.g., "Sh-conf admits the call" or "the *Sh4 idempotency contract* fires clause (ii)"), the more specific name is used. "The framework's preservation theorems" refers to Sh0–Sh4 (and the per-K discipline preservation theorems where contextually clear). *Conditional status reminder.* Sh4, FunctionalDependencyDiscipline, and SingleHomeCoverageDiscipline are each *theorems under their named layer-discipline contract* (the *Sh4 idempotency contract*, the *FDD functional-dependency contract*, and the *single-home commitment* respectively) — not substrate-enforced axioms. Each preservation theorem's statement carries an explicit "conditional on" tag at its theorem heading; subsequent prose that aggregates them as "the framework's preservation theorems" is shorthand for the conjunction of those conditional theorems, with the implicit understanding that any layer breaking a contract loses the corresponding theorem at the resulting state. Sh0–Sh3 are not conditional in the same way — their conclusions hold under the *Emit_K routing commitment* alone (every class-(iii) emission of `K ∈ T_cat` routes through `Emit_K`); the routing commitment is recorded as a framework commitment but applies to every K in scope rather than per-K.

*Locally derived NAT primitives.* AllocatedAddressAntichain's Step 3.1 and LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit's Step II.0 cite two ℕ-level primitives — *NAT-card* (cardinality of finite ℕ-subsets via strictly-increasing enumerations, with additivity over disjoint unions) and *NAT-sub* (partial subtraction `m − n` defined when `n ≤ m`) — that are not separate axioms in the foundation. Both are derived locally from the foundation's available NAT axioms (NAT-closure, NAT-order, NAT-discrete, NAT-addcompat, NAT-wellorder), supplemented by one explicit Peano-style clause (the appendix's (Peano-rec): `m + (n + 1) = (m + n) + 1`) that the appendix introduces and uses to derive ℕ-commutativity and ℕ-associativity from the listed axioms. The derivations are routed to *Appendix: Local NAT Primitives* at the end of this document; downstream proofs cite NAT-card and NAT-sub by name, with ℕ-commutativity and ℕ-associativity now fully derived within the appendix from the listed axioms + (Peano-rec). A future draft may extend the foundation to enumerate (Peano-rec) explicitly and absorb the appendix into the foundation; the present draft keeps (Peano-rec) localized to the appendix and cites the derived ℕ-commutativity/ℕ-associativity at NAT-sub's invocation sites.


## The Address-Set Projection

Shape constraints operate on a *syntactic* projection of `(F, G)` — the slot-address sets extracted from canonical-form endsets — together with an *allocated-address* projection that bridges the syntactic check to substrate semantics. Two projections matter.

**Definition — Coverage Projection.** For each tuple `(a, F, G) ∈ L_K`:

`cov : L_K → ℘(T) × ℘(T)`

`cov(a, F, G) = (coverage(F), coverage(G))`

where `coverage(·)` is the union of address sets denoted by the endset's spans (Definition, ASN-0043). By PrefixSpanCoverage (ASN-0043), the coverage of a single unit-depth span at `x` is `{t ∈ T : x ≼ t}`, which is *infinite* in `T` by T0(a)/T0(b) (ASN-0034). The set-theoretic cardinality `|coverage(F)|` is therefore infinite for every non-empty canonical-form `F`, so cardinality constraints cannot be stated against `|coverage(F)|` directly.

The address-set view is a lossy projection — by L5 (EndsetSetSemantics, ASN-0043), endsets with different span decompositions can have identical coverage. For shape purposes the loss is intentional: shapes are predicates over what addresses a slot references, not over how those addresses are denoted.

**Definition — AllocatedCoverage.** For an endset `F` and reachable state `Σ`:

`cov_allocated(F, Σ) := coverage(F) ∩ A^Σ`

where `A^Σ = dom(Σ.C) ∪ dom(Σ.L)` is the address universe at Σ (ASN-0086). This set is finite at every Σ (since `A^Σ = dom(Σ.C) ∪ dom(Σ.L)` is finite by the content-store finiteness scaffolding for `dom(Σ.C)` and L-fin (ASN-0043) for `dom(Σ.L)`) and monotone non-decreasing along `⊑̂`: `Σ ⊑̂ Σ'` entails `cov_allocated(F, Σ) ⊆ cov_allocated(F, Σ')` because `A^Σ ⊆ A^{Σ'}` and `coverage(F)` is a pure function of the endset value.

**Definition — CanonicalSlotForm.** An endset `F` is in *canonical-slot form* iff there exists a finite set `X_F ⊆ T` such that

`F = {(x, δ(1, #x)) : x ∈ X_F}`

The elements of `X_F` are the *slot addresses* of `F`. `X_F` is uniquely recoverable from any canonical-form `F` by reading the start address of each unit-depth span; equivalently, `X_F = {s ∈ T : (E (s, ℓ) ∈ F :: ℓ = δ(1, #s))}` is a well-defined set-valued function of `F`. The comprehension recovers exactly `X_F` because `F`'s span set is `{(x, δ(1, #x)) : x ∈ X_F}` by the canonical-slot equation, so a span has start `s` iff `s = x` for some `x ∈ X_F` iff `s ∈ X_F`; the comprehension's witness `(s, ℓ) ∈ F` forces `ℓ = δ(1, #s)` by canonical form, with no ambiguity in the displacement (every span in `F` has its displacement determined by its start). We write `slot_addrs(F) = X_F`.

`|slot_addrs(F)|` is a finite natural number (since `F` is a finite endset by ASN-0043's `Endset = ℘_fin(Span)`). For canonical-form `F`, `coverage(F) = (∪ x ∈ X_F : {t : x ≼ t})` — infinite in `T` when `X_F ≠ ∅`; what shape constraints check is the finite syntactic `slot_addrs(F)`.

The shape framework restricts every shape-conformant emission's `F` and `G` to canonical-slot form. The substrate as defined by ASN-0043 permits non-canonical endsets (L4); the shape framework rejects non-canonical emissions via Sh-conf below. This is a discipline imposed by the framework, not a substrate-level constraint.

**Lemma — AllocatedAddressAntichain.** For every reachable state `Σ` at a substrate-conforming layer (which, by the framework-wide commitment surfaced in *Scope and Substrate Scaffolding*, honors the `subspace_I(·) = E(·).1` identification), and every `x ∈ A^Σ`:

`cov_allocated({(x, δ(1, #x))}, Σ) = {x}`

*Element-level character of `A^Σ`.* The hypothesis `x ∈ A^Σ` suffices to invoke the lemma: every address in `A^Σ = dom(Σ.C) ∪ dom(Σ.L)` is element-level with non-empty element field. The link side `dom(Σ.L)` is element-level by L1 (LinkElementLevel, ASN-0043) with `zeros(·) = 3` and `#E(·) ≥ 2` by L1b. The content side `dom(Σ.C)` is element-level by the element-level content-address clause of the substrate-conforming-layer scaffolding (Scope and Substrate Scaffolding). Both sides rest on the framework-wide `subspace_I(·) = E(·).1` invariant (established once, framework-wide, in the *Framework-wide layer commitment* paragraph of Scope and Substrate Scaffolding); the "every address in `A^Σ` is element-level" claim is consumed throughout the framework — Sh4 idempotency contract clause (i.a)'s allocated-set arguments, the *Codomain convention for partial templates* (which presupposes `#x ≥ 1` for canonical-slot span construction at `b ∈ A_rel^Σ` per R5/L13), the single-home contract's preservation arguments, every per-shape template walkthrough — under the same framework-wide invariant, with no per-citation re-qualification needed. Span well-formedness `(x, δ(1, #x))` under T12 (SpanWellDefinedness, ASN-0034) holds because `#x ≥ 1` for every `x ∈ T` (T0, ASN-0034), so the bare hypothesis `x ∈ A^Σ` (under the framework-wide commitment) suffices for both the span well-formedness check and the element-level case analysis below.

*Proof.* `coverage({(x, δ(1, #x))}) = {t : x ≼ t}` by PrefixSpanCoverage. The intersection with `A^Σ` is `S := {a ∈ A^Σ : x ≼ a}`. By Prefix reflexivity (ASN-0034), `x ∈ S`. For the reverse, fix `a ∈ S`; we show `a = x` by case on the domains.

*Case 1* (`x, a ∈ dom(Σ.L)`): By R0a (FlatLinkDomain, ASN-0086), `dom(Σ.L)` is a tumbler-prefix antichain, so `x ≼ a ⟹ x = a`.

*Case 2* (`x, a ∈ dom(Σ.C)`): By the content-store antichain assumption (Scope and Substrate Scaffolding above), `dom(Σ.C)` is a tumbler-prefix antichain, so `x ≼ a ⟹ x = a`.

*Case 3* (`x` and `a` lie in different domains). Sub-case 3a treats `x ∈ dom(Σ.L), a ∈ dom(Σ.C)`; Sub-case 3b treats `x ∈ dom(Σ.C), a ∈ dom(Σ.L)`. By L1 (LinkElementLevel, ASN-0043) and L1c (LinkAllocatorConformance, ASN-0043) on the link side — which via T10a.4 supplies T4-validity for every link-side address — together with the element-level content-address scaffolding clause on the content side — which supplies T4-validity directly for every content-side address — both `x` and `a` are element-level (`zeros(x) = zeros(a) = 3`) and T4-valid in either sub-case; Step 3.2 below establishes E-field non-emptiness (`#E(·) ≥ 1`) from T4's last-position non-zero clause `t_{#t} ≠ 0` alone (well-formed because both `x` and `a` are T4-valid), so both `E(x).1` and `E(a).1` are well-defined.

*Case-symmetry across Sub-cases 3a and 3b.* Sub-cases 3a and 3b share the hypothesis `x ≼ a` and discharge Steps 3.1 and 3.2 identically: those two steps quantify over the positional structure of `x` and `a` (their zero positions, their E-field offsets, their componentwise agreement under the prefix relation) without reference to which side is link versus content, so both steps deliver identical conclusions in both sub-cases. The two sub-cases diverge only at Step 3.3, where the subspace partition scaffolding clauses assign `E(·).1` based on domain membership. *Dependence audit on Step 3.3.* Step 3.3 consumes domain membership at exactly three sites: (1) assigning `E(x).1` (link side ⇒ `s_L`; content side ⇒ `s_C`); (2) assigning `E(a).1` (symmetric); (3) invoking the disjointness predicate `s_L ≠ s_C`. Sites (1) and (2) are pure projections — each reads a single literal subspace identifier off `x`'s (resp. `a`'s) domain via the *Link subspace partition* / *Content subspace partition* scaffolding clauses, with no inter-dependence between the two assignments and no consultation of Σ-indexed state beyond domain membership. Site (3) consults the disjointness predicate `s_L ≠ s_C`, which is *symmetric in its two arguments* (`≠` is a symmetric relation on ℕ), so the swap reduces to a single identity move: `s_L = s_C` ⟺ `s_C = s_L`, both equally contradicting the scaffolding's disjointness. Steps 3.1 and 3.2 are written once and apply to both sub-cases; Step 3.3 is written out explicitly for *both* sub-cases below (Steps 3.3a and 3.3b) to make the three substantive moves visible at each side without relying on a "by symmetry" gesture — the explicit version of each is six lines total and surfaces the side-label swap as concrete identifier substitutions rather than as a meta-comment.

*Step 3.1 — Shared zero positions.* The prefix `x ≼ a` gives `#x ≤ #a` and componentwise agreement `aᵢ = xᵢ` for `1 ≤ i ≤ #x`. Both `x` and `a` carry `zeros(·) = 3` (the Case 3 hypothesis on element-level addresses), so the zero-index set `Z_x := {i : 1 ≤ i ≤ #x ∧ xᵢ = 0}` has cardinality 3; let its three elements in strict order be `n_1 < n_2 < n_3` (NAT-card supplies the strictly-increasing enumeration of finite ℕ-subsets — see *Appendix: Local NAT Primitives* — and the strict-monotone enumeration is unique). Componentwise agreement at positions `n_1, n_2, n_3 ≤ #x` gives `aₙ₁ = aₙ₂ = aₙ₃ = 0`, so `{n_1, n_2, n_3} ⊆ Z_a := {i : 1 ≤ i ≤ #a ∧ aᵢ = 0}`. Both sides have cardinality 3 (the right side by the Case 3 hypothesis `zeros(a) = 3`), and NAT-card's subset-with-equal-cardinality identity (Appendix) then forces `Z_a = {n_1, n_2, n_3}` — `a`'s three zero positions coincide with `x`'s. This step holds in both sub-cases because it consumes only the prefix relation `x ≼ a` and the shared-`zeros = 3` constraint, neither of which references domain membership.

*Step 3.2 — E-field first-position agreement.* The E-field of `x` is non-empty (we need only `#E(x) ≥ 1`, not the stronger `≥ 2` from L1b or its content-side analog, since Step 3.2's conclusion uses only the first position of `E(x)`): T4's last-position non-zero clause `t_{#t} ≠ 0` applied to `x` gives `x_{#x} ≠ 0`, while `x_{n_3} = 0`, so `n_3 ≠ #x`; combined with `n_3 ≤ #x` (since `n_3` is a position of `x`) this gives `n_3 < #x`, i.e., `#x − n_3 ≥ 1`. The E-field's positional range `n_3 + 1 .. #x` and the resulting length identity `#E(x) = #x − n_3` are read off T4a (SyntacticEquivalence, ASN-0034) — T4a identifies field segments as maximal contiguous non-zero sub-sequences delimited by the zeros, fixing the E-field as the segment after the third zero `n_3` — combined with T4b (UniqueParse, ASN-0034), which makes that segment a uniquely computable projection of `x`, and T4c (LevelDetermination, ASN-0034), which identifies the `zeros = 3` case with the four-field (N, U, D, E) hierarchy so the segment after `n_3` is labeled as the E-field. So `#E(x) ≥ 1`, and the first E-field position `E(x).1` is defined. The same E-field non-emptiness holds for `a` by the symmetric application of T4's last-position non-zero clause at `a`. By T4a + T4b + T4c (ASN-0034) applied independently to `x` and `a` — both element-level and sharing the same three zero positions `n_1 < n_2 < n_3` — the E-field of `x` occupies positions `n_3 + 1 .. #x` and the E-field of `a` occupies positions `n_3 + 1 .. #a` (T4a supplies the segment-between-zeros formula; T4b supplies uniqueness; T4c supplies the level-to-segment labeling); the index offset `E(·).j = ·_{n_3 + j}` for `1 ≤ j ≤ #E(·)` follows from the same triple. The componentwise agreement `xᵢ = aᵢ` on `1 ≤ i ≤ #x` from `x ≼ a`, instantiated at `i = n_3 + 1` (which satisfies `n_3 + 1 ≤ #x` since `#E(x) ≥ 1`), yields `x_{n_3 + 1} = a_{n_3 + 1}`; substituting the T4a + T4b + T4c index offset on both sides (taking `j = 1`) gives `E(x).1 = E(a).1`. This step holds in both sub-cases because the T4-validity citations and the T4a + T4b + T4c E-field structure apply uniformly to element-level addresses without reference to subspace identifier.

*Step 3.3a — Subspace contradiction (Sub-case 3a: `x ∈ dom(Σ.L), a ∈ dom(Σ.C)`).* The link subspace partition scaffolding gives `E(x).1 = s_L` directly (`x ∈ dom(Σ.L)`); the content subspace partition scaffolding gives `E(a).1 = s_C` directly (`a ∈ dom(Σ.C)`); the *Content subspace partition* scaffolding clause fixes `s_L ≠ s_C`. But Step 3.2 gives `E(x).1 = E(a).1`. Substituting, `s_L = s_C`, contradicting the scaffolding's disjointness. Sub-case 3a vacuous.

*Step 3.3b — Subspace contradiction (Sub-case 3b: `x ∈ dom(Σ.C), a ∈ dom(Σ.L)`).* The content subspace partition scaffolding gives `E(x).1 = s_C` directly (`x ∈ dom(Σ.C)`); the link subspace partition scaffolding gives `E(a).1 = s_L` directly (`a ∈ dom(Σ.L)`); the *Content subspace partition* scaffolding clause fixes `s_C ≠ s_L`. But Step 3.2 gives `E(x).1 = E(a).1`. Substituting, `s_C = s_L`, contradicting the scaffolding's disjointness. Sub-case 3b vacuous. The three substantive moves (subspace assignment on `x`, subspace assignment on `a`, disjointness invocation) parallel Sub-case 3a's Step 3.3a with the identifier names exchanged at sites (1) and (2); site (3)'s disjointness predicate `s_L ≠ s_C` is symmetric and licenses the same contradiction in both readings (per the *Dependence audit* in the Case-symmetry preamble above).

Both sub-cases of Case 3 are vacuous. ∎

*Worked example — Case 3 (cross-domain) walkthrough.* To exhibit the cross-domain contradiction concretely, fix subspace identifiers `s_C = 5` and `s_L = 7` (any two distinct positive naturals work; we pick small values for legibility). Take `x = [1, 0, 2, 0, 1, 0, 7, 1] ∈ T` — a tumbler of length 8 with zeros at positions 2, 4, 6 (so `zeros(x) = 3`), satisfying T4 (no adjacent zeros, `x_1 = 1 ≠ 0`, `x_8 = 1 ≠ 0`). Suppose `x ∈ dom(Σ.L)`: T4b places `E(x) = [x_7, x_8] = [7, 1]` with `#E(x) = 2`, satisfying L1b's `#E(·) ≥ 2` and R0a-Cor2's `#E(·) = 2`; by the *Link subspace partition* scaffolding clause, `E(x).1 = s_L = 7`, consistent with `x_7 = 7`. Now take `a = [1, 0, 2, 0, 1, 0, 7, 1, 5]` — extending `x` by one component with value 5 — and suppose toward contradiction that `a ∈ dom(Σ.C)` and `x ≼ a`. This instantiates Sub-case 3a (`x ∈ dom(Σ.L)`, `a ∈ dom(Σ.C)`), matching the sub-case the formal proof walks. The proof's Steps 3.1, 3.2, 3.3a unfold as follows.

*Step 3.1 (concrete).* The zero-index set of `x` is `Z_x = {2, 4, 6}`; NAT-card at `n = #x = 8` with `|Z_x| = 3` yields the strictly-increasing enumeration `n_1 = 2, n_2 = 4, n_3 = 6`. Componentwise agreement on positions 1..8 (from `x ≼ a`) gives `a_2 = x_2 = 0`, `a_4 = x_4 = 0`, `a_6 = x_6 = 0`, so `a` carries zeros at the same three positions. Inspecting `a` directly: positions 1, 3, 5, 7, 8, 9 carry `1, 2, 1, 7, 1, 5` (all nonzero), so no zero exists at position 9 or in `{1, 3, 5, 7, 8}`. Hence `zeros(a) = 3`, consistent with `a` being element-level per the element-level content-address scaffolding clause, and `a`'s three zero positions coincide with `x`'s — `{2, 4, 6}`.

*Step 3.2 (concrete).* `n_3 + 1 = 7`. T4b places `E(x)` at positions `7..#x = 7..8`, so `E(x) = [x_7, x_8] = [7, 1]` and `#E(x) = 2`; T4b places `E(a)` at positions `7..#a = 7..9`, so `E(a) = [a_7, a_8, a_9] = [7, 1, 5]` and `#E(a) = 3`. Componentwise agreement at position 7 (from `x ≼ a` at `i = 7 ≤ #x = 8`) gives `a_7 = x_7 = 7`, i.e., `E(a).1 = E(x).1 = 7`.

*Step 3.3a (concrete).* By the *Link subspace partition* scaffolding clause applied to `x ∈ dom(Σ.L)`, `E(x).1 = s_L = 7`. By the *Content subspace partition* scaffolding clause applied to `a ∈ dom(Σ.C)`, `E(a).1 = s_C = 5`. Step 3.2 forces `E(x).1 = E(a).1`, so substituting concrete values yields `7 = 5`, contradicting the scaffolding's disjointness `s_L ≠ s_C`. The supposition `a ∈ dom(Σ.C) ∧ x ≼ a` is therefore impossible — and at any choice of distinct `s_C, s_L`, by the same argument with the literal subspace values renamed. Sub-case 3a is vacuous at this example, matching the proof's general conclusion.

Sub-case 3b (`x ∈ dom(Σ.C), a ∈ dom(Σ.L)`) admits an *alternative length-based argument* that supplements the formal subspace-contradiction route above. The argument combines an asymmetry between the two sides of the substrate-conforming-layer scaffolding: R0a-Cor2 (ASN-0086) strengthens L1b on the link side to `#E(a) = 2` for every `a ∈ dom(Σ.L)`, while the *Element-level content addresses* scaffolding clause (Scope and Substrate Scaffolding) supplies only the weaker `#E(x) ≥ 2` for `x ∈ dom(Σ.C)` — the substrate-conforming layer does not commit to a symmetric `#E(·) = 2` strengthening on `dom(Σ.C)`. From Step 3.1's zero-position coincidence `Z_x = Z_a = {n_1, n_2, n_3}` (applied independently of subspace assignment, as the Case-symmetry preamble notes) and T4a + T4b + T4c's E-field positional identity (E-field at positions `n_3 + 1 .. #(·)`, so `#(·) = n_3 + #E(·)`), R0a-Cor2 gives `#a = n_3 + #E(a) = n_3 + 2` and the content-side scaffolding gives `#x = n_3 + #E(x) ≥ n_3 + 2`. The prefix relation `x ≼ a` supplies `#x ≤ #a` (Prefix, ASN-0034), so the inequality chain `n_3 + 2 ≤ #x ≤ #a = n_3 + 2` collapses to `#x = #a = n_3 + 2`. Componentwise agreement of `x` and `a` on positions `1 .. #x = #a` (from the prefix relation, exhausted once `#x = #a`) then yields `x = a` by T3 (CanonicalRepresentation, ASN-0034) — but `x ∈ dom(Σ.C)`, `a ∈ dom(Σ.L)`, and the disjointness `dom(Σ.C) ∩ dom(Σ.L) = ∅` (a direct consequence of L14, ASN-0043, combined with the *Content subspace partition* scaffolding clause that places every content address in L14's `s_C`-scoped slice) make this impossible. The argument therefore closes Sub-case 3b through a different chain than Step 3.3b's E-field-first-position subspace contradiction: it consults R0a-Cor2 and Step 3.1 to force length equality, then invokes L14's disjointness. The chain still depends on the subspace partition through L14 (L14's disjointness is itself the substrate's subspace-based disjointness fact, scoped via the *Content subspace partition* scaffolding clause), so the argument is not strictly subspace-partition-free — it relocates the subspace consultation from `E(·).1` agreement (Step 3.3b) to L14's `s_C`-scoping while exposing R0a-Cor2's link-side length constraint as additional structural redundancy. The general subspace-contradiction route (Steps 3.1, 3.2, 3.3b) remains in the formal proof above because the lemma is stated against the bare L1b hypothesis (where `#E(·) ≥ 2` does not force length equality on either side), but no concrete worked example is supplied for Sub-case 3b: on the substrate-conforming layer the case is unreachable by either route, and any illustrative example would necessarily exhibit a configuration the substrate never produces. Readers wanting the symbol-by-symbol parallel to Sub-case 3a may apply Steps 3.1, 3.2, 3.3b directly to any L1b-admissible cross-domain pair; the formal text supplies the argument independently of any worked instance.

The lemma underwrites the syntactic-to-semantic bridge: a canonical-slot endset at an allocated address `x` denotes exactly `{x}` among allocated addresses, so `slot_addrs(F) = {x}` matches "what allocated address does this slot refer to" with no ambiguity. Without this lemma, "the slot at `x`" could resolve to multiple allocated addresses when `x` has allocated descendants — which is precisely what the antichain rules out at element level.


## Shape

**Definition — Shape.** A *shape* is a tuple

`Sh_K = (c_F, c_G, t_F, t_G, idem)`

with components:

- `c_F, c_G ∈ {0, 1, *, 0|1}` — *cardinality bounds* on the slot-address counts `|slot_addrs(F)|` and `|slot_addrs(G)|`. The values 0 and 1 are exact; `*` denotes "any natural number"; `0|1` denotes "0 or 1 exactly."
- `t_F, t_G` — *target-domain restrictions*. Each is one of the symbolic constants `A_doc`, `A_rel`, `A`, or the distinguished value `-` (used when the corresponding cardinality is `0`). At each state Σ the symbol expands to the corresponding allocated set: `A_doc ↦ A_doc^Σ = dom(Σ.C)`, `A_rel ↦ A_rel^Σ = dom(Σ.L)`, `A ↦ A^Σ = A_doc^Σ ∪ A_rel^Σ`, `- ↦ -^Σ = ∅` (the empty set, used only when the corresponding cardinality is `0`, in which case `X_F ⊆ ∅ ⟺ X_F = ∅`, which holds by `c_F = 0`).
- `idem ∈ {⊤, ⊥}` — the *idempotency flag*.

**Definition — ShapeWellFormedness.** A shape `Sh_K = (c_F, c_G, t_F, t_G, idem)` is *syntactically well-formed* iff all four of the following implications hold:

- `c_F = 0 ⟹ t_F = -`
- `t_F = - ⟹ c_F = 0`
- `c_G = 0 ⟹ t_G = -`
- `t_G = - ⟹ c_G = 0`

The shape registry admits only well-formed shapes. The cardinality side of each implication tests the *literal* registry value `0`, not the broader set `{0, 0|1}`: the values `0` and `0|1` are distinct entries in `{0, 1, *, 0|1}`, so the antecedent `c_F = 0` is false when `c_F = 0|1`. Similarly, `t_F = -` is the literal registry value `-`, distinct from `A_doc`, `A_rel`, and `A`.

*Behavior at `c_F = 0|1`.* Neither `c_F = 0` fires (since `0|1 ≠ 0`) nor `t_F = -` fires at a `c_F = 0|1` row: `t_F = -` at a `c_F = 0|1` row would violate the well-formedness implication `t_F = - ⟹ c_F = 0` (antecedent `t_F = -` holds, consequent `c_F = 0` fails since `0|1 ≠ 0`), so ShapeWellFormedness excludes such rows from the registry. The exclusion is semantically motivated, not merely syntactic: at `t_F = -`, clause (d) of Sh-conf expands to `slot_addrs(F) ⊆ -^Σ = ∅`, which forces `slot_addrs(F) = ∅` — making the non-empty branch of `c_F = 0|1` (where `|slot_addrs(F)| = 1`) uninstantiable, since no `slot_addrs(F)` can simultaneously be a singleton and a subset of `∅`. The implication `t_F = - ⟹ c_F = 0` therefore enforces the discipline that `t_F = -` is reserved for shapes whose F-slot is *always* empty (`c_F = 0`), not shapes whose F-slot is *sometimes* empty (`c_F = 0|1`). Both implications on the F-side are vacuously satisfied at a well-formed `c_F = 0|1` row; `t_F` may take any of `A_doc, A_rel, A`. Thus the Provenance shape `(1, 0|1, A, A, ⊤)` is well-formed: the G-side antecedents `c_G = 0` and `t_G = -` are both false (`0|1 ≠ 0` and `A ≠ -`), so both G-side implications are vacuously satisfied. When the slot is empty at emission time (the `0` branch of `0|1`), clause (d) of Sh-conf reads `∅ ⊆ A^Σ`, vacuously true; when the slot is non-empty (the `1` branch), clause (d) enforces `slot_addrs ⊆ A^Σ` normally. The same reading applies to any future shape carrying `c_X = 0|1` paired with a non-`-` target domain.

*Why the constraint matters.* Without these implications, two registry entries with operationally equivalent meaning — e.g., `(c_F = 0, t_F = A_doc)` and `(c_F = 0, t_F = -)`, both rendering clause (d) of Sh-conf vacuous on F since `slot_addrs(F) = ∅ ⊆ X` for any X — could disagree on the registered shape value. That disagreement would break the per-class constancy of `shape(·)` and the downstream catalog reasoning that case-splits on the registered shape tuple (e.g., the canonical shape catalog below indexes template families by shape; two operationally equivalent but syntactically distinct entries would index different rows).

**Definition — CardinalityMatch.** For `n ∈ ℕ` and `c ∈ {0, 1, *, 0|1}`:

`match(n, c) ≡ (c = 0 ∧ n = 0) ∨ (c = 1 ∧ n = 1) ∨ (c = * ∧ n ∈ ℕ) ∨ (c = 0|1 ∧ n ∈ {0, 1})`

**Definition — TypedRelationCatalog.** Fix a distinguished set `T_cat ⊆ T_admissible` *finite up to `~`* (equivalently, the quotient `T_cat / ~` is finite) that is *closed under coverage-equivalence* (ASN-0086, `~` definition): `K ∈ T_cat ∧ K ~ K' ⟹ K' ∈ T_cat`. Equivalently, `T_cat` is the union of finitely many `~`-equivalence classes; each class is itself infinite as an endset set (many endsets share the same coverage by L5, ASN-0043), but only finitely many classes appear in the catalog. Concretely, `T_cat` is specified by listing one representative per class, with closure under `~` implicit. "Finite distinguished set" in earlier drafts is misleading: a non-empty `~`-class has infinitely many endset members, so `T_cat` itself is not finite as a set of endsets — finiteness lives at the quotient level.

*Decidable membership.* Because `T_cat` is closed under `~` and finite at the quotient level, the predicate `K ∈ T_cat` is the coverage-class membership test `[K] ∈ T_cat / ~` — equivalently, "there exists `K'` in the registered representative list with `K ~ K'`". The test is decidable on arbitrary `K ∈ T_admissible` by checking `coverage(K) = coverage(K_rep)` against each of the finitely many registered representatives `K_rep`: coverage is a pure function of the endset value (Definition — Coverage, ASN-0043), and coverage-equality of two finite span sets is decidable per the derivation below. The membership predicate is therefore *not* a literal-equality test on the endset value `K` itself (which would reject every coverage-equivalent endset whose value differs from the listed representative); it is a coverage-equivalence test against the representative list. Subsequent prose referring to "the membership test `K ∈ T_cat`" or to "the literal membership test" abbreviates this coverage-equivalence check; the word "literal" applies to the registry's representative list (literally enumerated) and to the well-definedness of the check (no state-indexed quantification), not to the comparison operation on endsets (which is coverage-equivalence, not value-equality).

*Decidability of coverage-equality on finite span sets.* For any finite endset `E = {(s_1, ℓ_1), ..., (s_n, ℓ_n)}`, each span `(s_i, ℓ_i)` denotes the half-open interval `I_i := {t ∈ T : s_i ≤ t < s_i ⊕ ℓ_i}` under T1 (LexicographicOrder, ASN-0034), well-formed by T12 (SpanWellDefinedness, ASN-0034). `coverage(E) = ⋃_{1 ≤ i ≤ n} I_i` is a finite union of such intervals. To test `coverage(E) = coverage(E')` for two finite endsets `E`, `E'`, the procedure is: (1) compute the two endpoint sets `{s_i, s_i ⊕ ℓ_i : 1 ≤ i ≤ n}` and `{s'_j, s'_j ⊕ ℓ'_j : 1 ≤ j ≤ n'}` — finite by finiteness of `E`, `E'`, and each endpoint computable from T1 and TumblerAdd's constructive definition (ASN-0034); (2) sort the union of endpoints under T1 (decidable by T2, IntrinsicComparison, ASN-0034) into a finite ordered partition of the tumbler line into intervals delimited by consecutive endpoints; (3) for each delimited interval, test whether it lies in `coverage(E)` (by checking if any `I_i` contains a representative of the interval — the left endpoint suffices, again decidable by T1/T2) and symmetrically in `coverage(E')`; (4) `coverage(E) = coverage(E')` iff every delimited interval has the same inclusion outcome on both sides. The procedure terminates in time polynomial in `n + n'` and uses only T1/T2/T12/TumblerAdd primitives. Hence coverage-equality on arbitrary finite span sets is decidable, with no restriction on span widths or canonical form required at the registered-representative list. (Restricting registered representatives to canonical-slot form — `E = {(x, δ(1, #x)) : x ∈ X_E}` — would simplify the procedure further: under canonical form, coverage-equality reduces to equality of the prefix-antichain projections of `X_E` and `X_{E'}` under `≼`, also decidable but via a different argument. The catalog does not require canonical form on registered representatives, so the more general procedure above is the one the membership test relies on.)

*Worked example — coverage-equality on a non-canonical-form representative pair.* The procedure's correctness on non-canonical-form inputs is exhibited here against two endsets with structurally distinct span decompositions and identical coverage. Take `E := {([5], [3])}` — a single span whose displacement `[3]` is *not* in canonical-slot form (canonical-slot at `s = [5]` would require `δ(1, #s) = δ(1, 1) = [1]`, but the displacement here is `[3]`, with first nonzero value `3 ≠ 1`). The span's endpoints are `s = [5]` and `s ⊕ ℓ = [5] ⊕ [3] = [8]` (TumblerAdd at action point 1 sums the components), so the interval is `I = {t : [5] ≤ t < [8]}`. Take `E' := {([5], [1]), ([6], [1]), ([7], [1])}` — three canonical-slot spans (each displacement is `δ(1, 1) = [1]`) covering the same range as `E` by decomposing it into three unit-depth pieces. Both endsets are well-formed finite span sets, and `E ≠ E'` literally (different cardinalities and different span decompositions), so this is a genuine non-canonical-form test of the procedure rather than a re-formulation of canonical-form set-equality. The procedure runs as follows. *Step (1) — endpoint computation.* Endpoints of `E`: `{[5], [5] ⊕ [3]} = {[5], [8]}`. Endpoints of `E'`: `{[5], [5] ⊕ [1], [6], [6] ⊕ [1], [7], [7] ⊕ [1]} = {[5], [6], [6], [7], [7], [8]} = {[5], [6], [7], [8]}` (after de-duplication). *Step (2) — sorted union and partition.* Under T1, the sorted union is `[5] < [6] < [7] < [8]`. The tumbler line is partitioned into the five intervals delimited by consecutive endpoints: `(−∞, [5])`, `[[5], [6])`, `[[6], [7])`, `[[7], [8])`, `[[8], +∞)`. *Step (3) — per-interval inclusion testing.* For each delimited interval, the left endpoint serves as the representative (or any element within the interval, for the open-on-the-left first interval); membership in each coverage is decidable by T1/T2 against each `I_i`.

| Interval | Representative | In `coverage(E)`? | In `coverage(E')`? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `(−∞, [5])` | `[4]` | `[4] < [5] = s_1`, so `[4] ∉ I_1`; not in coverage. ✗ | `[4] < [5] = s'_1`; not in any `I'_j`. ✗ |
| `[[5], [6])` | `[5]` | `[5] ≤ [5] < [8] = s_1 ⊕ ℓ_1`, so `[5] ∈ I_1`; in coverage. ✓ | `[5] ≤ [5] < [6] = s'_1 ⊕ ℓ'_1`, so `[5] ∈ I'_1`; in coverage. ✓ |
| `[[6], [7])` | `[6]` | `[5] ≤ [6] < [8]`, so `[6] ∈ I_1`; in coverage. ✓ | `[6] ≤ [6] < [7] = s'_2 ⊕ ℓ'_2`, so `[6] ∈ I'_2`; in coverage. ✓ |
| `[[7], [8])` | `[7]` | `[5] ≤ [7] < [8]`, so `[7] ∈ I_1`; in coverage. ✓ | `[7] ≤ [7] < [8] = s'_3 ⊕ ℓ'_3`, so `[7] ∈ I'_3`; in coverage. ✓ |
| `[[8], +∞)` | `[8]` | `[8] ≮ [8]`, so `[8] ∉ I_1`; not in coverage. ✗ | `[8] ≮ [8]`, so `[8] ∉ I'_3`; not in `I'_1`, `I'_2` either by direct check; not in coverage. ✗ |

*Step (4) — match outcomes.* Every interval has the same inclusion outcome on both sides (✗, ✓, ✓, ✓, ✗). Therefore `coverage(E) = coverage(E')`. The procedure decides correctly despite the structural mismatch between `E` (one non-canonical span) and `E'` (three canonical-slot spans). *Closure check.* The test consumes only T1 (for sorting and inclusion comparisons), T2 (for the decidability of `≤` and `<`), T12 (for span well-formedness), TumblerAdd (for endpoint computation `s ⊕ ℓ`); no canonical-slot precondition is invoked, and the example exhibits the procedure operating uniformly across canonical-slot and non-canonical-form representatives. The catalog's coverage-equivalence test `K ~ K_rep` against the registered representative list (per the *Decidable membership* paragraph above) therefore admits non-canonical-form `K_rep` registrations without re-formulation; the procedure's general form discharges every catalog membership query at every reachable state.

*Lifetime constancy of `T_cat`.* The set `T_cat` is fixed at the substrate's initial state `Σ_init` and does not change as states evolve: at every reachable state Σ, the registered catalog is the same set `T_cat` declared at `Σ_init`. The lifetime constancy is required for the inductive baselines of Sh0–Sh4 to discharge uniformly. Each induction begins with "At `Σ_0`, every `L_K^{Σ_0} = ∅`; the universal quantifier is vacuous." A K admitted to `T_cat` only after some prior states have elapsed would face a non-vacuous baseline at its registration point — `L_K^{Σ_registered}` could be non-empty from class-(iii) emissions at coverage-equivalent type indices issued before K joined `T_cat` — and the induction's base case would not discharge. The framework forbids runtime extension of `T_cat`: layers that wish to introduce new typed relations must declare them at `Σ_init`, or face the burden of verifying `L_K^{Σ_registered} = ∅` at the registration point (equivalent to the framework's empty-baseline assumption).

For any `K ∈ T_admissible \ T_cat` (equivalently, every member of every class not represented), no shape is registered. The substrate's shape-conformance gate rejects `Emit_K` at unregistered types — the membership test `K ∈ T_cat`, decidable as the coverage-equivalence check against the representative list per the *Decidable membership* paragraph above (see Sh-conf below).

**Definition — ShapeRegistry.** A function

`shape : T_cat → Shape`

assigns each registered type its shape. Two properties:

- *Per-class constancy.* For `K, K' ∈ T_cat` with `K ~ K'`: `shape(K) = shape(K')`. The function `shape` factors through `T_cat / ~`.
- *Lifetime constancy.* `shape` is fixed across the substrate's lifetime; it does not change as states evolve.

*Registration interface.* The layer registers `shape` by the same per-class mechanism `T_cat` uses for membership: for each `~`-equivalence class in `T_cat / ~`, the layer registers one representative endset `K_rep` (the same `K_rep` that appears in `T_cat`'s representative list per the *Decidable membership* paragraph above) together with a shape value `shape(K_rep) ∈ Shape`. The function `shape` is then *defined by extension*: for any `K ∈ T_cat`, the registry resolves `shape(K)` by finding the unique registered representative `K_rep` with `K ~ K_rep` (the same coverage-equivalence test that decides `K ∈ T_cat`) and returning `shape(K_rep)`. Per-class constancy is then *automatic from the registration interface*: any `K, K' ∈ T_cat` with `K ~ K'` resolve to the same representative `K_rep` (by transitivity of `~`) and therefore to the same `shape(K_rep)` value. The layer never needs to verify per-class constancy after registration — the interface enforces it by construction. This makes the constancy property a *consequence* of the registration mechanism rather than an independent invariant the layer must maintain across `~`-equivalent endset values; downstream consumers reasoning about Sh0–Sh4's inductive arguments at coverage-equivalent K's (e.g., the *Emit_K routing commitment* Case B argument that consults `shape(K') = shape(K)` whenever `K ~ K'`) cite per-class constancy and the registration interface jointly delivers it.

Lifetime constancy is a substrate-level commitment, not derivable from R0…R7a. It is what lets Sh-conf evaluate emissions against a stable shape that matches the shape under which prior tuples of the same type were emitted, so the inductive proofs of Sh0–Sh3 can rely on a fixed conformance predicate. Mutable shape re-registration (e.g., relaxing a cardinality bound after some tuples are already emitted) would invalidate the induction; the framework forbids it. The lifetime constancy at the registry level reads as: the representative list `T_cat / ~` and the function `shape ∘ (·/~)` are both fixed at `Σ_init` and do not change as states evolve.

**Definition — Conformance.** A tuple `(a, F, G) ∈ L_K^Σ` (with `K ∈ T_cat`) is *shape-conformant at state Σ* iff all of the following hold:

(a) `F` is in canonical-slot form; let `X_F = slot_addrs(F)`.
(b) `G` is in canonical-slot form; let `X_G = slot_addrs(G)`.
(c) `match(|X_F|, shape(K).c_F) ∧ match(|X_G|, shape(K).c_G)`.
(d) `X_F ⊆ shape(K).t_F^Σ ∧ X_G ⊆ shape(K).t_G^Σ`, with the symbolic `t` expanded per the Shape definition. When `t_F = -` (only legal under `c_F = 0`), the F-side of (d) is vacuously satisfied since `X_F = ∅`; symmetric for G.

Write `conf_K^Σ(F, G)` for this predicate.

*Structural gates.* Clauses (a) and (b) jointly form the **canonical-form gate** (one gate, two operands); clause (c) is the **cardinality gate**; clause (d) is the **target-domain gate**. Three independent gates, each rejecting independently. When the worked examples below refer to "Sh-conf's three independently-checked structural gates", they index the canonical-form, cardinality, and target-domain gates in that order; a non-canonical F (clause (a)) and a non-canonical G (clause (b)) are distinguishable as clause-level failures but both fall under the same gate.

*State-dependence and monotone discharge.* Conformance is state-indexed because clause (d) depends on the allocated sets `A_doc^Σ, A_rel^Σ, A^Σ`. These sets grow monotonically along `⊑̂`: `Σ ⊑̂ Σ'` entails `A^Σ ⊆ A^{Σ'}` and analogous for the partition sets (by L12a, ASN-0043, for `dom(Σ.L)` and by the content-store monotonicity scaffolding assumption (Scope and Substrate Scaffolding) for `dom(Σ.C)`). Therefore `conf_K^Σ(F, G) ⟹ conf_K^{Σ'}(F, G)` for every `Σ ⊑̂ Σ'`: once conformant, a tuple remains conformant under every reachable future state. This monotonicity is what permits the inductive arguments of Sh0–Sh3 to commute with arbitrary `↦*` transitions.


## The Conformance Axiom

**Sh-conf — ShapeConformanceAxiom.** The framework restricts ASN-0086's `Emit_K` (the relational-layer operation) by adding two preconditions: `K ∈ T_cat` and `conf_K^Σ(F, G)`. These are *added* to ASN-0086's existing preconditions; they do not displace them. The combined success condition is:

`Emit_K(Σ, d, F, G)` succeeds iff *ASN-0086's preconditions hold (specifically `d ∈ dom(Σ.M)`, with the regime simplification of `wp_086` below) and `K ∈ T_cat` and `conf_K^Σ(F, G)`*. Equivalently, the framework imposes two new conjuncts atop ASN-0086's `wp_086`: failure of either added conjunct, or failure of any ASN-0086 conjunct, produces `⊥`. The framework extends ASN-0086's `Emit_K` return type from `Σ' × A_rel^{Σ'}` to `(Σ' × A_rel^{Σ'}) ∪ {⊥}` where `⊥` is a distinguished rejection token: on any failure (substrate or framework), `Emit_K` returns `⊥` and leaves the state unchanged (no `↦`-step occurs). The layer-discipline contracts of Sh4 and FunctionalDependencyDiscipline below additionally return `⊥` on suppression (clause (ii) of their respective contracts); a caller that needs to distinguish rejection-by-Sh-conf from rejection-by-discipline-suppression may invoke the layer-callable candidate-set queries `C_K(F, G, Σ)` and `C_fd_K(F, Σ)` defined below before issuing the `Emit_K` call. The framework does not impose a finer-grained sum type at the substrate boundary; callers wanting that granularity wrap `Emit_K` using the standard caller-side classification protocol documented below (*Caller-side rejection classification*), which mirrors the framework's internal gate ordering and translates each gate's `⊥` into a layer-specific token.

**Definition — LayerCallableCandidateSets.** For each `K ∈ T_cat`, the framework exposes the following layer-callable candidate-set queries:

`C_K : Endset × Endset × Σ → ℘_fin(A_K^Σ)` defined when `shape(K).idem = ⊤` (i.e., K registered under the *Sh4 idempotency contract*):

`C_K(F, G, Σ) := {τ ∈ A_K^Σ : slot_addrs(F_τ) = slot_addrs(F) ∧ slot_addrs(G_τ) = slot_addrs(G)}`

`C_fd_K : Endset × Σ → ℘_fin(A_K^Σ)` defined when K is registered under the *FDD functional-dependency contract*:

`C_fd_K(F, Σ) := {τ ∈ A_K^Σ : slot_addrs(F_τ) = slot_addrs(F)}`

Both queries are *side-effect-free reads* of the framework's relational state at Σ: each is computed by the same two-step Observe-then-postfilter procedure that the Sh4 idempotency contract's clause (i) and the FDD functional-dependency contract's clause (i) respectively prescribe internally — `C_K(F, G, Σ)` via `Observe_K(slot_addrs(F), slot_addrs(G), oper)` followed by exact slot-pair-equality postfilter, and `C_fd_K(F, Σ)` via `Observe_K(slot_addrs(F), ∅, oper)` followed by exact F-slot-equality postfilter — but exposed under their own framework-level names so that callers may invoke them *outside* the contract's internal computation, before deciding whether to issue `Emit_K`. The well-formedness of the queries on canonical-slot F, G is inherited from Sh0/Sh1 (which make `slot_addrs(F_τ)` and `slot_addrs(G_τ)` finite sets on every τ ∈ A_K^Σ); the Observe step's finite-domain semantics make the post-filter's exact-equality test decidable in finite time on the pattern endsets (which are finite per Endset's `℘_fin(Span)` typing).

These queries are *the* layer-callable disambiguation mechanism: by calling `C_K(F, G, Σ)` ahead of `Emit_K(Σ, d, F, G)`, a caller can predict whether an Sh4-registered K will suppress the call (`C_K ≠ ∅`) and distinguish that from a Sh-conf rejection (which would result from a separate condition: F/G non-canonical, cardinality mismatch, target-domain miss, or K ∉ T_cat). Analogously for `C_fd_K` and FDD-registered K. Callers that decline to invoke the queries pre-emission are still well-served by `Emit_K`'s `⊥` return, but cannot tell rejection-by-Sh-conf from rejection-by-suppression from the `⊥` alone.

*Single-home discipline interaction.* The *single-home commitment* requires no candidate-set query — its pre-emission check is a literal-equality test `d = d_K` against a per-K registration constant — so no analogous `C_sh_K` query is needed. A caller wanting to predict single-home rejection simply tests `d ?= d_K` directly.

*Caller-side rejection classification (the standard wrapping pattern).* The framework returns a single `⊥` token at every rejection branch — registry rejection (`K ∉ T_cat`), canonical-form rejection (Sh-conf clause (a) or (b)), single-home rejection (clause (i) of the *single-home commitment*), discipline-suppression rejection (clause (ii) of the *Sh4 idempotency contract* or *FDD functional-dependency contract*), cardinality rejection (Sh-conf clause (c)), and target-domain rejection (Sh-conf clause (d)). For callers that need finer-grained classification, the framework specifies a standard caller-side wrapping pattern: invoke the following side-effect-free queries in order against the pre-call state, halting at the first failure to identify the gate that would reject. The numbering mirrors the *Gate Ordering (consolidated)* clause below, so a caller running these checks ahead of `Emit_K` predicts the exact gate that fires:

1. *Registry check* — Test `K ∈ T_cat` via the coverage-equivalence check against the registered representative list (per the *Decidable membership* paragraph in the TypedRelationCatalog Definition). Failure ⇒ registry rejection.
2. *Single-home check* (if K registered under SingleHomeCoverageDiscipline) — Test `d ?= d_K` against the per-K registration constant. Failure ⇒ single-home rejection.
3. *Canonical-form check* — Test whether F and G are each in canonical-slot form (per the CanonicalSlotForm Definition; the test is decidable on a finite endset by reading each span's start and verifying its displacement equals `δ(1, #start)`). Failure ⇒ canonical-form rejection.
4. *Discipline-suppression check* (if K registered under the *Sh4 idempotency contract* or the *FDD functional-dependency contract*) — Invoke `C_K(F, G, Σ)` (Sh4) or `C_fd_K(F, Σ)` (FDD) per the LayerCallableCandidateSets Definition above. Non-empty ⇒ discipline-suppression rejection.
5. *Cardinality check* — Test `match(|slot_addrs(F)|, shape(K).c_F)` and `match(|slot_addrs(G)|, shape(K).c_G)`. Failure ⇒ cardinality rejection.
6. *Target-domain check* — Test `slot_addrs(F) ⊆ shape(K).t_F^Σ` and `slot_addrs(G) ⊆ shape(K).t_G^Σ`. Failure ⇒ target-domain rejection.

A caller that passes all six checks may issue `Emit_K(Σ, d, F, G)` and (assuming ASN-0086's substrate-side `d ∈ dom(Σ.M)` precondition holds) expects the substrate K.λ-step to fire. Layers that need a sum-typed return at their own boundary wrap `Emit_K` with this protocol and translate each numbered failure into a layer-specific token; the framework supplies the underlying decidable checks but leaves the sum-type construction to the wrapping layer, so that layers can choose their own granularity (e.g., merge the canonical-form and cardinality checks into a single "structural rejection" token, or expose every gate as a distinct case). The protocol uses only side-effect-free reads of the pre-call state; running it ahead of `Emit_K` introduces no `↦`-step and is safe to compose into a caller's pre-emission validation pipeline.

*Effective weakest-precondition under Sh-conf.* Under the framework's *Emit_K routing commitment*, ASN-0086's `wp_086` collapses; the named result is *Corollary — EffectiveWpSimplification*, established after the Target Domain section (after Sh1 and Sh3 are available as inputs). Downstream proofs cite the corollary directly.

*Stratified proof order.* The framework's proofs are organized as an explicit, acyclic stratification — the document order below reflects the stratification directly, so a reader following the document linearly never reaches a proof site before its consumed lemmas: (1) Sh-conf axiom (this section, posited); (2) Sh0 and Sh1 inductions (Cardinality section, each independently induct on `↦*` against Sh-conf and ASN-0086's upstream R2/R3/LinkStoreInvarianceUnderArrangement); (3) Sh2 and Sh3 inductions (Target Domain section, each consumes Sh0 or Sh1 respectively as a state-indexed lemma for the well-formedness of `slot_addrs(·)` in its universal-quantifier body — see the *Well-formedness* clauses of Sh2 and Sh3 — but its inductive argument over `↦*` does not invoke Sh2/Sh3's own conclusions); (4) LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit lemma (its own section below, independent of Sh0–Sh3 — its proof consumes only ASN-0086's R0a-Cor1/FreshEmissionAddress/L1/L1a, T10a.7/T3/Prefix, the scaffolding clauses, and the Appendix's NAT primitives (NAT-card at Step II.1's zero-count additivity, and NAT-sub at Step II.0's suffix-length definition `#w := #a − #b` and at Step II.1's closing line `zeros(w) = 0`)); (5) EffectiveWpSimplification corollary (after Sh3, consumes Sh1 and Sh3 at `K := R` together with the LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit lemma); (6) Sh4 induction (Idempotency section). Sh4's proof consumes Sh0–Sh3 as state-indexed lemmas throughout, and additionally consumes the *Lemma — RetractionSelfFreshness* — a sub-lemma stated within the Sh4 section before the induction's Base case — at Case C's `K ~ R` sub-case (using part (i) alone) and at Case D's case-description (using the full conclusion `τ_new ∈ A_R^{Σ'}` to establish that the case-description equation `A_R^{Σ'} = (A_R^Σ ∪ {τ_new}) \ leaving` holds as a theorem rather than as a stipulation). RetractionSelfFreshness consumes Sh1 and Sh3 at `K := R` plus LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit; promoting it to a named sub-lemma stratified before Case D separates the structural establishment of "what enters A_R^{Σ'}" from Case D's pairwise-distinctness argument on the established active subset. Sh4's proof does *not* directly invoke EffectiveWpSimplification, since the substrate's `wp_086` is discharged inside the Sh-conf admission path that the proof presupposes rather than re-derived at any of Case A/B/C/D — the EffectiveWpSimplification corollary is the named export of that discharge for downstream consumers, but Sh4's own argument reaches the substrate K.λ-step via the Sh4 idempotency contract clause (iii) plus Sh-conf, not via a textual citation of the corollary. The Sh4 stratification is documented at the Sh4 *Stratification* sub-clause; the same acyclicity claim repeated here clarifies the cross-section dependencies at the document level. No cycle: each stratum's proofs consume only earlier-numbered strata.

The `⊥` rejection corresponds to wp failure on either source: a `K ∉ T_cat` failure (Sh-conf's first conjunct) blocks the wp at the registry boundary; a `¬conf_K^Σ(F, G)` failure (Sh-conf's second conjunct, decomposing into clauses (a)–(d)) blocks the wp at the shape gates; an ASN-0086 wp failure (e.g., `d ∉ dom(Σ.M)`) blocks the wp at the substrate boundary. Each source contributes its own rejection site; the layer's call site reaches the substrate primitive K.λ only when all conjuncts hold simultaneously.

*Scope.* Sh-conf binds `Emit_K`, not the substrate primitive K.λ. K.λ remains permissive at the substrate level: ASN-0086's R0 admits any `(F, G, K)` triple with `K ∈ T_admissible` at a fresh K.λ-emitted address. The framework's discipline is realized through the *Emit_K routing commitment* of Scope and Substrate Scaffolding: every class-(iii) emission of a type `K ∈ T_cat` routes through `Emit_K`, and Sh-conf rejects non-conformant `Emit_K` calls before they reach K.λ. The inductive proofs of Sh0–Sh3 invoke the *Emit_K routing commitment* to conclude that every new tuple in `L_K^{Σ'}` for `K ∈ T_cat` arrived via an `Emit_K` call subject to Sh-conf. Sh-conf gates only `Emit_K` (class iii of ASN-0086's `→`-vocabulary); the framework imposes no precondition on K.σ (document registration, class i) or K.α (content allocation, class ii). These two substrate primitives continue to operate under ASN-0086's contract unmodified — K.σ extends `dom(Σ.M)` and K.α extends `dom(Σ.C)`, neither touches `Σ.L`, and neither is affected by the catalog `T_cat` or the shape registry. *Routing of K-op frame-condition facts.* The K-op frame-condition properties used here — that K.σ touches only `Σ.M`, K.α touches only `Σ.C`, K.λ touches only `Σ.L`, and the two non-affected stores are preserved pointwise per step — are sourced through ASN-0086's `SubstrateConformingLayer` Definition (which catalogs the L/S/M/C invariants and chain-discipline commitments a conforming layer preserves at every step) rather than via any ASN outside this review's foundation (ASN-0034, ASN-0043, ASN-0086); equivalent attribution to the `→ — DomExtendingTransition` Definition in ASN-0086 yields the same frame-condition facts through ASN-0086 alone. The Sh0–Sh3 induction's Case A explicitly subsumes K.σ-steps and K.α-steps as transitions that leave `Σ.L` (and therefore every `L_K^Σ`) pointwise unchanged; the framework's preservation theorems hold uniformly across them by R2, R3, and LinkStoreInvarianceUnderArrangement without any framework-side gate firing.

*Justification.* This is an axiom about the framework's layer-level enforcement, not a theorem derivable from R0…R7a. R0 (TupleAddressFreshness, ASN-0086) alone permits any `(F, G, K)` triple with `K ∈ T_admissible` to be emitted at a fresh address. Sh-conf narrows the admissible triples — those traveling through `Emit_K` — to those whose `F, G` are in canonical-slot form, whose slot-address cardinalities match the registered shape, and whose slot addresses land in the registered target domains.

Without Sh-conf, the cardinality and target-domain consequences (Sh0–Sh3 below) would not hold across state transitions — they would be vacuously true on an empty `L_K` and immediately false after the first non-conformant emission.

*Routing Failure Modes (consolidated).* The *Emit_K routing commitment* (Scope and Substrate Scaffolding) is load-bearing for the framework's preservation theorems and per-K disciplines. A layer that bypasses `Emit_K` to invoke the substrate primitive K.λ directly with a type `K ∈ T_cat` violates the routing commitment; the framework provides no detection mechanism (K.λ is permissive at the substrate level — ASN-0086 R0 admits any `(F, G, K)` triple with `K ∈ T_admissible` regardless of canonical-slot or shape conformance). When the routing commitment is violated:

*(a) Preservation theorems that lose their guarantees.* Sh0 (canonical-slot + cardinality on F), Sh1 (canonical-slot + cardinality on G), Sh2 (target-domain on F), Sh3 (target-domain on G), and Sh4 (idempotency under the *Sh4 idempotency contract*) — each preservation theorem's Case B argument cites the routing commitment to conclude that every new tuple in `L_K^{Σ'}` for `K ∈ T_cat` arrived via an `Emit_K` call subject to Sh-conf. A K.λ bypass introduces a tuple in `L_K^{Σ'}` whose `(F, G)` was never gated; the IH-plus-Case-B argument no longer closes and Sh0–Sh4 may fail at the resulting state. EffectiveWpSimplification (which discharges `wp_086`'s `NoCraftedSpanReachesD` and unit-depth-discipline disjunct using Sh1/Sh3 at `K := R`) loses its inputs and so loses its conclusion at Retraction-related call sites; the same applies transitively to any framework theorem citing Sh0–Sh3 at the current state.

*(b) Templates that become ill-defined.* SlotAccessorTotality (the totality of `from₁`, `to₁` at `c_F = 1`, `c_G = 1` respectively) fails on `L_K^Σ` if Sh0/Sh1 fails — a bypassed tuple may carry non-canonical F or G, breaking the `slot_addrs(·)` extraction. Per-shape base templates (`is_K`, `pair_K`, `from_K`, `to_K`, `from_addrs_K`, `to_addrs_K` across DirectedPair, NonIdempotentDirectedPair, Resolution, Retraction, Provenance, Classifier, Tuple-Classifier) become undefined or multi-valued where they consume `from₁`/`to₁` on bypassed tuples. Opt-in templates `K_target_of` (DirectedPair + FDD) and `latest_K_for_addr` (NonIdempotentDirectedPair Coverage + SHCD) lose well-definedness when their underlying disciplines fail (see (c) below). Parametric templates `unresolved_K_comments_via`, `all_K_resolved_via` (Comment) consume `to₁` and become undefined symmetrically.

*(c) Per-K disciplines that lose preservation under routing violation.* The *Sh4 idempotency contract* (clauses (i)–(iii) on idempotent K, securing pairwise slot-pair distinctness on `A_K^Σ`), the *FDD functional-dependency contract* (clauses (i)–(iii) at FDD-registered K, securing from-slot uniqueness on `A_K^Σ`), and the *single-home commitment* (clauses (i)–(ii) at SHCD-registered K, securing the homed-set property `(A τ ∈ L_K^Σ :: home(addr(τ)) = d_K)`) each presuppose that every class-(iii) emission of K routes through `Emit_K` so the contract's pre-emission check (Observe-then-Emit for Sh4/FDD; literal home-equality for SHCD) fires at every emission site. A K.λ bypass enters `L_K^{Σ'}` without consulting the contract; suppression and home-rejection are silently dropped at the bypass site, and the discipline's invariant may fail at the resulting state — Sh4 admits a duplicate slot-pair, FDD admits a duplicate from-slot, SHCD admits a tuple homed at `d ≠ d_K`. Each discipline's preservation theorem (proved by induction over `↦*`) presupposes the routing commitment at every K.λ-step in its Case B; the proof's Case B step is unsound at bypassed steps.

A layer that wishes to host bypassed emissions for types `K ∈ T_cat` — e.g., for migration from a pre-framework substrate state — must verify the bypass set is empty (equivalent to the framework's empty-baseline assumption) before claiming any framework theorem. The framework's empty-baseline assumption (Initial-State Baseline section) is the strongest condition that subsumes "no bypass has occurred"; weaker conditions (e.g., per-tuple-conformance applied to bypassed tuples) suffice for Sh0–Sh3 but not for Sh4 or any of the per-K disciplines (as analyzed under *Scope of the per-tuple-conformance relaxation* in the Initial-State Baseline section).

*Gate Ordering (consolidated).* The framework's per-K disciplines and Sh-conf's structural gates fire at every `Emit_K(Σ, d, F, G)` call site in a fixed sequence. Each per-K discipline contract specifies its local *Ordering with Sh-conf* clause (see the *Sh4 idempotency contract* section, the *FDD functional-dependency contract* sub-section, and the *single-home commitment* sub-section). The aggregate gate ordering at a call site for K with any registered discipline reads as follows:

1. **Single-home check** (if K registered under SingleHomeCoverageDiscipline): literal-equality test `d ?= d_K`. No `Observe_K` invocation, no state-dependent computation. On mismatch (`d ≠ d_K`), the *single-home commitment* clause (i) rejects the call outright with `⊥` and no subsequent gate fires. On match, the call proceeds to gate 2. (Skipped entirely at K not registered under SHCD.)

2. **Sh-conf canonical-form gate** (Sh-conf clauses (a) and (b)): test that `F` and `G` are each in canonical-slot form. On failure, `Emit_K` returns `⊥`; no subsequent per-K discipline contract fires (the per-K contracts' Observe steps would consume `slot_addrs(F)`/`slot_addrs(G)`, which are undefined for non-canonical endsets). On pass, the call proceeds to gate 3.

3. **Per-K Observe-then-Emit contract** (if K registered under Sh4 idempotency or FDD): either the *Sh4 idempotency contract* (when `shape(K).idem = ⊤` and K is not under FDD) or the *FDD functional-dependency contract* (when K is FDD-registered), executing clauses (i)–(iii) — Observe the candidate set, post-filter, then suppress or issue. On suppression (clause (ii) of either contract), `Emit_K` returns `⊥` and no subsequent gate fires. On issue (clause (iii)), the call proceeds to gate 4. (Skipped entirely at K not registered under any Observe-then-Emit discipline.)

4. **Sh-conf cardinality/target-domain gates** (Sh-conf clauses (c) and (d)): cardinality `match` on `|slot_addrs(F)|` and `|slot_addrs(G)|`; target-domain inclusion `slot_addrs(F) ⊆ t_F^Σ` and `slot_addrs(G) ⊆ t_G^Σ`. On failure of any conjunct, `Emit_K` returns `⊥`. On pass, the call proceeds to gate 5.

5. **Substrate primitive K.λ** (ASN-0086): the call invokes K.λ at home `d` with value `(F, G, K)`. K.λ's first/subsequent-emission protocol fires, depositing the new tuple at the fresh address `a_emit(Σ, d)`. The call returns `(Σ', addr) ∈ Σ' × A_rel^{Σ'}`.

*Mutual exclusion of FDD and SHCD at the same K.* FDD requires `shape(K).idem = ⊤` (because FDD strengthens Sh4, which itself requires `idem = ⊤`); SHCD is the SHCD-opt-in extension of NonIdempotentDirectedPair and attaches only to K with `shape(K) = (1, 1, A_doc, A_doc, ⊥)`. Since `shape(K).idem` is a single fixed value per K (per-class constancy, lifetime constancy), no single K can simultaneously satisfy both `idem = ⊤` and `idem = ⊥`. The framework therefore forbids registering both disciplines on the same K, and the gate sequence at any call site invokes *at most one* of gates 1 (SHCD) and 3 (FDD); gate 3's Sh4 sub-branch invokes the bare *Sh4 idempotency contract* only at K with `idem = ⊤` not registered under FDD (FDD subsumes Sh4 per the *FDD subsumes the Sh4 idempotency contract* paragraph in the FDD sub-section). At a K registered under neither FDD nor SHCD, gates 1 and 3 are both skipped; the sequence reduces to gates 2 → 4 → 5 (Sh-conf's structural gates plus K.λ).

*Why this ordering.* The single-home check is first because it is the cheapest (literal equality, no state consultation) and is structurally orthogonal to Sh-conf's clauses — a mismatch can be detected without any access to `slot_addrs`, `shape`, or `A^Σ`. The canonical-form gate (Sh-conf clauses (a)/(b)) is second because the per-K Observe-then-Emit contracts presuppose canonical-form `F`/`G` to compute `slot_addrs(·)` for the Observe step. The Observe-then-Emit contract is third because its candidate-set query gates duplicate-suppression before any irreversible state mutation. The cardinality/target-domain gates (Sh-conf clauses (c)/(d)) are fourth because they test the structural invariants Sh0–Sh3 will subsequently preserve; placing them after the Observe-then-Emit contract avoids redundant gate-firing for calls that would be suppressed by the contract anyway. K.λ is fifth and last because it is the only gate that mutates state. The framework's `⊥` return at any of gates 1–4 short-circuits subsequent gates, so the sequence is operationally a chain of conditional advances with at most one substrate-mutating step at the end.


## Nullify Compatibility

This section collects the framework's commitments at the boundary with ASN-0086's Nullify operation: the mandatory baseline registration of the retraction type `R`, the supersession of ASN-0086's Nullify-as-sole-`R`-producer discipline at the catalog level, the joint-compatibility condition between Sh-conf's extended return type and ASN-0086's Nullify postcondition, and the framework's deliberate set-semantics commitment for duplicate bare-form Nullify calls.

*Audit-slice set-semantics commitment (load-bearing change from ASN-0086).* Under this framework, two consecutive bare-form `Nullify(Σ, d_retr, a)` calls at the same target `a` produce *only one* tuple in `L_R^Σ` — not two. The first call passes the *Sh4 idempotency contract*'s clause (iii) and emits; the second call's candidate set contains the first tuple, so clause (ii) suppresses and `Emit_K` returns `⊥` without extending `L_R`. Layers requiring audit-grade multiset-semantics (one tuple per retraction event, with duplicates preserved for audit reconstruction) must use *attributed retraction* — i.e., `c_F ≥ 1` with a distinguishing from-slot value at each emission — rather than the bare `Nullify` alias. The "Attributed Retraction" walkthrough below exercises `c_F = 1` and `c_F = 2`. This is a deliberate commitment of the Retraction shape registration `(*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)` (specifically `idem = ⊤`); it is not a side effect, not a hidden semantic shift, and not something the framework recovers from elsewhere. ASN-0086's `Nullify` postcondition was specified under the implicit assumption that every well-formed call produces a fresh `(Σ', _)` pair, which is *no longer true* for duplicate bare-form calls under this framework. The *active-subset* content of ASN-0086's postcondition — single-tuple scope and stable nullification — is preserved in both the issue and suppress branches (formalized as the NullifyActiveSubsetCompatibility Corollary below); only the audit-slice multiplicity is lost. Downstream layers that consumed ASN-0086's Nullify expecting multiset-semantics must migrate to attributed retraction to recover audit-event granularity; the *Migration discipline for bare-Nullify multiset consumers* sub-clause below specifies the concrete migration recipe (call-site classification, attributed-form replacement, caller-context address conventions, and downstream readout pattern). Layers consuming only the active-subset content are unaffected.

*Interaction with Nullify.* `Nullify(Σ, d_retr, a) ≡ Emit_R(Σ, d_retr, ∅, {(a, δ(1, #a))})` (ASN-0086). The framework imposes a *baseline registration requirement*: the retraction type `R` (named by ASN-0086's Definition — RetractionType) is registered in `T_cat` with `shape(R) = (*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)` (the Retraction row of the canonical catalog below). This registration is a precondition of the framework's preservation theorems — without it, every `Nullify` call (and every other `Emit_R`) fails Sh-conf's first conjunct `K ∈ T_cat` and is rejected with `⊥`, leaving the substrate's retraction primitive uncallable through the relational layer. R-registration is mandatory for any layer instantiating the framework. With R registered, Sh-conf admits every well-formed Nullify call:

- `F = ∅` is canonical-slot form with `X_F = ∅`; `match(0, *)` holds; `∅ ⊆ A^Σ` trivially.
- `G = {(a, δ(1, #a))}` is canonical-slot form with `X_G = {a}`; `match(1, 1)` holds; `{a} ⊆ A_rel^Σ` holds by Nullify's P1 precondition `a ∈ A_rel^Σ`.

The substrate's retraction primitive is shape-conformant by construction, with no special-case exemption needed at the framework's gates — but the baseline R-registration in `T_cat` is what makes the conformant call admissible at the registry boundary.

*Supersession of ASN-0086's Nullify-as-sole-R-producer discipline.* ASN-0086's relational-layer commitment restricts R-emissions to the Nullify alias only — formally: "callers may invoke `Emit_K` only at type indices `K` satisfying `K ≁ R`; every `R`-typed emission is routed through the `Nullify` alias", where Nullify pins `F = ∅`. The framework's Retraction shape `(*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)` *supersedes* this layer-level restriction at the catalog level.
