Shape-conformant `Emit_R` calls admit any canonical-slot `F` (including `F ≠ ∅`, exercised at `c_F = 1` and `c_F = 2` in the "Attributed Retraction" walkthrough below); the Nullify alias is the `c_F = 0` boundary of the broader admissible set, not the sole route. The substrate-level guarantee that motivated ASN-0086's restriction — every emission in `L_R^Σ` has G-coverage `{t : b ≼ t}` for some `b ∈ A_rel^Σ` (the *unit-depth retraction discipline*) — is preserved unchanged by Sh-conf's `c_G = 1` plus canonical-slot gates on the Retraction shape, which force exactly that G-form on every shape-conformant R-emission. The supersession is therefore confined to the from-slot: ASN-0086's automatic NoCraftedSpanReachesD discharge (which depends on the unit-depth G-form, not on F) continues to apply, and EffectiveWpSimplification below cites the framework's Sh-conf gates on G rather than ASN-0086's Nullify-as-sole route as the unit-depth-discipline witness. Layers committed to ASN-0086's stricter Nullify-as-sole route may emit only `c_F = 0` R-tuples (i.e., via the Nullify alias); the framework's preservation theorems hold uniformly on that sub-regime as a special case.

*Compatibility with ASN-0086's Nullify postcondition.* ASN-0086's `Emit_K` is typed `Σ × dom(Σ.M) × Endset × Endset → Σ' × A_rel^{Σ'}` with no `⊥`-case, and `Nullify`'s postcondition (single-tuple-scope nullification, R6a stability) assumes a `Σ'` is produced. The framework's return-type extension to `(Σ' × A_rel^{Σ'}) ∪ {⊥}` would, if Sh-conf rejected `R`-typed calls, leave ASN-0086's `Nullify` postcondition unmet at every Nullify call site. The baseline registration requirement closes the Sh-conf-side of this gap: under the mandatory R-registration, every well-formed `Nullify(Σ, d_retr, a)` call (i.e., one satisfying ASN-0086's P0/P1/P2 preconditions) passes Sh-conf's structural gates via the conformance checks just enumerated. But Sh-conf is not the only framework gate that fires at an `Emit_R` call site: because `shape(R).idem = ⊤` (the Retraction row's idempotency flag), the *Sh4 idempotency contract* (defined in the Sh4 section) also applies to every `Emit_R` call routed through the relational layer, including every Nullify-aliased call. The contract's clause (ii) suppresses an emission with `C(F, G, Σ) ≠ ∅` — i.e., when some prior tuple in `A_R^Σ` shares the same slot-address pair. For Nullify-aliased calls, `F = ∅` is fixed, so `slot_addrs(F) = ∅` matches any prior tuple's F-slot only when that prior tuple also has `slot_addrs(F_τ) = ∅`; combined with `slot_addrs(G) = {a}`, the candidate set is `C(∅, {(a, δ(1, #a))}, Σ) = {τ ∈ A_R^Σ : slot_addrs(F_τ) = ∅ ∧ slot_addrs(G_τ) = {a}}` — i.e., the set of prior *bare-form* Nullify-aliased tuples in the active subset whose target address is `a`. Two sub-regimes need to be distinguished at the Nullify call site:

- *First-Nullify-of-target.* If no prior bare-form retraction tuple in `A_R^Σ` targets `a` (regardless of attributed retractions in `A_R^Σ` whose F-slot is non-empty), then `C = ∅`, the Sh4 contract clause (iii) issues the call, and Sh-conf's structural gates (canonical-form, cardinality, target-domain) admit it as enumerated above. The call returns `(Σ', _) ∈ Σ' × A_rel^{Σ'}` and ASN-0086's Nullify postcondition holds by the same argument the baseline registration would have delivered in the absence of Sh4.
- *Duplicate-Nullify-of-target.* If a prior bare-form Nullify-aliased tuple in `A_R^Σ` already targets `a`, then `C ≠ ∅`, the Sh4 contract clause (ii) *suppresses* the call, `Emit_K` returns `⊥`, and no `↦`-step occurs. ASN-0086's Nullify postcondition is *not* delivered as a fresh `(Σ', _)` pair at this call site — there is no Σ'.

The duplicate case appears at first reading to be incompatible with ASN-0086's Nullify postcondition, but it is *operationally compatible* with respect to ASN-0086's intended semantics. The substantive interface claim — that the framework's return-type extension from `Σ' × A_rel^{Σ'}` to `(Σ' × A_rel^{Σ'}) ∪ {⊥}` and its admission of the `⊥`-branch at duplicate calls preserves ASN-0086's active-subset content of the Nullify postcondition — is stated formally as the following corollary.

**Corollary — NullifyActiveSubsetCompatibility.** Under the framework's *Sh4 idempotency contract* with `R` registered in `T_cat` (baseline registration requirement), every `Nullify(Σ, d_retr, a)` call satisfying ASN-0086's P0/P1/P2 preconditions delivers ASN-0086's *active-subset content* of the Nullify postcondition — specifically: (i) single-tuple scope, the property that any state `Σ_target` operationally reached at the conclusion of the call (where `Σ_target = Σ'` in the clause (iii) branch and `Σ_target = Σ` in the clause (ii) branch) satisfies `{t : a ≼ t} ∩ A_rel^{Σ_target} = {a}`; and (ii) nullification, `a ∈ nullified(Σ_target)` stable under R6a — *whether the contract's clause (iii) admits a fresh `(Σ', _)` or clause (ii) suppresses to `⊥`*.

*Proof.* Case-split on the *Sh4 idempotency contract* clause that fires at the call site.

*Case A (clause (iii), `C = ∅`, no prior bare-form retraction targets `a`).* The call returns `(Σ', _) ∈ Σ' × A_rel^{Σ'}` and emits a fresh retraction tuple `τ_new ∈ L_R^{Σ'}` with `G_{τ_new} = {(a, δ(1, #a))}`. Set `Σ_target := Σ'`. *(i) single-tuple scope.* By R0a (FlatLinkDomain, ASN-0086) applied at Σ', `dom(Σ'.L)` is a tumbler-prefix antichain. The K.λ-step extends `dom(Σ.L)` by exactly `addr(τ_new)` (ASN-0086, R0), so `a ∈ dom(Σ.L) ⊆ dom(Σ'.L)` (P1 precondition together with L12a, ASN-0043). The antichain property gives `{t : a ≼ t} ∩ dom(Σ'.L) ⊆ {a}`; combined with `a ∈ A_rel^{Σ'} = dom(Σ'.L)`, `{t : a ≼ t} ∩ A_rel^{Σ'} = {a}`. ✓ *(ii) nullification.* `τ_new ∈ L_R^{Σ'}` has `coverage(G_{τ_new}) = {t : a ≼ t}` by PrefixSpanCoverage (ASN-0043); by reflexivity of `≼`, `a ∈ coverage(G_{τ_new})`. By Definition (nullified, ASN-0086) at Σ' — whose existential ranges over `L_R^{Σ'}` — `a ∈ nullified(Σ')`. R6a (RetractionStability, ASN-0086) then guarantees `a` remains nullified across every subsequent `↦*`-extension, so the stability clause holds at `Σ_target := Σ'`. ✓

*Case B (clause (ii), `C ≠ ∅`, prior bare-form retraction `τ_prior ∈ A_R^Σ ⊆ L_R^Σ` already targets `a`).* The call returns `⊥` and no `↦`-step fires, so `Σ_target := Σ` (operationally identical to the pre-call state). We show (i) and (ii) hold at this Σ. *(i) single-tuple scope.* By R0a applied at Σ, `dom(Σ.L)` is a tumbler-prefix antichain, so `{t : a ≼ t} ∩ A_rel^Σ ⊆ {a}`; combined with `a ∈ A_rel^Σ` (P1 precondition), `{t : a ≼ t} ∩ A_rel^Σ = {a}`. ✓ *(ii) nullification.* Let `τ_prior` be any element of `C` (non-empty by the case hypothesis `C ≠ ∅`, which supplies the existential witness; only one witness is needed since Definition (nullified, ASN-0086) at Σ is itself existential, so a single `τ_prior ∈ L_R^Σ` with `a ∈ coverage(G_{τ_prior})` suffices to establish `a ∈ nullified(Σ)` regardless of how many other elements `C` contains). Then `τ_prior ∈ A_R^Σ ⊆ L_R^Σ` has `a ∈ coverage(G_{τ_prior})` by the construction of `C`'s witness (the candidate-set membership requires `slot_addrs(G_{τ_prior}) = slot_addrs(G_{Nullify-call}) = {a}`, hence `coverage(G_{τ_prior}) ⊇ {a}` by PrefixSpanCoverage). By Definition (nullified, ASN-0086) at Σ — whose quantifier ranges over `L_R^Σ`, not `A_R^Σ` — `a ∈ nullified(Σ)`. R6a then guarantees `a` remains nullified across every subsequent `↦*`-extension, so the stability clause holds at Σ_target := Σ. ✓

Both cases deliver the active-subset content of the postcondition. ∎

*Audit-slice multiplicity is not preserved (and the framework records this as a deliberate commitment).* The suppressed Case B call would have added a fresh tuple `τ_dup` to `L_R^Σ`, recording a *second* retraction audit event for the same target; under suppression, that second tuple never enters the audit slice, so a downstream audit query enumerating `L_R^{Σ'}` sees one retraction event rather than two. This loss-of-audit-multiplicity is *not* part of the Corollary's preserved content — the framework's preservation theorems (Sh0–Sh4) hold either way, because their universal quantifiers range over `L_K^Σ` and `A_K^Σ` which are both well-defined at the suppressed Σ_target. By registering `shape(R) = (*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)` with `idem = ⊤`, the framework chooses set-semantics over multiset-semantics for the bare-form retraction slice. Layers requiring multiset-semantics audit-event recording for retraction must declare an attributed-form retraction with a from-slot value that distinguishes the second event from the first (Sh4 distinguishes by slot-pair, so attaching distinct F-slot attribution lifts the suppression); the *FDD functional-dependency contract* and the *single-home commitment* are the framework's general mechanisms for tightening or loosening this set-vs-multiset semantics on a per-K basis.

*Migration discipline for bare-Nullify multiset consumers.* The framework records the previous paragraph as a deliberate semantic shift from ASN-0086; the present sub-clause specifies the concrete operational pattern that recovers per-event audit multiplicity, so that downstream consumers previously assuming multiset semantics under ASN-0086's bare `Nullify` have an actionable migration path. The discipline is documented here as part of the framework's interface contract with ASN-0086 consumers, not as a layer-level recommendation.

*(M1) Classify call sites by downstream consumption.* Walk the layer's call graph for `Nullify(Σ, d_retr, a)` invocations and partition them: (a) *audit-slice consumers* read `L_R^Σ` directly — enumerating tuples, counting per-event records, or consuming per-tuple metadata such as `addr(·)` or `home(·)` — and require multiset semantics; (b) *active-subset consumers* read only `nullified(Σ)`, `A_K^Σ` for any K, or the "is this address retracted" Boolean, and are already compatible with the framework's set-semantics commitment by NullifyActiveSubsetCompatibility. Only sites in partition (a) require migration; sites in (b) require none.

*(M2) Replace each (M1)-classified bare-Nullify site with attributed retraction.* At each site requiring multiset semantics, replace

`Nullify(Σ, d_retr, a)` ≡ `Emit_R(Σ, d_retr, ∅, {(a, δ(1, #a))})`

with the attributed-form call

`Emit_R(Σ, d_retr, {(c, δ(1, #c))}, {(a, δ(1, #a))})`

where `c ∈ A^Σ` is a *caller-context address* the layer commits to uniquely distinguishing the calling context responsible for this specific retraction event from the contexts of any other retraction event the layer wishes to record as distinguishable. The framework imposes no constraint on `c`'s semantic role; the load-bearing property is uniqueness across the retraction events the layer wishes to distinguish.

*(M3) Sh4 admits attributed retractions at distinct caller-context addresses.* The *Sh4 idempotency contract*'s candidate-set check at each migrated call site reads `C(F, G, Σ) = {τ ∈ A_R^Σ : slot_addrs(F_τ) = {c} ∧ slot_addrs(G_τ) = {a}}`; suppression at clause (ii) fires only when a prior attributed retraction with the *same* caller-context address `c` *and* the same target `a` already sits in `A_R^Σ`. Distinct `c` values across successive retractions of `a` therefore admit distinct tuples in `L_R^Σ` — the audit slice accumulates one tuple per (caller-context, target) pair by Sh4's structural guarantee, recovering exactly the per-event multiplicity that ASN-0086's bare `Nullify` implicitly recorded under the un-shaped (pre-Sh4) substrate.

*(M4) Caller-context address conventions (layer choice).* The layer picks a convention for `c` matching its audit model. Three canonical choices, each well-defined under the framework's preservation theorems:

- *Per-call timestamp address.* `c = t_call`, an address allocated at retraction time recording the call's wall-clock moment (or any monotone counter the layer maintains). Distinct timestamps at each call yield distinct `c` values, so every retraction event is admitted; the audit slice records both the retraction and a temporal coordinate.
- *Per-caller identity address.* `c = d_caller`, the document address of the actor (user, process, agent) issuing the retraction. Distinct callers retracting the same target yield distinct audit tuples; *duplicate retractions from the same caller* remain Sh4-suppressed (a property layers may want, since "the same caller retracts twice" carries no new information beyond the first event).
- *Per-event composite address.* `c = e_event`, an address allocated specifically as a retraction-event marker, with the layer maintaining a one-to-one mapping between `e_event` allocations and retraction events. The strongest discipline, recovering ASN-0086's pre-framework multiset semantics exactly.

The layer is responsible for guaranteeing whichever uniqueness property its chosen convention requires (e.g., monotone counter allocation for per-call timestamps; one-to-one allocation for per-event composites); the framework's preservation theorems hold uniformly under any of (M4)'s readings.

*(M5) Downstream readout of the migrated audit slice.* Consumers reading the migrated audit slice enumerate `L_R^Σ` (or filter by target via `from_K(a)` from the Retraction shape's base template family, which under `c_F = 1`-restricted attributed retractions retrieves every tuple targeting `a`) and project each tuple's F-slot via `slot_addrs(F_τ)` to recover the caller-context address. The per-event multiplicity that ASN-0086's bare `Nullify` recorded implicitly via fresh `addr(·)` allocation is now explicit at the F-slot. Consumers that previously read `|{τ ∈ L_R^Σ : slot_addrs(G_τ) = {a}}|` to count retraction events on `a` continue to read the same count under attributed retraction — under (M3)'s per-event distinctness, the audit-slice cardinality on `a` equals the number of distinct caller-context retraction events on `a`.

*Migration is not required at all bare-Nullify sites.* Sites classified under (M1)(b) require no change; the framework's set-semantics commitment is compatible with their consumption by NullifyActiveSubsetCompatibility. Migration applies only to sites whose downstream consumption reads audit-slice multiplicity directly, per (M1)(a). Layers may run a hybrid policy — migrating only the (M1)(a) sites and leaving (M1)(b) sites unchanged — without compromising the framework's preservation theorems at either subset.

Equivalently: *the framework's preservation theorems and ASN-0086's `Nullify` postcondition are jointly compatible exactly when R is registered in `T_cat`*, with the understanding (formalized by *Corollary — NullifyActiveSubsetCompatibility*) that "compatible" reads at the active-subset level (claim (i) and (ii) of the postcondition's operationally-significant content), not at the audit-slice multiplicity level. A substrate layer that declines to register R falls outside the framework's scope; its `Nullify` calls are uncallable through the relational layer, and the framework makes no claims about its reachable states. A substrate layer that registers R but requires audit-grade multiset-semantics for duplicate retractions must use attributed retraction (`c_F = 1` or `c_F = 2` in the Retraction shape) rather than the bare `Nullify` alias; the framework's preservation theorems hold uniformly in either regime, but the duplicate-call suppression behavior described above applies only to bare-form Nullify-aliased calls (where `slot_addrs(F) = ∅`).

*Per-consumer compatibility commitments.* The framework's extension of ASN-0086's `Emit_K` return type from `Σ' × A_rel^{Σ'}` to `(Σ' × A_rel^{Σ'}) ∪ {⊥}` is consumed by every ASN-0086 surface that destructures `Emit_K`'s return tuple. The table below enumerates the affected consumers and the framework's compatibility commitment at each:

| ASN-0086 consumer | Surface form | Affected by `⊥`-return? | Framework's compatibility commitment |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Nullify` alias | `Nullify(Σ, d_retr, a) ≡ Emit_R(Σ, d_retr, ∅, {(a, δ(1, #a))})` (ASN-0086) | Yes — Nullify's caller destructures the return | NullifyActiveSubsetCompatibility Corollary (above): active-subset content (i) single-tuple scope + (ii) nullification preserved across both clause (iii) (`(Σ', _)` branch) and clause (ii) (`⊥` branch) of the Sh4 contract. Audit-slice multiplicity is deliberately not preserved. |
| Direct relational-layer `Emit_K` calls | Any layer code issuing `Emit_K(Σ, d, F, G)` and destructuring `(Σ', a)` | Yes — direct callers see the `⊥`-token on Sh-conf rejection or per-K-discipline suppression | Callers must dispatch on `⊥` before composing further operations on the return tuple. The framework's `C_K(F, G, Σ)` (Sh4) and `C_fd_K(F, Σ)` (FDD) candidate-set queries let callers predict suppression before issuing the call; single-home rejection is predictable by a literal-equality check `d ?= d_K`. No active-subset content is at stake here because the predicates the call would have established are unspecified — the caller's downstream contract is the caller's responsibility. |
| ASN-0086 composite operations consuming `Emit_K`'s return | Substrate exposes no composite operations that wrap `Emit_K` and consume its return tuple beyond the `Nullify` alias listed above | No new surface — only `Nullify` qualifies | None beyond the `Nullify` row. Future ASN-0086 composites (if introduced) would each require their own compatibility commitment under the framework. |
| `Observe_K` (read-only) | `Observe_K : Σ × ℘_fin(T) × ℘_fin(T) × View → ℘_fin(L_K^Σ)` (ASN-0086) | No — `Observe_K` does not return a `Σ' × A_rel^{Σ'}` tuple; it reads `L_K^Σ` or `A_K^Σ` without state mutation | Unaffected by the return-type extension. The framework's `C_K`, `C_fd_K` queries are built atop `Observe_K`'s read-only semantics and inherit its `⊥`-free signature. |
| Substrate primitive K.λ | Class-(iii) `→`-step (ASN-0086, `→` Definition) | No — K.λ is the substrate primitive Sh-conf rejects *before* invocation; layers routing through `Emit_K` never see K.λ's interface, only the framework's `Emit_K` interface | K.λ's substrate-level semantics remain unchanged; the framework's `⊥`-token is layer-side, not substrate-side. A layer that bypasses `Emit_K` to invoke K.λ directly violates the *Emit_K routing commitment* (see Scope and Substrate Scaffolding) and is outside the framework's scope. |

The first row's commitment is the load-bearing one (formalized as the NullifyActiveSubsetCompatibility Corollary); the second row delegates to caller-side dispatch via the layer-callable candidate-set queries; rows three through five clarify the surfaces the framework's return-type extension does *not* affect. Layers consuming ASN-0086 through any surface not listed above are out of the framework's scope by absence rather than by exclusion — the framework makes no claims at such surfaces and consumes none.

**Lemma — SubstrateConsumerActiveSubsetCompatibility.** A general framework-level companion to the NullifyActiveSubsetCompatibility Corollary that establishes Sh-conf's `⊥`-extension as active-subset-content-preserving across every ASN-0086 consumer satisfying a stated condition. Let `S` be any ASN-0086 surface — operation, alias, or composite — that, at a call site, destructures `Emit_K`'s return tuple `(Σ', a) ∈ Σ' × A_rel^{Σ'}` and asserts a load-bearing postcondition `φ_S(Σ_target)` (where `Σ_target` denotes the operationally-reached post-call state — `Σ_target := Σ'` on the substrate-success branch and `Σ_target := Σ` on any framework-rejection branch). `S` is *active-subset-content-preserving under Sh-conf's `⊥`-extension* iff its postcondition `φ_S` satisfies the following two conditions:

(α) *Substrate-level success path.* When `Emit_K` returns `(Σ', a)` (any branch in which Sh-conf clauses (a)–(d) and every applicable per-K-discipline contract's clause (iii) admit the call and the substrate K.λ-step fires), `φ_S(Σ')` holds at `Σ_target := Σ'` by ASN-0086's standard substrate-level argument (e.g., R0a, R1, R3, R6a applied to the post-K.λ state) plus the framework's preservation theorems (Sh0–Sh4 and any registered per-K disciplines, each holding at `Σ'` by their own preservation inductions).

(β) *Layer-level rejection path.* When `Emit_K` returns `⊥` (any framework gate rejects: Sh-conf's structural gates clauses (a)–(d), the *Sh4 idempotency contract* clause (ii), the *FDD functional-dependency contract* clause (ii), or the *single-home commitment* clause (i)), no `↦`-step fires and `Σ_target := Σ` (operationally identical to the pre-call state). `φ_S(Σ)` holds at `Σ_target := Σ` by *either*:
- (β.i) *Witnessed by a prior tuple.* Some prior tuple already in `L_K^Σ` (or `L_R^Σ`, for retraction-witnessed predicates) witnesses `φ_S`'s existential content at the pre-call state — the operationally-relevant case for per-K-discipline suppression where the suppressed emission would have duplicated existing state; *or*
- (β.ii) *Vacuously satisfied.* `φ_S`'s substantive content does not require a new tuple's existence — the relevant case when Sh-conf's structural gates reject (the caller's pre-call invariant carries no `φ_S` obligation that requires the rejected emission), or when the consumer's postcondition is expressible purely over the pre-call state's relational-layer structure (e.g., antichain properties, address-distinctness, type-disjointness).

*Proof.* (α) is the consumer's standard substrate-level postcondition discharge, unchanged from ASN-0086's contract on the substrate primitive K.λ plus the framework's per-K preservation theorems applied at `Σ'`. (β) follows because every framework-rejection branch leaves the relational-layer state pointwise unchanged: Sh-conf rejection returns `⊥` *before* invoking K.λ (so `Σ'.L = Σ.L`, `Σ'.M = Σ.M`, `Σ'.C = Σ.C`); per-K-discipline suppression at clause (ii) of Sh4/FDD returns `⊥` *before* the call proceeds to K.λ (same state-preservation); single-home commitment rejection at clause (i) returns `⊥` *before* Sh-conf's structural gates evaluate (same state-preservation). The consumer's `φ_S`, expressed over `A_K^{Σ_target}` or any other relational-layer slice at `Σ_target`, is then evaluated at `Σ_target := Σ` and is preserved by the consumer's case-specific witness (β.i) or by the consumer's vacuous-satisfaction reading (β.ii). ∎

*Consumed by.* Every ASN-0086 surface in the *Per-consumer compatibility commitments* table above qualifies under this Lemma's conditions:
- *Nullify alias (Row 1).* The NullifyActiveSubsetCompatibility Corollary is the load-bearing instance. (α) corresponds to Case A of that Corollary's proof (substrate-level admit, fresh `τ_new`), where `φ_S = ` "single-tuple scope at `Σ'`" + "`a ∈ nullified(Σ')` stable under R6a" holds by R0a applied at `Σ'` plus PrefixSpanCoverage + Definition (nullified, ASN-0086) at the new tuple. (β) corresponds to Case B (Sh4 contract clause (ii) suppress), branch (β.i): the prior bare-form retraction `τ_prior ∈ L_R^Σ` witnesses `a ∈ nullified(Σ)`, and R0a applied at the unchanged Σ witnesses the single-tuple-scope clause.
- *Direct relational-layer Emit_K calls (Row 2).* The caller's `φ_S` is its own contract; this Lemma supplies the compatibility framework for whatever `φ_S` the caller declares. A caller whose `φ_S` requires *a fresh tuple's existence in `A_K^{Σ_target}`* — i.e., the caller anticipates a new tuple's addition — would fall under (β.ii) at the Sh-conf-rejection branch (the caller's pre-call invariant carried no such expectation if the call could be rejected) or under (β.i) at the per-K-discipline suppression branch (a prior tuple already witnesses the equivalent slot-pair, satisfying any "this slot-pair exists" reading of `φ_S`). Layer-callable candidate-set queries `C_K(F, G, Σ)` and `C_fd_K(F, Σ)` let the caller predict which branch fires before issuing the call, choosing the appropriate `φ_S` reading pre-emption.
- *ASN-0086 composites consuming Emit_K's return (Row 3).* The substrate currently exposes no such composites beyond the Nullify alias (Row 1); future composites inherit (α)/(β) compatibility via this Lemma by satisfying the relational-layer-state-only condition on `φ_S`. A composite proposing a `φ_S` that consults state components beyond `(Σ.C, Σ.M, Σ.L)` (e.g., layer-supplied accessors, external clocks) falls outside the Lemma's scope and would require a per-composite compatibility commitment instead.
- *Observe_K (Row 4).* Unaffected by the `⊥`-extension because `Observe_K` does not destructure `Emit_K`'s return tuple; the Lemma applies vacuously. The framework's `C_K`, `C_fd_K` queries are built atop `Observe_K`'s `⊥`-free signature.
- *Substrate primitive K.λ (Row 5).* The Lemma applies indirectly through whichever framework gate admits or rejects the call to `Emit_K`; K.λ itself never sees the `⊥` token. Layers that bypass `Emit_K` to invoke K.λ directly violate the *Emit_K routing commitment* and are outside the Lemma's scope (the bypassed call never enters the framework's gate ordering, so neither (α) nor (β) is well-defined at the bypass site).

*Compatibility envelope (load-bearing scope statement, with exhaustiveness proof).* The Lemma characterizes the *complete* set of ASN-0086 surfaces the framework's `⊥`-extension is compatible with: surfaces whose load-bearing postcondition is expressible over `(Σ.C, Σ.M, Σ.L)`-slice content at the operationally-reached `Σ_target`, evaluated at success on `Σ'` and at rejection on the unchanged `Σ`. We strengthen the envelope from an asserted scope to a *theorem*: no compatibility argument outside the envelope characterization is available within the framework's interface — surfaces falling outside the envelope require explicit per-surface compatibility commitments rather than inheriting compatibility through this Lemma's (α)/(β) machinery.

*Exhaustiveness — no compatible surface outside the envelope.* Let `S` be any ASN-0086 surface destructuring `Emit_K`'s return tuple with a load-bearing postcondition `φ_S(Σ_target)`. We show that if `S` is active-subset-content-preserving under Sh-conf's `⊥`-extension (i.e., `φ_S(Σ_target)` holds at both the (α)-branch's `Σ_target := Σ'` and the (β)-branch's `Σ_target := Σ`), then `φ_S` is necessarily expressible over `(Σ.C, Σ.M, Σ.L)`-slice content at `Σ_target` — contrapositively, any `φ_S` consulting state outside `(Σ.C, Σ.M, Σ.L)` falls outside the envelope and cannot be discharged by the Lemma's general (α)/(β) argument.

Suppose, toward contradiction, that some `S` is compatibility-preserving and yet `φ_S` consults a state component `X` outside `(Σ.C, Σ.M, Σ.L)` — e.g., a layer-supplied accessor `mtime(·)` (per the rejected `K_is_fresh` candidate in the Sh5 audit table), an external clock `wallclock_time(Σ_target)`, or any layer-side projection not in the framework's substrate-conforming-layer interface. Two paths exhaust the failure mode.

*Path (a) — Distinguishability via state-component update.* Consider a hypothetical layer-side mutation of `X` that changes its value between two otherwise-identical reachable states `Σ_a, Σ_b` with `Σ_a.C = Σ_b.C, Σ_a.M = Σ_b.M, Σ_a.L = Σ_b.L` and `X(Σ_a) ≠ X(Σ_b)`. At each state, fire an `Emit_K` call rejected by Sh-conf at the same gate (e.g., a non-canonical F at both). Both calls return `⊥`, leaving the operationally-reached `Σ_target` equal to the respective pre-call state (`Σ_target_a = Σ_a`, `Σ_target_b = Σ_b`). The framework's Lemma's (β) branch discharges `φ_S(Σ_a)` and `φ_S(Σ_b)` from the same pre-call invariants — but `X(Σ_a) ≠ X(Σ_b)` makes `φ_S(Σ_a) ≠ φ_S(Σ_b)` possible whenever `φ_S` actually consults `X`, so the Lemma's discharge cannot establish both simultaneously from any single argument grounded in `(Σ.C, Σ.M, Σ.L)`-slice content. The Lemma's (β) branch's reading "`φ_S(Σ)` holds at `Σ_target := Σ`" presupposes `φ_S`'s value is determined by `(Σ.C, Σ.M, Σ.L)`-slice projections at `Σ`; a `φ_S` consulting `X` lies outside that determination, so the (β) branch's discharge is unavailable.

*Path (b) — Vacuous-satisfaction reading collapses outside the envelope.* The Lemma's (β.ii) branch — "vacuous satisfaction, `φ_S`'s substantive content does not require a new tuple's existence" — presupposes `φ_S` is expressible purely over the pre-call state's relational-layer structure: antichain properties, address-distinctness, type-disjointness, etc. A `φ_S` consulting `X` outside `(Σ.C, Σ.M, Σ.L)` has no relational-layer-structural reading of its substantive content; the (β.ii) reading collapses (vacuous satisfaction would require the consumer's pre-call invariant to carry the `φ_S` obligation without reference to `X`, but the very inclusion of `X` in `φ_S` precludes this). The (β.i) branch — "prior-tuple witness" — likewise quantifies over `L_K^Σ` (or `L_R^Σ`) at the pre-call state, which is a `(Σ.C, Σ.M, Σ.L)`-slice projection; no prior-tuple witness can carry an `X`-dependent obligation through (β.i) without consulting `X` directly, which is again outside the slice.

Both paths discharge from the same root: the Lemma's (α)/(β) machinery is constructed against `(Σ.C, Σ.M, Σ.L)`-slice content, so any `φ_S` consulting state components outside the slice falls outside the discharge envelope and cannot inherit compatibility from this Lemma. The envelope is therefore *exhaustive*: every surface compatible-under-the-Lemma satisfies the slice condition, and every surface satisfying the slice condition is compatible-under-the-Lemma. Surfaces outside the envelope require explicit per-surface compatibility commitments — the framework records this as the *operational meaning* of "Sh-conf's `⊥`-extension preserves active-subset content": within the envelope, the extension is compatible by this Lemma's general argument; outside it, per-surface commitments take over.

*Worked example — `K_is_fresh` consulting `mtime` falls outside the envelope.* The Sh5 audit table's rejected `K_is_fresh` candidate (cited in the Layer composite paragraph of the FunctionalDependencyDiscipline subsection) is a concrete instance of the envelope's outer boundary: its postcondition `mtime(K_target_of(d)) ≥ mtime(d)` consults `mtime`, a layer-supplied accessor outside the framework's substrate-conforming-layer interface. Path (a)'s distinguishability argument fires directly: two states with identical `(Σ.C, Σ.M, Σ.L)` but different `mtime(·)` projections would carry different `K_is_fresh(d)` values, breaking the Lemma's (β) discharge for any rejection-branch invocation. The composite remains operationally usable under the framework — it is recorded as a Layer composite in the FunctionalDependencyDiscipline subsection — but its compatibility with Sh-conf's `⊥`-extension cannot be inherited from this Lemma; the consuming layer must supply its own per-composite compatibility commitment (e.g., a discipline ensuring `mtime` consultation only at `(α)`-success branches, never at `(β)`-rejection branches). The exhaustiveness proof above is what makes this requirement *visibly necessary* rather than asserted ad hoc.


## Initial-State Baseline

This section pins down the framework's inductive baseline — the `Σ_init` from which every preservation proof below discharges its base case — and clarifies the scope of the per-tuple-conformance relaxation against the stricter empty-baseline requirement.

*Initial-state baseline for preservation proofs.* The inductive proofs of Sh0–Sh4 below presuppose an initial state `Σ_init` with `L_K^{Σ_init} = ∅` for every `K ∈ T_cat`. The base cases discharge against this baseline. References to `Σ_0` in the proofs below denote this `Σ_init`. States reached before the framework's *Emit_K routing commitment* was honored — e.g., substrate states from a prior process generation in which `Emit_K` did not exist, or any reachable state in which a class-(iii) emission of a type `K ∈ T_cat` bypassed `Emit_K` — are outside the framework's scope; the framework's preservation theorems make no claims about such states. A substrate that wants the framework's guarantees from a given starting point must verify `L_K^{Σ_init} = ∅` (which discharges all base cases uniformly).

*Global empty-link-store strengthening at `Σ_init`.* The framework additionally commits to the strictly stronger condition `dom(Σ_init.L) = ∅` globally — i.e., `L_K^{Σ_init} = ∅` for every `K ∈ T_admissible`, not just for every `K ∈ T_cat`. This is a substrate-level commitment of the framework's `Σ_init`: every framework instantiation begins from the substrate's literal initial state with no class-(iii) emissions of any type (registered or unregistered) yet performed. The strengthening is *not* derivable from the per-K empty-baseline `L_K^{Σ_init} = ∅` for `K ∈ T_cat` alone — substrate-level K.λ emissions at unregistered types `K' ∈ T_admissible \ T_cat` are not forbidden by ASN-0086's R0 alone, and the per-K-cat baseline does not constrain `L_{K'}^{Σ_init}` for unregistered K'. The global strengthening is what underwrites the walkthroughs' `dom(Σ_0.L) = ∅` derivation through K.σ/K.α-only prefixes from `Σ_init`: with `dom(Σ_init.L) = ∅` and K.σ/K.α steps both preserving `Σ.L` pointwise (per ASN-0086's `→ — DomExtendingTransition` Definition), the empty-link-store property propagates through any K.σ/K.α-only path to the walkthrough's `Σ_0`. This is what makes K.λ's first-emission branch predicate `{ℓ' ∈ dom(Σ_0.L) : origin(ℓ') = home_K} = ∅` fire uniformly at each walkthrough's first emission per home, regardless of whether class-(iii) emissions at unregistered types could otherwise be in scope. The per-K empty-baseline remains the necessary condition for Sh0–Sh4's induction bases; the global strengthening is an additional substrate-level commitment that the walkthroughs and the K.λ-first-emission-branch reasoning consume by name.

*Scope of the per-tuple-conformance relaxation.* The weaker check "every tuple in `L_K^{Σ_init}` satisfies `conf_K^{Σ_init}`" is *equivalent* to the empty-baseline only for the per-tuple lemmas Sh0–Sh3, whose universals quantify over a single tuple at a time and whose body is `conf_K^Σ`. It does *not* suffice for Sh4 (whose body is a pairwise predicate `(slot_addrs(F_τ), slot_addrs(G_τ)) = (slot_addrs(F_{τ'}), slot_addrs(G_{τ'})) ⟹ addr(τ) = addr(τ')`: two distinct tuples in `L_K^{Σ_init}` could each independently satisfy `conf_K^{Σ_init}` while jointly sharing a slot-pair, falsifying Sh4 at the base case), nor for the *FDD functional-dependency contract*'s preservation (whose pairwise universal over from-slot values can be violated by two tuples in `L_K^{Σ_init}` sharing a from-slot value, even though each is per-tuple conformant), nor for the *single-home commitment*'s preservation (whose homed-set universal `(A τ ∈ L_K^Σ :: home(addr(τ)) = d_K)` requires every tuple in `L_K^{Σ_init}` to have its home equal to `d_K`, a global constraint that `conf_K^{Σ_init}` — which never inspects `home(addr(τ))` — does not enforce). For Sh4, the *FDD functional-dependency contract*, and the *single-home commitment*, the empty-baseline `L_K^{Σ_init} = ∅` is required at every K under those contracts; the per-tuple-conformance relaxation alone is insufficient.

*Convention for per-walkthrough registered-catalog declarations.* Throughout the worked examples below, every walkthrough operates under the following baseline convention without re-stating it: `T_cat` is declared at `Σ_init` per lifetime constancy (so every named K is registered at the framework's initial state, not introduced mid-stream); `R` is registered with `shape(R) = (*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)` as the framework's mandatory baseline retraction type per the *Interaction with Nullify* paragraph above; `L_K^{Σ_init} = ∅` for every `K ∈ T_cat` discharges the empty-baseline assumption of Sh0–Sh4 and every per-K discipline contract; and `dom(Σ_init.L) = ∅` globally (per the *Global empty-link-store strengthening* above) propagates through any K.σ/K.α-only prefix to the walkthrough's `Σ_0`. Each walkthrough's "Registered catalog" paragraph therefore declares only what is *distinctive* — the K's it exercises and any per-K discipline registrations (FunctionalDependencyDiscipline, SingleHomeCoverageDiscipline) — and omits the baseline restatement that would otherwise repeat in every section.


## Lemma — LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit

This lemma stands as the framework's bridge from Sh-conf's structural gates to ASN-0086's `wp_086` simplification at Retraction-typed emissions. It is stated, proved, and exported here without consuming Sh0–Sh4: its proof draws only on ASN-0086's R0a-Cor1/FreshEmissionAddress/L1/L1a, ASN-0034's T10a.7/T3/Prefix, the scaffolding clauses, and NAT-card. Downstream, the EffectiveWpSimplification Corollary (after Target Domain) consumes both this lemma and Sh1/Sh3.

**Lemma — LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit.** Let Σ be reachable from `Σ_init` under the framework's *Emit_K routing commitment*. For every `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)` and every `d ∈ dom(Σ.M)`:

`b ⋠ a_emit(Σ, d)`

*Generality.* The Lemma is stated about an *arbitrary* link-store address `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)`, not specifically about retraction-tuple slot addresses. This generalization is sound — the proof below uses only `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)` and `d ∈ dom(Σ.M)`, with no appeal to `b`'s membership in any particular slot of any particular relation — and it makes the Lemma directly applicable at both consumption sites in the EffectiveWpSimplification Corollary below: discharging `NoCraftedSpanReachesD(Σ, d)` (where `b` ranges over the slot addresses of *prior* R-tuples' G-endsets) and discharging the `(K ≁ R ∨ a_emit(Σ, d) ∉ coverage(G))` disjunct under `K ~ R` (where `b` is the slot address of the *new* emission's G-endset under Sh-conf admittance). Both sites supply `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)` through Sh-conf clause (d) at `t_G = A_rel` of Retraction's catalog row.

*Proof.* Case-split on whether `b` and `a_emit(Σ, d)` share a home.

*Identification of `origin(·)` and `home(·)` for the proof's scope.* ASN-0086's FreshEmissionAddress states the first-emission gating predicate as `{ℓ' ∈ dom(Σ.L) : origin(ℓ') = d}`, while R0a-Cor1 states the contiguous-prefix postcondition over `{a ∈ dom(Σ.L) : home(a) = d}`. The two projections refer to the same per-address projection — `origin(·)` is FreshEmissionAddress's name and `home(·)` is L1a's name (`home(a) = N(a).0.U(a).0.D(a)`) for the same function. The substrate-conforming layer's *Per-document link sub-allocator chains* scaffolding clause enumerates `{ℓ : home(ℓ) = d}` as the same chain whose enumeration FreshEmissionAddress and R0a-Cor1 jointly characterize, fixing the identification `origin(·) = home(·)` on element-level link addresses. We use `home(·)` throughout this proof and treat FreshEmissionAddress's `origin(·)` as the same function under this identification.

*Case I — `home(b) = d`.* Both `b` and `a_emit(Σ, d)` lie in `A_L(d)`'s chain enumeration. The case hypothesis `home(b) = d` together with `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)` places `b` in the homed set at `d`, so the homed set is non-empty; by R0a-Cor1 (ContiguousPrefix, ASN-0086) this forces `J_d^Σ ≥ 0` (the `J_d^Σ = -1` sentinel encodes an empty homed set, which the case hypothesis rules out), and `b` is enumerated at some chain index `0 ≤ i ≤ J_d^Σ` via `b = inc^i(d.0.s_L.1, 0)`. *FreshEmissionAddress branch forced under Case I.* Because the homed set at `d` is non-empty under the case hypothesis, FreshEmissionAddress (ASN-0086) selects the *subsequent-emission* branch (the first-emission branch's gating predicate `{ℓ' ∈ dom(Σ.L) : home(ℓ') = d} = ∅` — rewritten using the identification above — is *false* in Case I, since it contains at least `b` by the case hypothesis). The subsequent-emission branch sets `a_emit(Σ, d) = inc(ℓ_prev, 0)` where `ℓ_prev = max{ℓ' ∈ dom(Σ.L) : origin(ℓ') = d}` under T1; by R0a-Cor1 this maximum is the chain element at index `J_d^Σ`, so `a_emit(Σ, d) = inc^{J_d^Σ + 1}(d.0.s_L.1, 0)` lies at chain index `J_d^Σ + 1`. By the *Uniform link sub-allocator chain length* scaffolding clause (Scope and Substrate Scaffolding), `#b = #a_emit(Σ, d)`. By T10a.7 (EnumerationInjectivity, ASN-0034) applied to the chain at `A_L(d)`, distinct chain indices yield distinct tumblers; since `b`'s chain index `i ≤ J_d^Σ < J_d^Σ + 1 = a_emit(Σ, d)`'s chain index, `b ≠ a_emit(Σ, d)`. Two equal-length distinct tumblers are prefix-incomparable: if `b ≼ a_emit(Σ, d)`, then by Prefix (ASN-0034) the length clause `#b ≤ #a_emit(Σ, d)` combined with equal lengths forces componentwise agreement on all of `1..#b = 1..#a_emit(Σ, d)`, which by T3 (CanonicalRepresentation, ASN-0034) makes them identical — contradiction. So `b ⋠ a_emit(Σ, d)`.

*Case II — `home(b) ≠ d`.* Suppose toward contradiction `b ≼ a_emit(Σ, d)`. Set `a := a_emit(Σ, d)`. From Prefix (ASN-0034), `b ≼ a` unfolds to `#b ≤ #a` and componentwise agreement `aᵢ = bᵢ` for `1 ≤ i ≤ #b`. The case structure mirrors AllocatedAddressAntichain's Case 3 — explicit numbered Steps II.1 (zero-count additivity establishes a shared zero-position layout), II.2 (T4b drives positionwise N/U/D agreement), II.3 (L1a delivers the home contradiction) — preceded by a length dispatch on `#b = #a` versus `#b < #a` that handles the equal-length degenerate case at the front of the argument rather than at the end. Throughout, both `b` and `a` carry `zeros = 3`: for `b` by L1 (LinkElementLevel, ASN-0043) on `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)`; for `a` by K.λ's construction — first-emission `a = [d.0.s_L.1]` carries two zeros from `zeros(d) = 2` (by the *Document address structure* scaffolding clause, Scope and Substrate Scaffolding) plus one zero from the separator at position `#d + 1`, with `s_L > 0` (by the *Link subspace partition* scaffolding clause's positivity commitment) and the trailing `1` both non-zero, totaling 3; subsequent-emission `a = inc(ℓ_prev, 0)` preserves zeros (TA5(c), ASN-0034, modifies only position `sig(ℓ_prev)`, and on T4-valid `ℓ_prev` — T4-validity established by the two-step chain L1c → T10a.4: L1c (LinkAllocatorConformance, ASN-0043) places `ℓ_prev ∈ dom(Σ.L)` in a T10a-conforming allocator chain, and T10a.4 (T4PreservationUnderDiscipline, ASN-0034) propagates T4-validity to every chain output — the sig position carries a non-zero value whose incremented value remains non-zero) from `zeros(ℓ_prev) = 3` (L1 at `ℓ_prev`).

*Length dispatch (sub-case II.A: `#b = #a`).* By T3 (CanonicalRepresentation, ASN-0034) applied to the equal-length componentwise agreement, `b = a`. The function `home(·) := N(·).0.U(·).0.D(·)` is a deterministic projection (well-defined on T4-valid addresses with `zeros = 3` via T4b, regardless of `dom(Σ.L)` membership), so equal arguments produce equal outputs: `home(b) = home(a)`. K.λ's construction gives `home(a) = d` in both emission branches. *First-emission branch.* `a = [d.0.s_L.1]` carries the document prefix `d` at positions `1..#d`; `d` itself has `zeros(d) = 2` (by the *Document address structure* scaffolding clause), so `a`'s third zero is the separator at position `#d + 1`. The prefix of `a` ending at position `#d` is `d`, so T4b's `home(a) = N(d).0.U(d).0.D(d) = d`. *Subsequent-emission branch.* `a = inc(ℓ_prev, 0)` where `ℓ_prev` is a chain element at `d` with `home(ℓ_prev) = d` (per the *Per-document link sub-allocator chains* scaffolding clause). By TA5 postcondition (c), `inc(·, 0)` modifies only position `sig(ℓ_prev)` and leaves every other position unchanged. On T4-valid `ℓ_prev` (established via L1c → T10a.4), TA5-SigValid gives `sig(ℓ_prev) = #ℓ_prev`, which by the *Uniform link sub-allocator chain length* scaffolding clause equals the chain's base length `#[d.0.s_L.1] = #d + 3`; hence `sig(ℓ_prev) = #d + 3 > #d + 1`, strictly past the third zero. So `inc(·, 0)` leaves positions `1..#d` and the third zero at position `#d + 1` untouched, and the T4b projection `N(·).0.U(·).0.D(·)` (which reads only positions `1..n_3 − 1 ≤ #d`) yields `home(inc(ℓ_prev, 0)) = home(ℓ_prev) = d`. Either branch gives `home(a) = d`. Hence `home(b) = d`, contradicting `home(b) ≠ d`. ∎ (II.A)

*Length dispatch (sub-case II.B: `#b < #a`).* We construct a strictly-shorter suffix `w` of `a` after `b` and derive the home contradiction through Steps II.1–II.3.

*Step II.0 — Local suffix construction.* Define the suffix `w` of `a` after `b` with `#w := #a − #b`. NAT-sub (Appendix) makes `#a − #b ∈ ℕ` well-defined under `#b ≤ #a` (from Prefix's length conjunct), and the sub-case hypothesis `#b < #a` gives `#w ≥ 1`. Set `wⱼ := a_{#b + j}` for `1 ≤ j ≤ #w` (each `a_{#b + j} ∈ ℕ` by T0's component projection); T0's comprehension clause then admits `w ∈ T`. Write `a = b · w` as shorthand for the join: `aᵢ = bᵢ` for `1 ≤ i ≤ #b` and `aᵢ = w_{i − #b}` for `#b < i ≤ #a`.

*Step II.1 — Zero-count additivity establishes `zeros(w) = 0`.* The join `a = b · w` partitions `a`'s zero-index set: `Z_a := {i : 1 ≤ i ≤ #a ∧ aᵢ = 0} = Z_b ⊔ Z_w^shift`, where `Z_b := {i : 1 ≤ i ≤ #b ∧ bᵢ = 0}` and `Z_w^shift := {i : #b < i ≤ #a ∧ w_{i − #b} = 0}`. NAT-card's additivity over disjoint unions (Appendix) gives `|Z_a| = |Z_b| + |Z_w^shift|`, and the strictly-increasing bijection `i ↦ i − #b` between `Z_w^shift` and `Z_w := {j : 1 ≤ j ≤ #w ∧ wⱼ = 0}` gives `|Z_w^shift| = |Z_w|`. Hence `zeros(a) = zeros(b) + zeros(w)`. With `zeros(a) = zeros(b) = 3` (preamble), NAT-sub yields `zeros(w) = 0`.

*Step II.2 — N/U/D agreement via T4b's positional index ranges.* Let `b`'s three zero positions be `n_1 < n_2 < n_3` (NAT-card enumeration of `Z_b`, exactly as in AllocatedAddressAntichain's Step 3.1, with the same strictly-increasing enumeration's uniqueness clause). Since `zeros(w) = 0` (Step II.1), the join `a = b · w` forces `a`'s three zero positions to coincide with `b`'s: at each `n_k ≤ #b`, componentwise agreement on `1..#b` gives `a_{n_k} = b_{n_k} = 0`, so `{n_1, n_2, n_3} ⊆ Z_a`; and `Z_a` cannot carry any additional zero — at index `m ∈ (#b, #a]`, `a_m = w_{m − #b}` and `m − #b ∈ Z_w = ∅` rules out `a_m = 0`; at index `m ∈ [1, #b]` with `m ∉ {n_1, n_2, n_3}`, `a_m = b_m ≠ 0` by `m ∉ Z_b`. So `a`'s three zero positions are exactly `n_1 < n_2 < n_3`.

By T4a + T4b + T4c (ASN-0034) applied independently to `b` and `a` (both T4-valid: `b` by L1c → T10a.4 on the link side; `a` by construction), the field projections occupy fixed positional ranges keyed off the shared zero positions:
- `N(b)` and `N(a)` both occupy positions `1..n_1 − 1`;
- `U(b)` and `U(a)` both occupy positions `n_1 + 1..n_2 − 1`;
- `D(b)` and `D(a)` both occupy positions `n_2 + 1..n_3 − 1`.

T4a (SyntacticEquivalence, ASN-0034) supplies the segment-between-zeros formula — field segments are the maximal contiguous non-zero sub-sequences delimited by the zeros, so the segments delimited by `n_1, n_2, n_3` occupy exactly the ranges named above; T4b (UniqueParse, ASN-0034) makes each segment a uniquely computable projection; and T4c (LevelDetermination, ASN-0034) maps the `zeros = 3` case to the four-field (N, U, D, E) hierarchy, identifying the three pre-`n_3` segments with the N, U, D fields respectively. All three positional ranges lie within `1..n_3 − 1`, and `n_3 ≤ #b` (since `n_3` is a position of `b`), so `n_3 − 1 < n_3 ≤ #b` places every N/U/D position of both `b` and `a` strictly inside `1..#b`. Componentwise agreement `aᵢ = bᵢ` for `1 ≤ i ≤ #b` therefore forces N, U, D agreement positionwise. Rather than appealing to a "symmetric arguments" gesture at the U-range and D-range, we exhibit the per-range identification at each of the three ranges explicitly — one sentence per range — so that the bridge from AllocatedAddressAntichain's Step 3.2 (single-position unfolding at `n_3 + 1` to establish `E(x).1 = E(a).1`) to the three-range unfolding here is visible at each application site rather than collapsed into an analogy.

*N-range identification (`1..n_1 − 1`).* T4a's segment-between-zeros formula at the segment delimited by the left boundary `1` and the right boundary `n_1` (the first zero of both `b` and `a`) identifies the maximal contiguous non-zero sub-sequence at positions `1..n_1 − 1` as the N-field segment of both `b` and `a` (the formula applies independently to each address, but the shared zero position `n_1` from Step II.2's preamble makes the segment's positional range coincide). T4b's uniqueness clause makes this segment a uniquely computable projection of each address — `N(b)` is the unique segment at this range in `b`, and `N(a)` is the unique segment at this range in `a`. T4c's level-to-segment labeling maps the `zeros = 3` case to the four-field hierarchy and identifies the first pre-`n_1` segment as the N-field at both addresses. Componentwise agreement `aᵢ = bᵢ` from `b ≼ a` instantiated at each `i ∈ {1, …, n_1 − 1}` (every such `i` is `≤ n_1 − 1 ≤ #b` by `n_1 ≤ #b`, so the agreement holds) gives `(N(a))ᵢ = aᵢ = bᵢ = (N(b))ᵢ`; quantifying over `i` in the N-range yields `N(a) = N(b)` componentwise, hence `N(a) = N(b)` by T3 (CanonicalRepresentation, ASN-0034) after equal-length verification (both N-field projections have length `n_1 − 1` by the shared range).

*U-range identification (`n_1 + 1..n_2 − 1`).* T4a's segment-between-zeros formula at the segment delimited by the left boundary `n_1 + 1` (one past the first zero) and the right boundary `n_2` (the second zero) identifies the maximal contiguous non-zero sub-sequence at positions `n_1 + 1..n_2 − 1` as the U-field segment of both `b` and `a`. T4b's uniqueness clause makes `U(b)` and `U(a)` the unique segments at this range in their respective addresses. T4c's labeling identifies the segment between the first and second zeros as the U-field. Componentwise agreement at each `i ∈ {n_1 + 1, …, n_2 − 1}` (every such `i` is `≤ n_2 − 1 ≤ #b` by `n_2 ≤ #b`) gives `(U(a))ᵢ = aᵢ = bᵢ = (U(b))ᵢ`; quantifying over the U-range yields `U(a) = U(b)` componentwise, hence by T3 with equal lengths (both U-field projections have length `n_2 − n_1 − 1` by the shared range).

*D-range identification (`n_2 + 1..n_3 − 1`).* T4a's segment-between-zeros formula at the segment delimited by the left boundary `n_2 + 1` (one past the second zero) and the right boundary `n_3` (the third zero) identifies the maximal contiguous non-zero sub-sequence at positions `n_2 + 1..n_3 − 1` as the D-field segment of both `b` and `a`. T4b's uniqueness clause makes `D(b)` and `D(a)` the unique segments at this range in their respective addresses. T4c's labeling identifies the segment between the second and third zeros as the D-field. Componentwise agreement at each `i ∈ {n_2 + 1, …, n_3 − 1}` (every such `i` is `≤ n_3 − 1 ≤ #b` by `n_3 ≤ #b`) gives `(D(a))ᵢ = aᵢ = bᵢ = (D(b))ᵢ`; quantifying over the D-range yields `D(a) = D(b)` componentwise, hence by T3 with equal lengths (both D-field projections have length `n_3 − n_2 − 1` by the shared range).

The three per-range identifications jointly deliver `N(a) = N(b) ∧ U(a) = U(b) ∧ D(a) = D(b)`. *Bridge to AllocatedAddressAntichain's Step 3.2.* AllocatedAddressAntichain's Step 3.2 unfolds the single position `n_3 + 1` to establish `E(x).1 = E(a).1` using T4a's segment formula at the post-`n_3` segment (the E-field segment), T4b's uniqueness on the E-field projection, and T4c's labeling of the post-`n_3` segment as the E-field. The three per-range identifications above carry out the same T4a + T4b + T4c reasoning at three different segment positions (N at `1..n_1 − 1`, U at `n_1 + 1..n_2 − 1`, D at `n_2 + 1..n_3 − 1`) — each segment delimited by its own pair of zero boundaries, with T4a's formula instantiated against each pair, T4b's uniqueness applied to the resulting projection, and T4c's labeling applied to the segment's position in the four-field hierarchy. The mechanics are *uniform* across each segment by the uniform application of T4a + T4b + T4c, with each field segment's positional offset computed against its delimiting zeros (left boundary `0` or a zero-position; right boundary `n_k` or `#b`); the present exhibition makes each application visible at its citation site rather than relying on analogy. The three identifications close the N/U/D agreement that Step II.3 then composes into the home contradiction.

*Step II.3 — Home contradiction.* The function `home(·) := N(·).0.U(·).0.D(·)` is a deterministic projection of any T4-valid address with `zeros = 3`; T4b (UniqueParse, ASN-0034) makes N, U, D total under that constraint, so `home(·)` is well-defined on every such address regardless of whether the address belongs to `dom(Σ.L)` at the current state. (L1a from ASN-0043 records the *invariant* — that this projection lands `home(a)` in `dom(Σ.M)` for every link-store address `a ∈ dom(Σ.L)` — but the *function definition* itself rests on T4b alone and applies at any T4-valid `zeros = 3` address, including the not-yet-emitted `a = a_emit(Σ, d)`. Both `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)` and `a` are T4-valid with `zeros = 3` by the case preamble, so `home(b)` and `home(a)` are both well-defined as projections.) Substituting Step II.2's three field-equalities into the projection formula gives `home(b) = N(b).0.U(b).0.D(b) = N(a).0.U(a).0.D(a) = home(a)`. K.λ's construction gives `home(a) = d` (first-emission deposits at `[d.0.s_L.1]` whose home is `d`; subsequent-emission inherits home from the parent chain at `d`). So `d = home(b)`, contradicting `home(b) ≠ d`. ∎ (II.B)

Either sub-case yields `b ⋠ a_emit(Σ, d)`, closing Case II.

Either case yields `b ⋠ a_emit(Σ, d)`. ∎

*Worked examples — Cases I and II at concrete tumblers.* To exhibit both branches of the proof's case-split concretely, fix `s_L = 7` and a document `d = [1, 0, 2, 0, 3] ∈ dom(Σ.M)` with `#d = 5` and `zeros(d) = 2` (consistent with the *Document address structure* scaffolding clause).

*Case I example (same-home, distinct chain indices).* Suppose `dom(Σ_0.L) = ∅` and three link emissions occur at `d`, producing chain elements `ℓ_0, ℓ_1, ℓ_2` of `A_L(d)`:
- ℓ_0 at chain index 0: `a_emit(Σ_0, d) = [d.0.s_L.1] = [1, 0, 2, 0, 3, 0, 7, 1]` (first-emission branch).
- ℓ_1 at chain index 1: `a_emit(Σ_1, d) = inc(ℓ_0, 0) = [1, 0, 2, 0, 3, 0, 7, 2]` (subsequent-emission branch).
- ℓ_2 at chain index 2: `a_emit(Σ_2, d) = inc(ℓ_1, 0) = [1, 0, 2, 0, 3, 0, 7, 3]`.

Now at Σ_3 (after all three emissions), `a_emit(Σ_3, d) = inc(ℓ_2, 0) = [1, 0, 2, 0, 3, 0, 7, 4]` lies at chain index 3. Take `b := ℓ_0` (so `b ∈ dom(Σ_3.L)` with `home(b) = d`). By the *Uniform link sub-allocator chain length* scaffolding, `#b = #a_emit(Σ_3, d) = 8`. By T10a.7, chain index 0 ≠ chain index 3 ⟹ `b ≠ a_emit(Σ_3, d)`; direct verification: `b_8 = 1 ≠ 4 = a_emit(Σ_3, d)_8`. Suppose toward contradiction `b ≼ a_emit(Σ_3, d)`. With equal lengths, Prefix's componentwise-agreement clause forces componentwise equality on all 8 positions, hence `b = a_emit(Σ_3, d)` by T3 — contradicting the chain-index distinctness. So `b ⋠ a_emit(Σ_3, d)`. ✓

*Case II.B example (cross-home, nontrivial suffix, exercising Steps II.0–II.3).* Take a longer document `d_long = [1, 0, 2, 0, 3, 4]` with `#d_long = 6` and `zeros(d_long) = 2` (zeros at positions 2 and 4 only; positions 5, 6 carry `3, 4`). The first emission at `d_long` is `a := a_emit(Σ, d_long) = [d_long.0.s_L.1] = [1, 0, 2, 0, 3, 4, 0, 7, 1]` with `#a = 9` and zeros at positions 2, 4, 7. Take `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)` as the first emission at the shorter `d` from above: `b = [1, 0, 2, 0, 3, 0, 7, 1]` with `#b = 8`, `home(b) = d ≠ d_long`. Now `#b = 8 < 9 = #a`, instantiating Sub-case II.B. Querying `b ⋠ a`: direct verification at position 6 gives `b_6 = 0 ≠ 4 = a_6`, so `b ⋠ a` immediately.

To exhibit the general proof's Steps II.0–II.3, suppose toward contradiction `b ≼ a`. *Step II.0:* the suffix `w` has length `#w = #a − #b = 1` with components `w_1 := a_9 = 1`, so `w = [1]`. *Step II.1:* `Z_b = {2, 4, 6}`, `Z_w = ∅` (since `w_1 = 1 ≠ 0`), and `Z_w^shift = ∅`; NAT-card additivity gives `|Z_a| = |Z_b| + |Z_w^shift| = 3 + 0 = 3`, hence `zeros(w) = 0`. *Step II.2:* `b`'s three zero positions `n_1 = 2, n_2 = 4, n_3 = 6` lift to `a`'s zero positions exactly (verifiable: `a_2 = 0, a_4 = 0, a_6 = 4 ≠ 0`, exposing a structural mismatch — `b_6 = 0` but `a_6 = 4` — which means `b ≼ a` was false in the first place; the contradiction surfaces at Step II.2 here rather than at Step II.3). *Step II.3 (under the counterfactual that Step II.2's zero-position-sharing held):* by L1a, `home(a) = home(b)`, but `home(a) = d_long` and `home(b) = d ≠ d_long`, contradicting `home(b) ≠ d`. (The example illustrates both the additivity-driven argument and how the direct verification at position 6 reaches an earlier contradiction by a shorter route — `b_6 = 0` would force `a_6 = 0`, but `a_6 = 4` — confirming the additivity-driven conclusion against the concrete component values.)

*Sub-case II.A example (equal-length cross-home, exercising T3-driven collapse).* For an equal-length sub-case at the same shorter document `d`, suppose `b' ∈ dom(Σ.L)` with `#b' = #a_emit(Σ, d)`, `home(b') ≠ d`, and `b' ≼ a_emit(Σ, d)`. By Sub-case II.A's argument: Prefix's componentwise agreement on `1..#b' = 1..#a_emit(Σ, d)` covers every position of both tumblers, and T3 collapses to `b' = a_emit(Σ, d)`. By L1a, `home(b') = home(a_emit(Σ, d)) = d`, contradicting `home(b') ≠ d`. The argument bypasses Steps II.0–II.3 entirely — no suffix is needed and no NAT-card additivity fires — and closes at T3 + L1a alone. The structural simplicity of Sub-case II.A is why it dispatches by length-equality at the front of the case rather than appearing as a degenerate boundary of Sub-case II.B.

*Step II.1's discharge collapses to a trivial bound at the current substrate scaffolding.* Under the *Document address structure* scaffolding clause (`zeros(d) = 2` for every `d ∈ dom(Σ.M)`), K.λ's emission at `d` produces `a` with `#a = #d + 3` and `zeros(a) = 3`; for `b ≼ a` with `zeros(b) = 3` (L1), `b` must include all three of `a`'s zeros, so `#b ≥ #d + 1`. T4's last-position non-zero clause `b_{#b} ≠ 0` further forces `#b ≥ #d + 2` (since position `#d + 1` is a zero in `a` and hence in `b` by componentwise agreement). Composing, `#w = #a − #b ≤ #d + 3 − (#d + 2) = 1`, so the only substrate-reachable Sub-case II.B configuration has `#w = 1`; combined with `zeros(b) = zeros(a) = 3` and a single-position `w`, the additivity decomposition of Step II.1 reduces to "the one extra position of `a` past `b` carries a non-zero value", which the structural bound delivers by inspection (the Case II.B example at `#w = 1` walks the position-6 mismatch explicitly). Step II.1's NAT-card additivity, expressed in its general form against any `#w ≥ 1` with arbitrary `zeros(w) ≥ 0`, is not exercised non-trivially at any substrate-reachable input.

*The general formulation is retained as an aside for future extension.* The general form of Step II.1 — citing T0/Prefix/T4/NAT-card additivity uniformly over `#w ≥ 1` without consulting the current scaffolding's specific `zeros(d) = 2` clause — is retained nonetheless, not as a load-bearing step at the substrate-reachable subset but as a forward-looking aside. A hypothetical future scaffolding admitting `zeros(d) > 2` (e.g., a substrate extension where document addresses carry an additional zero-delimited field beyond the four-field N.U.D.E hierarchy) would make `#w ≥ 2` substrate-reachable and Step II.1's additivity would fire non-trivially at the new regime; the general formulation discharges Sub-case II.B uniformly against any such extension without recomputing against each scaffolding change. The substrate-conforming layer's current commitment to `zeros(d) = 2` (recorded once at the *Document address structure* scaffolding clause) is sufficient to collapse Step II.1 to the trivial bound at the present catalog, and the framework's substantive contradiction at substrate-reachable inputs surfaces at Step II.2 (zero-position layout via T4b's positional ranges) or Step II.3 (N/U/D agreement forcing the home contradiction), not at Step II.1. Readers tracking the proof at the present scaffolding may treat Step II.1 as a trivial structural-bound step and consult its general form only when reasoning about extensions admitting deeper K.λ emissions.

The Lemma's one-directional reading — it asks `b ⋠ a_emit(Σ, d)`, not the converse `a_emit(Σ, d) ⋠ b` — matches what both consumers in the EffectiveWpSimplification Corollary (below, after Target Domain) need: each consumer's coverage set has the form `{t : b ≼ t}` (PrefixSpanCoverage, ASN-0043), and the question is whether `a_emit(Σ, d)` lies in it. The converse direction `a_emit(Σ, d) ≼ b` is not asked about by any wp_086 conjunct.


## Cardinality (Sh0, Sh1)

**Sh0 — FromSlotCanonicalAndCardinalityFixed.** For each `K ∈ T_cat`, every tuple in `L_K^Σ` at every reachable state Σ has `F` in canonical-slot form with `|slot_addrs(F)|` matching `c_F`:

`(A K ∈ T_cat, (a, F, G) ∈ L_K^Σ :: F is canonical-slot form ∧ match(|slot_addrs(F)|, shape(K).c_F))`

*Proof.* By induction on the broad transition relation `↦*` from the initial state `Σ_0`. Reachable states are reached under `↦*` (the broader relation including arrangement-modifying steps), not just `→*`, so the induction must cover both transition classes.

*Base case.* At `Σ_0 = Σ_init` (per the framework's empty-baseline assumption, *Initial-state baseline for preservation proofs* in the Initial-State Baseline section above), every `L_K^{Σ_0} = ∅`; the universal quantifier is vacuous.

*Inductive step.* Suppose the property holds at Σ; let `Σ ↦ Σ'` be a single broad transition. We fix a particular `K ∈ T_cat`. `L_K` is monotone non-decreasing along `↦*` by R3 (TypedSliceMonotonicity, ASN-0086) on `→`-steps and LinkStoreInvarianceUnderArrangement (ASN-0086) on `↦ \ →`-steps, with strict increase confined to K.λ-steps emitting at K or `~`-equivalent type (by R0 (TupleAddressFreshness, ASN-0086)'s freshness — the new tuple's address is fresh in `dom(Σ.L)`, so it is fresh in `L_K^Σ` too); K.σ-steps, K.α-steps, and K.λ-steps at non-`~`-equivalent types all leave `L_K` pointwise unchanged. `L_K` never contracts. The only effects on `L_K` are unchanged (Case A) or extended by one tuple (Case B). The contraction cases that appear in Sh4 over `A_K` (which filters `L_K` by `nullified(·)` membership) do not arise here because Sh0's universal ranges over `L_K`, not `A_K`. We split on whether the step affects `L_K`.

*Case A: `L_K^{Σ'} = L_K^Σ`.* The relation `L_K` is unchanged. The property is inherited tuple-by-tuple from the IH; existing tuples retain their values by R2 (TupleAddressPermanence, ASN-0086). This case covers all K.σ-steps and K.α-steps (which preserve `Σ.L` pointwise, hence `L_K` for every K, including this K); all K.λ-steps emitting a tuple of type `K'` not coverage-equivalent to K (since `L_K` slices only addresses whose stored type satisfies `coverage(e₃) = coverage(K)`); and all arrangement-modifying steps in `↦ \ →` (by LinkStoreInvarianceUnderArrangement, ASN-0086).

*Case B: `L_K^{Σ'} = L_K^Σ ∪ {τ_new}` for a single new tuple.* By the *Emit_K routing commitment* (Scope and Substrate Scaffolding), this is a K.λ-step originating as an `Emit_K` call at type K (or a K'-typed call with `K ~ K'`; by T_cat's `~`-closure, `K' ∈ T_cat`, and `Emit_{K'}` consults `shape(K') = shape(K)` by `~`-constancy). Sh-conf admitted that call only because `conf_K^Σ(F, G)` held — in particular, by Sh-conf *clause (a)*, `F` is in canonical-slot form, and by *clause (c)*, `match(|slot_addrs(F)|, shape(K).c_F)`. The new tuple satisfies the property; existing tuples retain their values by R2 and their conformance by the IH.

Both cases preserve the property at the chosen K; quantifying over K closes the induction. ∎

**Sh1 — ToSlotCanonicalAndCardinalityFixed.** The G-side analog of Sh0:

`(A K ∈ T_cat, (a, F, G) ∈ L_K^Σ :: G is canonical-slot form ∧ match(|slot_addrs(G)|, shape(K).c_G))`

*Proof.* By induction on `↦*` from `Σ_0`. Reachable states are reached under `↦*`, so the induction must cover both `→`-steps and arrangement-modifying steps in `↦ \ →`. Fix `K ∈ T_cat`.

*Base case.* At `Σ_0 = Σ_init` (per the framework's empty-baseline assumption, *Initial-state baseline for preservation proofs* in the Initial-State Baseline section above), `L_K^{Σ_0} = ∅`; the universal quantifier is vacuous.

*Inductive step.* Suppose the property holds at Σ; let `Σ ↦ Σ'` be a single broad transition. `L_K` is monotone non-decreasing along `↦*` by R3 (TypedSliceMonotonicity, ASN-0086) on `→`-steps and LinkStoreInvarianceUnderArrangement (ASN-0086) on `↦ \ →`-steps, with strict increase confined to K.λ-steps emitting at K or `~`-equivalent type (by R0 (TupleAddressFreshness, ASN-0086)'s freshness); K.σ-steps, K.α-steps, and K.λ-steps at non-`~`-equivalent types all leave `L_K` pointwise unchanged. So the only effects on `L_K` are Case A (unchanged) or Case B (one new tuple). Split on whether the step affects `L_K`.

*Case A* (`L_K^{Σ'} = L_K^Σ`). The relation is unchanged; existing tuples retain their values by R2 (TupleAddressPermanence, ASN-0086), and the property is inherited tuple-by-tuple from the IH. This case covers the same step classes enumerated under Sh0's Case A: K.σ-steps and K.α-steps (which preserve `Σ.L` pointwise); K.λ-steps emitting a tuple of type `K'` not coverage-equivalent to K (since `L_K` slices only addresses whose stored type satisfies `coverage(e₃) = coverage(K)`); and arrangement-modifying steps in `↦ \ →` (by LinkStoreInvarianceUnderArrangement, ASN-0086).

*Case B* (`L_K^{Σ'} = L_K^Σ ∪ {τ_new}` for a single new tuple). By the *Emit_K routing commitment* (Scope and Substrate Scaffolding), this is a K.λ-step originating as an `Emit_K` call at type K (or a `~`-equivalent type `K'`; by T_cat's `~`-closure, `K' ∈ T_cat`, and `shape(K') = shape(K)` by per-class constancy). Sh-conf admitted that call only because `conf_K^Σ(F, G)` held — in particular, by Sh-conf's *clause (b)*, `G` is in canonical-slot form, and by *clause (c)*, `match(|slot_addrs(G)|, shape(K).c_G)`. So τ_new satisfies the G-side property: the first conjunct of Sh1's body is discharged by clause (b); the second conjunct by clause (c). The argument is uniform over every admissible value of `c_G ∈ {0, 1, *, 0|1}` — Sh-conf clause (c) tests `match(·, c_G)` directly, so for shapes registering `c_G = *` (e.g., BundledDirectedPair below) the body's second conjunct discharges for every `n ∈ ℕ` matching `*` via `match(n, *)`. Existing tuples retain their values by R2 and their G-side conformance by the IH.

Both cases preserve the property at the chosen K; quantifying over K closes the induction. ∎

*Justification of the constraint.* Without uniform slot-cardinality per type, "the `d` in this tuple's `G`" is ambiguous when `|slot_addrs(G)|` varies — predicates that bind G's slot positions cannot be defined uniformly. Fixed cardinality lets templates parameterize on slot positions: a `(1, 1)`-shape relation has well-defined `from₁(τ)` and `to₁(τ)`; a `(0, 1)`-shape relation has only `to₁(τ)`; an `(*, 1)`-shape relation has `to₁(τ)` plus a from-set accessor returning a set.

*Consequences.*

(a) *Predicates have stable signatures.* For shape `(0, 1, -, A_doc, _)`, predicates take a single document argument: `is_K : A_doc → Bool`. For shape `(1, 1, A_doc, A_doc, _)`, predicates take an ordered pair. Signatures are determined by the shape, not by individual emissions.

(b) *Counting and aggregation are well-defined.* "How many comment-tuples target d?" is a number because `|slot_addrs(G)| = 1` (Sh1) and "targets d" has a uniform meaning across the relation.

(c) *Slot accessors are total on the relevant slots* (SlotAccessorTotality below).

(d) *Allocated-coverage matches syntactic intent.* By AllocatedAddressAntichain, for `c_F = 1` shapes with `slot_addrs(F) = {x}` and `x ∈ A^Σ`, `cov_allocated(F, Σ) = {x}` — the semantic "this slot refers to x" matches the syntactic `slot_addr(F) = x`. Sh-conf's target-domain check `X_F ⊆ t_F^Σ` requires `x ∈ A^Σ`, so the lemma's precondition is satisfied for every shape-conformant tuple.


## Target Domain (Sh2, Sh3)

**Sh2 — FromSlotTargetRestricted.** For each `K ∈ T_cat`:

`(A K ∈ T_cat, (a, F, G) ∈ L_K^Σ :: slot_addrs(F) ⊆ shape(K).t_F^Σ)`

(vacuous on the F-side when `t_F = -`, i.e., when `c_F = 0`).

*Well-formedness of `slot_addrs(F)`.* The body of Sh2's universal references `slot_addrs(F)`, which is defined only on canonical-slot F (CanonicalSlotForm Definition above). By Sh0 evaluated at the current state Σ — already proved by its own induction in the Cardinality section before this section — every τ ∈ L_K^Σ has F in canonical-slot form, so `slot_addrs(F)` is well-defined throughout the quantification. Sh2 consumes Sh0 as a previously-established state-indexed lemma (parallel to how Sh4 consumes Sh0–Sh3 as state-indexed lemmas; see Sh4's *Stratification* clause); Sh2's own inductive hypothesis quantifies only over the target-domain inclusion, not over canonical-form, which Sh0 supplies independently.

*Proof.* By induction on `↦*` from `Σ_0`. Fix `K ∈ T_cat`. `L_K` is monotone non-decreasing along `↦*` by R3 (TypedSliceMonotonicity, ASN-0086) on `→`-steps and LinkStoreInvarianceUnderArrangement (ASN-0086) on `↦ \ →`-steps, with strict increase confined to K.λ-steps emitting at K or `~`-equivalent type (by R0 (TupleAddressFreshness, ASN-0086)'s freshness); K.σ-steps, K.α-steps, and K.λ-steps at non-`~`-equivalent types all leave `L_K` pointwise unchanged. The inductive step splits on whether the transition leaves `L_K` unchanged (Case A) or extends it by one tuple (Case B); no contraction case for `L_K` arises.

*Base case.* At `Σ_0 = Σ_init` (per the framework's empty-baseline assumption, *Initial-state baseline for preservation proofs* in the Initial-State Baseline section above), `L_K^{Σ_0} = ∅` vacuously.

*Inductive step.*

*Case A: `L_K^{Σ'} = L_K^Σ`.* `L_K` is unchanged. The property is inherited on every existing tuple, with monotone preservation `X_F ⊆ t_F^Σ ⟹ X_F ⊆ t_F^{Σ'}` because `t_F^Σ ⊆ t_F^{Σ'}` (allocated-set monotonicity: link-side from L12a ASN-0043, content-side from the scaffolding assumption; arrangement steps preserve `dom(Σ.C)` and `dom(Σ.L)` so the inclusion is equality there). This case covers K.σ-steps, K.α-steps, K.λ-steps for non-K-coverage-equivalent types, and arrangement-modifying steps in `↦ \ →` (via LinkStoreInvarianceUnderArrangement).

*Case B: `L_K^{Σ'} = L_K^Σ ∪ {τ_new}`.* By the *Emit_K routing commitment*, the K.λ-step originates as an `Emit_K`-class call (at K or a `~`-equivalent type by T_cat's `~`-closure) subject to Sh-conf; the new tuple satisfies `X_F ⊆ t_F^Σ` by Sh-conf at emission, hence `X_F ⊆ t_F^{Σ'}` by monotonicity. Existing tuples retain their values by R2 and their target-domain conformance by the IH plus monotonicity. ∎

**Sh3 — ToSlotTargetRestricted.** Symmetric for `G`:

`(A K ∈ T_cat, (a, F, G) ∈ L_K^Σ :: slot_addrs(G) ⊆ shape(K).t_G^Σ)`

*Well-formedness of `slot_addrs(G)`.* Symmetric to Sh2's well-formedness clause: Sh3's body references `slot_addrs(G)`, defined only on canonical-slot G. By Sh1 evaluated at the current state Σ — already proved by its own induction in the Cardinality section — every τ ∈ L_K^Σ has G in canonical-slot form, so `slot_addrs(G)` is well-defined throughout the quantification. Sh3 consumes Sh1 as a previously-established state-indexed lemma.

*Proof.* By induction on `↦*` from `Σ_0`. Fix `K ∈ T_cat`. `L_K` is monotone non-decreasing along `↦*` by R3 (TypedSliceMonotonicity, ASN-0086) on `→`-steps and LinkStoreInvarianceUnderArrangement (ASN-0086) on `↦ \ →`-steps, with strict increase confined to K.λ-steps emitting at K or `~`-equivalent type (by R0 (TupleAddressFreshness, ASN-0086)'s freshness); K.σ-steps, K.α-steps, and K.λ-steps at non-`~`-equivalent types all leave `L_K` pointwise unchanged. The inductive step splits on whether the transition leaves `L_K` unchanged (Case A) or extends it by one tuple (Case B); no contraction case for `L_K` arises. The structure mirrors Sh2 with the substitution F → G throughout — clauses (b) and (c) of Sh-conf cited where Sh2 cites (a) and (c), and `t_G` substituted for `t_F`.

*Base case.* At `Σ_0 = Σ_init` (per the framework's empty-baseline assumption, *Initial-state baseline for preservation proofs* in the Initial-State Baseline section above), `L_K^{Σ_0} = ∅` vacuously.

*Inductive step.*

*Case A: `L_K^{Σ'} = L_K^Σ`.* `L_K` is unchanged. The property is inherited on every existing tuple, with monotone preservation `X_G ⊆ t_G^Σ ⟹ X_G ⊆ t_G^{Σ'}` because `t_G^Σ ⊆ t_G^{Σ'}` (allocated-set monotonicity: link-side from L12a ASN-0043, content-side from the scaffolding assumption; arrangement steps preserve `dom(Σ.C)` and `dom(Σ.L)` so the inclusion is equality there). This case covers K.σ-steps, K.α-steps, K.λ-steps for non-K-coverage-equivalent types, and arrangement-modifying steps in `↦ \ →` (via LinkStoreInvarianceUnderArrangement).

*Case B: `L_K^{Σ'} = L_K^Σ ∪ {τ_new}`.* By the *Emit_K routing commitment*, the K.λ-step originates as an `Emit_K`-class call (at K or a `~`-equivalent type by T_cat's `~`-closure) subject to Sh-conf. Sh-conf admitted that call only because `conf_K^Σ(F, G)` held — in particular, by Sh-conf clause (b), `G` is in canonical-slot form (so `slot_addrs(G) = X_G` is well-defined), and by Sh-conf clause (d) on the G-side, `X_G ⊆ t_G^Σ` at emission, hence `X_G ⊆ t_G^{Σ'}` by monotonicity. The set-containment argument is uniform over every G-side cardinality — when `c_G = *` and `|X_G| ≥ 2` (e.g., BundledDirectedPair's bundled multi-target emissions), Sh-conf clause (d) tests `X_G ⊆ t_G^Σ` as a finite-set inclusion regardless of `|X_G|`, and the monotonicity step `t_G^Σ ⊆ t_G^{Σ'}` lifts the inclusion uniformly. Existing tuples retain their values by R2 and their target-domain conformance by the IH plus monotonicity. ∎

*Retraction commutativity.* `A_K^Σ ⊆ L_K^Σ` always (immediate from ASN-0086's `A_K^Σ` Definition `{(a, F, G) ∈ L_K^Σ : a ∉ nullified(Σ)}`, since `nullified(Σ)` filters but never adds). Sh0–Sh3 universally quantify over `L_K^Σ`, so every tuple in `A_K^Σ` is also shape-conformant. Retraction removes a tuple from `A_K^Σ` but never introduces a non-conformant tuple. The shape predicates over `A_K^Σ` are therefore well-typed at every state, including post-retraction states. This closes the question of whether shape commutes with retraction.

*Justification of the constraint itself.* R4 (TupleAddressDisjointness, ASN-0086) gives two address kinds — `A_doc` and `A_rel` — but does not by itself constrain *which* kind appears in any given slot. Sh2/Sh3 do: each slot of each relation targets one fixed kind. Without this, the same predicate template would have to handle both document targets and tuple targets, and predicate type-checking at the slot level would fail.

*Consequences.*

(a) *Predicates are typed at the slot level.* For relations targeting documents (`t_G = A_doc`), `to₁(τ) ∈ A_doc^Σ`; for relations targeting tuples (`t_G = A_rel`, e.g. retraction, resolution), `to₁(τ) ∈ A_rel^Σ`. The codomain of `to₁` is determined by `t_G`, not by individual emissions.

(b) *Self-referential and document-targeting relations are syntactically distinguished.* A relation with `t_G = A_rel` is *about other relations*; a relation with `t_G = A_doc` is *about documents*. The active-subset machinery (ASN-0086's `A_K^Σ` Definition + Definition (nullified), which together compute `A_K^Σ` by filtering `L_K^Σ` against the addresses witnessed by each `L_R^Σ`-tuple's G-coverage) consumes only relations with `t_G = A_rel`; document-classifier predicates consume only relations with `t_G = A_doc`.

(c) *Operational composition is mechanically checkable.* Composing two relations through their slots requires joining slots have compatible target domains. Sh2/Sh3 make compatibility decidable from the shape registry alone.


## Corollary — EffectiveWpSimplification

With Sh1 and Sh3 now established by the preceding sections (Cardinality and Target Domain respectively), and Lemma — LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit established earlier (in its own section after Initial-State Baseline), the named wp-simplification result can now be discharged with no forward references. Downstream proofs (notably Sh4 below) cite this Corollary directly rather than re-deriving the simplification.

**Corollary — EffectiveWpSimplification.** Let Σ be reachable from `Σ_init` under the framework's *Emit_K routing commitment*, with `R ∈ T_cat` (the mandatory baseline registration of Nullify Compatibility — Sh1/Sh3 at `K := R` in Step 1 below, and per-class constancy `K ~ R ⟹ shape(K) = shape(R)` in Step 2's Case B, both presuppose this registration). The simplification result has two scopes that must be distinguished. *(Statement, conditional on substrate reach.)* For every `K ∈ T_admissible`, every `d ∈ dom(Σ.M)`, and every `F, G ∈ Endset`, *at any call site for which the framework's full gate stack admits `Emit_K(Σ, d, F, G)` — equivalently, the call reaches the substrate primitive K.λ at gate 5 of the Gate Ordering (consolidated) clause without being short-circuited to `⊥` by any prior gate*, ASN-0086's `wp_086` simplifies to `d ∈ dom(Σ.M) ∧ K ∈ T_admissible`. *"Sh-conf admits" is not synonymous with "reaches K.λ".* Per the Gate Ordering (consolidated) clause (Sh-conf section), per-K discipline contracts — the *Sh4 idempotency contract*, the *FDD functional-dependency contract*, and the *single-home commitment* — fire as additional gates *between or alongside* Sh-conf's structural gates: gate 1 (SHCD literal-equality check on `d`), gate 3 (Sh4 or FDD Observe-then-Emit contract clauses (i)–(iii)), Sh-conf clauses (a)/(b) at gate 2 and Sh-conf clauses (c)/(d) at gate 4, and K.λ at gate 5. Each of gates 1, 3 can short-circuit the call to `⊥` at a K registered under the corresponding discipline *even when every Sh-conf clause would have admitted the call*; equivalently, "Sh-conf admits the call" is the conjunction of Sh-conf's clauses (a)–(d) only, while "the call reaches K.λ" is the conjunction of *every* gate's admit condition, which strictly includes the Sh-conf admit and also the discipline non-suppression conditions at every applicable discipline. Calls that Sh-conf admits but a per-K discipline gates suppress (e.g., a duplicate-Nullify call at `R` under the Sh4 contract clause (ii), suppressed because `C(F, G, Σ) ≠ ∅`) return `⊥` at gate 1 or gate 3 without ever reaching K.λ; calls that Sh-conf rejects (`K ∉ T_cat`, non-canonical `F` or `G`, cardinality mismatch, or target-domain miss) return `⊥` at gate 2 or gate 4 also without reaching K.λ; in both cases `wp_086` is moot at the call site (its simplification is unevaluated and not claimed). The corollary's "conditional on substrate reach" form therefore reads: at any call site where *the full gate stack of gates 1–4 admits the call*, `wp_086` at gate 5 simplifies to `d ∈ dom(Σ.M) ∧ K ∈ T_admissible`. *(Effective wp at the framework's gate.)* Combining ASN-0086's simplified `wp_086` with the conjuncts every gate adds at the same call site, the *effective wp* of `Emit_K` under the framework — with the postcondition `(a, F, G) ∈ A_K^{Σ'}` requiring a *new* tuple to be deposited in the active subset by the call — simplifies to

`wp_eff(Emit_K(Σ, d, F, G), (a, F, G) ∈ A_K^{Σ'}) = d ∈ dom(Σ.M) ∧ K ∈ T_cat ∧ conf_K^Σ(F, G) ∧ Π_K(d, F, G, Σ)`

where `Π_K(d, F, G, Σ)` is the *per-K discipline non-suppression conjunct* defined as the conjunction of three implications, each conditional on K's discipline status. The *Sh4 idempotency contract* is *not* an opt-in registration: it fires automatically at every K with `shape(K).idem = ⊤` not registered under FDD (the contract's structural trigger is the idempotency flag of K's registered shape, per the Sh4 idempotency contract sub-section above). The remaining two contracts — the *FDD functional-dependency contract* and the *single-home commitment* — are genuine per-K opt-in registrations the layer adds at K's row of the catalog:
