- *K under SingleHomeCoverageDiscipline (opt-in registration):* `d = d_K` (the gate 1 literal-equality test);
- *K with `shape(K).idem = ⊤` and not registered under FDD (Sh4 fires automatically by shape):* `C(F, G, Σ) = ∅` (the gate 3 candidate-set emptiness test under Sh4);
- *K under the FDD functional-dependency contract (opt-in registration):* `C_fd(F, Σ) = ∅` (the gate 3 candidate-set emptiness test under FDD).

The four-case `Π_K` reduction by `(shape(K).idem, FDD-status, SHCD-status)`:

- *(idem = ⊤, not under FDD, not under SHCD):* the Sh4 conjunct fires automatically by shape; FDD and SHCD conjuncts vacuously true. `Π_K = C(F, G, Σ) = ∅`.
- *(idem = ⊤, under FDD):* the FDD conjunct fires (FDD subsumes Sh4 at the gate stack per the *FDD subsumes the Sh4 idempotency contract* paragraph); the Sh4 implication's antecedent "K with `idem = ⊤` and not under FDD" is *false* under FDD registration, so the Sh4 conjunct is vacuously true (the implication holds because its antecedent fails). SHCD vacuously true (SHCD requires `idem = ⊥`). `Π_K = C_fd(F, Σ) = ∅`.
- *(idem = ⊥, not under SHCD):* the Sh4 implication's antecedent "K with `idem = ⊤`" is false, so the Sh4 conjunct is vacuously true. FDD requires `idem = ⊤`, so the FDD conjunct is vacuously true. SHCD vacuously true (not registered). `Π_K = ⊤`.
- *(idem = ⊥, under SHCD):* the SHCD conjunct fires; Sh4 and FDD conjuncts vacuously true (Sh4 requires `idem = ⊤`; FDD requires `idem = ⊤`). `Π_K = d = d_K`.

The *Mutual exclusion of FDD and SHCD at the same K* clause (Gate Ordering, consolidated) forbids the (idem = ⊤, under FDD, under SHCD) and (idem = ⊥, under FDD, under SHCD) configurations: FDD requires `idem = ⊤` while SHCD requires `idem = ⊥`, and `shape(K).idem` is a single fixed value per K (per-class constancy, lifetime constancy). The four-case enumeration above therefore exhausts every admissible K-discipline configuration the framework recognizes. The earlier draft's wording "K registered under no per-K discipline ... `Π_K` reduces vacuously to `⊤`" applied a literal "no opt-in entries" reading that would erroneously drop the Sh4 conjunct at an idem = ⊤ K without an FDD registration; the present enumeration corrects this by routing every idem = ⊤ K (whether FDD-registered or not) to a substantive Π_K conjunct, preserving the framework's preservation argument for Sh4.

The effective-wp form holds at every call site, including the rejected ones: when any conjunct of `wp_eff` fails — `K ∉ T_cat`, `¬conf_K^Σ(F, G)`, or any active `Π_K`-implication's antecedent holding with its consequent failing — `wp_eff` evaluates to false and `Emit_K` returns `⊥` without invoking the substrate primitive K.λ, with the gate that fires identifying which conjunct failed (gate 1 ⇒ `d ≠ d_K` under SHCD; gate 2 ⇒ canonical-form failure ⇒ `¬conf_K^Σ(F, G)`'s clause (a)/(b); gate 3 ⇒ `C(F, G, Σ) ≠ ∅` under Sh4 or `C_fd(F, Σ) ≠ ∅` under FDD; gate 4 ⇒ cardinality or target-domain failure ⇒ `¬conf_K^Σ(F, G)`'s clause (c)/(d)). The wp_086 simplification's conditional precondition "reaches K.λ" is automatically satisfied (vacuously) at every rejected call site, since the substrate is never reached, and the simplification's predictive content over the rejected sub-regime is *that there is none* — exactly what the effective-wp form encodes via the `wp_eff = false` reading. *Necessity of `Π_K` for the named postcondition under the new-tuple-deposited reading.* The corollary's named postcondition `(a, F, G) ∈ A_K^{Σ'}` admits two readings that diverge precisely at contract-suppressed call sites:

(a) *New-tuple-deposited reading (adopted by this corollary).* The postcondition requires the call to deposit a *fresh* tuple `(a, F, G)` into `A_K^{Σ'}` — i.e., `a ∉ dom(Σ.L)` at the pre-call state and `a ∈ dom(Σ'.L)` after the call, with the triple `(a, F, G)` newly added by this very `Emit_K` invocation. This is the reading made explicit at the corollary statement above ("with the postcondition `(a, F, G) ∈ A_K^{Σ'}` requiring a *new* tuple to be deposited in the active subset by the call").

(b) *Set-membership reading (not adopted).* The postcondition would test only whether *some* triple `(a, F, G)` lies in `A_K^{Σ'}` regardless of which call deposited it; a pre-existing tuple already in `A_K^Σ` would satisfy this reading at `Σ' = Σ` even on a suppressed branch.

Under reading (a), `Π_K` is *necessary*: a contract-suppressed call (e.g., a duplicate Nullify under the Sh4 contract clause (ii) at `R`) satisfies the prior three conjuncts `d ∈ dom(Σ.M) ∧ K ∈ T_cat ∧ conf_K^Σ(F, G)` but deposits no new tuple (the suppression returns `⊥` at gate 3 without invoking K.λ, so `Σ' = Σ`; the call deposits no fresh `(a, F, G)` triple, and reading (a)'s "new tuple deposited" condition fails even though a pre-existing tuple in `A_K^Σ` with the same `(F, G)` exists). Adding `Π_K` to `wp_eff` closes this gap: at a contract-suppressed call site, the corresponding `Π_K`-implication's antecedent fires with its consequent failing (`C ≠ ∅` under Sh4, `C_fd ≠ ∅` under FDD, or `d ≠ d_K` under SHCD), so `Π_K` evaluates to false, `wp_eff` evaluates to false, and the wp accurately reports that no new tuple is depositable at this call site. Under reading (b), `Π_K` would *not* be necessary for sufficiency — the pre-existing tuple witnessing the suppression already satisfies the postcondition at the unchanged `Σ' = Σ` — but reading (b) is not the reading the corollary commits to, and downstream consumers that need the active-subset's *audit-event* content (e.g., layer composites tracking per-call retraction events) consume reading (a). The framework's commitment to reading (a) is consistent with the *Audit-slice set-semantics commitment* of Nullify Compatibility, which records that audit-slice multiplicity is preserved through attributed retraction rather than through bare-form duplicate calls; reading (a) is the framework's load-bearing postcondition reading at every call site where `Emit_K`'s return tuple is consumed for fresh-deposit purposes, and is the postcondition the `Π_K` necessity argument is keyed against.

*Proof.* `wp_086` carries two non-trivial conjuncts beyond `d ∈ dom(Σ.M) ∧ K ∈ T_admissible`: `NoCraftedSpanReachesD(Σ, d)` and `(K ≁ R ∨ a_emit(Σ, d) ∉ coverage(G))`. We discharge each via Lemma — LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit (established earlier in its own section) applied at the appropriate `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)`. The proof's two consumed inputs — Sh1/Sh3 at `K := R` (Step 1) and the Lemma (both Steps 1 and 2) — were each established by their own independent argument before the present section; no forward reference enters the proof.

*Step 1 — Discharge of `NoCraftedSpanReachesD(Σ, d)`.* For every prior R-tuple `(b̂, F', G') ∈ L_R^Σ`, the framework's preservation theorems applied at the current state Σ pin down `G'`'s structure: by Sh1 at `K := R` (Cardinality section), `G'` is canonical-slot with `match(|slot_addrs(G')|, 1)` (so `|slot_addrs(G')| = 1`); by Sh3 at `K := R` (Target Domain section), `slot_addrs(G') ⊆ t_G^Σ = A_rel^Σ = dom(Σ.L)`. Sh1 and Sh3 apply because R is registered in `T_cat` per the framework's baseline registration requirement, and both are theorems-under-conformance proved by induction on `↦*` and so deliver current-state structure for every τ ∈ L_R^Σ, including tuples emitted in prior states. The argument does not rely on Sh-conf-at-past-emission together with R2 plus monotonicity; the preservation theorems already discharge that bridge once and for all. So `G' = {(b', δ(1, #b'))}` for a unique `b' ∈ dom(Σ.L)`. By PrefixSpanCoverage (ASN-0043), `coverage(G') = {t : b' ≼ t}`. Applying the Lemma at `b := b'` (legal because `b' ∈ dom(Σ.L)`) gives `b' ⋠ a_emit(Σ, d)`, so `a_emit(Σ, d) ∉ coverage(G')`. Quantifying over `(b̂, F', G') ∈ L_R^Σ` discharges `NoCraftedSpanReachesD(Σ, d)` per ASN-0086's Definition.

*Step 2 — Discharge of `(K ≁ R ∨ a_emit(Σ, d) ∉ coverage(G))`.* Case-split on K's `~`-class.

- *Case A: `K ≁ R`.* The disjunct's first arm holds directly. The second arm need not be considered.
- *Case B: `K ~ R`.* The first arm of the disjunct is false; the second arm must be discharged. For the call to reach K.λ at all, every gate of gates 1–4 must admit (per the Gate Ordering (consolidated) clause); in particular, Sh-conf's structural gates 2 and 4 must admit. With K's shape inherited from R's catalog row via per-class constancy of `shape(·)` (`K ~ R ⟹ shape(K) = shape(R) = (*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)`), Sh-conf clauses (a)/(b) force the call's G canonical-slot, clause (c) at `c_G = 1` forces `|slot_addrs(G)| = 1`, and clause (d) at `t_G = A_rel` forces `slot_addrs(G) ⊆ A_rel^Σ = dom(Σ.L)`. So `G = {(b, δ(1, #b))}` for a unique `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)`. (Gate 3's Sh4 idempotency contract at R also fires at this call site, but its admit condition `C(F, G, Σ) = ∅` constrains *whether* the call advances to gate 5, not the *G-form* itself — the G-form is fixed by gate 2/4's structural admit. Step 2's discharge is independent of gate 3's branch.) Applying the Lemma at this *new-emission* `b` (legal because `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)`, supplied independently of any prior R-tuple — this is the case the Lemma's generalization makes directly applicable) gives `b ⋠ a_emit(Σ, d)`, hence by PrefixSpanCoverage `a_emit(Σ, d) ∉ {t : b ≼ t} = coverage(G)`. The disjunct's second arm holds.

Both Cases A and B discharge the disjunct, completing Step 2. (If any gate of gates 1–4 would reject the call — Sh-conf clause (a)/(b)/(c)/(d) at gate 2 or gate 4, the *Sh4 idempotency contract* or *FDD functional-dependency contract* at gate 3, or the *single-home commitment* at gate 1 — then the call returns `⊥` and never reaches K.λ, so wp_086 is moot at that call site; Step 2's discharge is consumed only at call sites that reach K.λ, which by the Gate Ordering (consolidated) clause are exactly the call sites at which every gate of gates 1–4 admits.)

*Step 3 — Assembling `wp_eff` from `wp_086` and the per-gate admit conjuncts.* With Steps 1 and 2 discharging `wp_086`'s two non-trivial conjuncts, `wp_086` reduces to `d ∈ dom(Σ.M) ∧ K ∈ T_admissible` at every call site that reaches K.λ. The framework's gates 1–4 each contribute their own admit conjuncts at the same call site: gate 2 and gate 4 (Sh-conf clauses (a)–(d)) contribute `K ∈ T_cat ∧ conf_K^Σ(F, G)`; gate 1 (single-home check at SHCD-registered K) contributes `K-under-SHCD ⟹ d = d_K` — the implication is the gate's admit condition, vacuously true at K not under SHCD; gate 3 (the *Sh4 idempotency contract* at Sh4-registered K not under FDD, or the *FDD functional-dependency contract* at FDD-registered K) contributes the candidate-set-empty implication `K-under-Sh4 ⟹ C(F, G, Σ) = ∅` and `K-under-FDD ⟹ C_fd(F, Σ) = ∅` respectively (both vacuously true at K registered under neither discipline). The conjunction of these three implications is `Π_K(d, F, G, Σ)` as defined in the corollary statement. Composing: at any call site that reaches K.λ, `wp_eff = wp_086 ∧ (gate 2/4 admit) ∧ (gate 1 admit) ∧ (gate 3 admit) = (d ∈ dom(Σ.M) ∧ K ∈ T_admissible) ∧ (K ∈ T_cat ∧ conf_K^Σ(F, G)) ∧ Π_K(d, F, G, Σ)`; since `T_cat ⊆ T_admissible`, `K ∈ T_cat` absorbs `K ∈ T_admissible`, yielding the named `wp_eff = d ∈ dom(Σ.M) ∧ K ∈ T_cat ∧ conf_K^Σ(F, G) ∧ Π_K(d, F, G, Σ)`. At a call site rejected by any of gates 1–4, the failing gate's conjunct evaluates to false at the rejection point, `wp_eff` evaluates to false, `Emit_K` returns `⊥`, no new tuple is deposited (`A_K^{Σ'} = A_K^Σ` since `Σ' = Σ` on the `⊥`-branch), and the named postcondition `(a, F, G) ∈ A_K^{Σ'}` (as a *new* tuple) is unsatisfiable — consistent with `wp_eff = false`. ∎

*Coverage-class disjointness from R as a per-shape registration check.* Step 2's case-split on K's `~`-class is load-bearing for the corollary's conclusion at the `K ~ R` branch: Sh-conf's gates on the new emission's G must force `G = {(b, δ(1, #b))}` with `b ∈ A_rel^Σ` for the Lemma — LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit to apply at the new G-slot. Sh-conf clauses (a)–(d) deliver this exactly when `shape(K) = shape(R) = (*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)`, by per-class constancy `K ~ R ⟹ shape(K) = shape(R)`. The contrapositive — `shape(K) ≠ shape(R) ⟹ K ≁ R` — is what makes new catalog rows with shape tuples distinct from R's *automatically* route to Case A (`K ≁ R`) at the case-split, bypassing the `K ~ R` branch's Lemma application. The framework imposes the following *explicit registration check* on every new catalog row, surfaced as **step 0 of Sh5(b)'s minimal review checklist** (Template Catalog section above): the proposed shape tuple is compared componentwise to Retraction's `(*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)`, and a row with `shape(K_rep) = shape(R) ∧ K_rep ≁ R` is rejected at step 0 before any template-body classification fires. A row with `shape ≠ shape(R)` is admissible as a non-R-equivalent shape (step 0 acceptance criterion (i)); a row with `shape = shape(R)` is admissible only via direct `~`-equivalence to R (step 0 acceptance criterion (ii)), and the registration mechanism's representative-resolution step routes K to the same R-class shape value (the new row is operationally a re-naming of R's coverage class rather than a fresh catalog entry). The framework's mandatory baseline R-registration commits one row to shape `(*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)`, and step 0 enforces that any other K registered at the same shape is `~`-equivalent to R. The catalog rows beyond R (Classifier, Tuple-Classifier, DirectedPair, NonIdempotentDirectedPair, Resolution, BundledDirectedPair, Provenance) all carry shape tuples distinct from R's on at least one component (verified at step 0 of their respective registrations), so per-class constancy forces every K registered at any of these rows to satisfy `K ≁ R`, and Step 2's Case A applies uniformly at every such K. The corollary's predictive content is therefore preserved under every catalog extension whose new shape row differs from R's on at least one component — including the BundledDirectedPair shape `(1, *, A_doc, A_doc, ⊤)`, which differs from R's `(*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)` on four components. Future catalog rows that share `c_G = 1, t_G = A_rel` but differ on `c_F` or `t_F` would inherit the same protection by the same argument; a hypothetical future row with `shape = shape(R)` exactly is admissible only after step 0's coverage-equivalence test confirms `K_rep ~ R`, in which case the new row's emissions and R's are interchangeable by `~`-equivalence and the corollary's `K ~ R` branch fires as designed. *Falsifiability.* The step-0 check is mechanically falsifiable in finite time: componentwise shape-tuple equality is decidable over the finite value sets of `{c_F, c_G, t_F, t_G, idem}` (each set has at most four members), and coverage-equivalence `K_rep ~ R` is decidable per the *Decidability of coverage-equality on finite span sets* paragraph (TypedRelationCatalog Definition); a catalog author or downstream tool walking the checklist can detect a violation at step 0 by running these two decidable tests against the proposed row's registration. The earlier draft's framing of this property as an "implicit registration check" was an under-specification — there was no procedural support for the check; the present draft promotes it to step 0 of Sh5(b)'s checklist, supplying the procedural gate the Step 2 Case A precondition relies on.

Downstream proofs that need to reason about `Emit_K`'s preconditions cite this Corollary directly rather than re-deriving the simplification.


## Slot Accessors

The cardinality and target-domain lemmas together permit total slot accessor functions.

**Definition — SetSlotAccessors.** For each `K ∈ T_cat`, define at every state Σ:

`from_K^Σ : L_K^Σ → ℘_fin(shape(K).t_F^Σ)` &nbsp; with &nbsp; `from_K^Σ(a, F, G) = slot_addrs(F)`

`to_K^Σ   : L_K^Σ → ℘_fin(shape(K).t_G^Σ)` &nbsp; with &nbsp; `to_K^Σ(a, F, G) = slot_addrs(G)`

These are total on `L_K^Σ` for any shape: Sh0/Sh1 guarantee canonical-slot form (so `slot_addrs` is defined); Sh2/Sh3 restrict the codomain to the registered target domain at the current state.

*Notational overload: `from_K^Σ` (tuple-to-slot-addresses, this Definition) vs `from_K` (address-to-tuple-set, catalog templates).* Two distinct functions share the name `from_K` (modulo Σ-superscript) in this framework, and the distinction is significant enough to flag explicitly here at the introduction of the first variant. The variant defined in *this* Definition (`from_K^Σ` and its partner `to_K^Σ`) is the *slot accessor* on tuples: input is a tuple `τ ∈ L_K^Σ`, output is the finite set of slot addresses extracted from `τ`'s endset. By contrast, the catalog template families below (DirectedPair, NonIdempotentDirectedPair, Retraction, Provenance) introduce a function also written `from_K` (typically without Σ-superscript in the catalog row) whose role is the *inverse image* of the point accessor `from₁` at a given address: input is an address `a` (in `A_doc`, `A_rel`, or `A` depending on the row's `t_F`), output is `{τ ∈ A_K^Σ : from₁(τ) = a}` — the set of active tuples whose from-slot equals `a`. The Σ-superscript convention disambiguates: `from_K^Σ(τ)` is the slot accessor (tuple-input); `from_K(a)` is the inverse-image template (address-input). At every catalog row's first use of `from_K`, the row's prose body makes the address-input semantics explicit by reading `from_K(a) ≡ {τ ∈ A_K^Σ : ...}`, distinguishing it visually from the slot-accessor reading. Consumers reasoning at dense proof sites (e.g., the Sh4 contract's clause (i.a) Observe-result analysis, the FDD contract's `C_fd(F, Σ)` computation) should consult the Σ-superscript and the argument's type — `τ ∈ L_K^Σ` versus `a ∈ A_doc^Σ` (or `A_rel^Σ`, `A^Σ`) — to determine which variant is meant; the two functions are never composable in either order (a slot-address-set output is not a valid tuple input, and a tuple-set output is not a valid address input). Renaming was considered (e.g., `from_addrs_of_K_tuple` vs `K_tuples_with_from`) but rejected because the overloaded names are entrenched in the canonical catalog and the Σ-superscript is sufficient to disambiguate at every textual site that matters.

**Definition — PointSlotAccessors.** For shapes with `c_F = 1`:

`from₁ : L_K^Σ → shape(K).t_F^Σ` &nbsp; with &nbsp; `from₁(τ) = the unique element of from_K^Σ(τ)`

For shapes with `c_G = 1`:

`to₁ : L_K^Σ → shape(K).t_G^Σ` &nbsp; with &nbsp; `to₁(τ) = the unique element of to_K^Σ(τ)`

For shapes with `c_F = 0|1` or `c_G = 0|1`, the partial accessor returns `⊥` (undefined) when the slot is empty:

`from₁⁻ : L_K^Σ → shape(K).t_F^Σ ∪ {⊥}` defined when `c_F ∈ {1, 0|1}`; returns `⊥` iff `|slot_addrs(F)| = 0`, and returns the unique element of `slot_addrs(F)` otherwise.

`to₁⁻ : L_K^Σ → shape(K).t_G^Σ ∪ {⊥}` defined analogously when `c_G ∈ {1, 0|1}`; returns `⊥` iff `|slot_addrs(G)| = 0`, and returns the unique element of `slot_addrs(G)` otherwise.

*Codomain convention for partial templates.* Every partial-valued template introduced in this framework declares its codomain as `(typed allocated set) ∪ {⊥}` where `⊥` is the distinguished partiality token. Concretely: `from₁⁻` lands in `t_F^Σ ∪ {⊥}`; `to₁⁻` lands in `t_G^Σ ∪ {⊥}`; `K_target_of` (DirectedPair under FDD) lands in `A_doc^Σ ∪ {⊥}`; `latest_K_for_addr` (NonIdempotentDirectedPair under SHCD) lands in `A_K^Σ ∪ {⊥}` (returning a tuple rather than an address, the tuple-valued analog). The pattern is uniform: every template whose body's "unique element" or "argmax" step can fail to produce a value declares the failure value as `⊥` and the success value's domain explicitly.

*Notational convention for shape symbols on the domain side.* Throughout this framework, when a template's signature writes a *bare* shape symbol (`A_doc`, `A_rel`, `A`, `t_F`, `t_G`) on the *domain* side — e.g., `from_K : A_doc → ℘_fin(A_K^Σ)` in the DirectedPair catalog row, `K_target_of : A_doc → A_doc^Σ ∪ {⊥}` in the singleton accessor, `pair_K : A_doc × A_doc → Bool` in the base templates — the bare symbol carries an *implicit Σ-quantification at the state of evaluation*: equivalently, the bare domain symbol `A_doc` is shorthand for "the address must lie in `A_doc^Σ` at the state Σ at which the template is invoked". The codomain side, by contrast, uses the explicit state-indexed form (`A_doc^Σ`, `A_K^Σ`, `t_F^Σ ∪ {⊥}`, etc.) directly. The asymmetry is notational only: at any concrete invocation state Σ, the domain symbol expands per the *Shape* Definition's symbol-expansion rule (`A_doc ↦ A_doc^Σ`, etc.), so the signature reads uniformly as `A_doc^Σ → A_doc^Σ ∪ {⊥}` (or the appropriate partner) when Σ is fixed. Equivalent signature spellings — e.g., `K_target_of^Σ : A_doc^Σ → A_doc^Σ ∪ {⊥}` with explicit superscript on both sides — are sometimes used when emphasis on state-dependence is required at the call site; both spellings denote the same family of state-indexed functions and the conversion is purely textual. The catalog rows favor the bare-symbol form on the domain side as a readability convention: it lets a row's signature row read independently of any one state, with the implicit Σ supplied by the consuming context.

**Lemma — SlotAccessorTotality.** When `shape(K).c_F = 1`, `from₁` is a total function on `L_K^Σ`. Similarly for `to₁` when `c_G = 1`.

*Proof.* By Sh0, every `τ ∈ L_K^Σ` has `F` in canonical-slot form with `|slot_addrs(F)| = 1` (since `match(n, 1) ⟺ n = 1`). A finite set of cardinality 1 has a unique element. Define `from₁(τ)` as that element. By Sh2, this element lies in `t_F^Σ`. ∎

For the rest of this document, we drop subscripts and write `from`, `to` when the shape unambiguously fixes which accessor is meant. We additionally use `addr(τ) = a` for the tuple address (R1, AddressInjectivity, ASN-0086).


## Idempotency (Sh4)

**Sh4 — IdempotencyDiscipline (conditional on the *Sh4 idempotency contract*).** When `shape(K).idem = ⊤` and the calling layer honors the *Sh4 idempotency contract* (defined below; the contract's clauses (i)–(iii) gate every `Emit_K` call site for such K), at most one *active* tuple in `L_K` shares any given slot-address pair. The contract is the load-bearing layer commitment: Sh4's conclusion fails at any state reachable by a contract-violating step, so callers depending on Sh4 must verify the contract's clauses are honored at every K with `shape(K).idem = ⊤`. For `τ = (a, F, G) ∈ L_K^Σ` we write `F_τ := F` and `G_τ := G` for the slot endsets of τ. Then:

`(A τ, τ' ∈ A_K^Σ : (slot_addrs(F_τ), slot_addrs(G_τ)) = (slot_addrs(F_{τ'}), slot_addrs(G_{τ'})) :: addr(τ) = addr(τ'))`

*Universal scope.* The two bound variables `τ` and `τ'` range independently over `A_K^Σ`, including the diagonal `τ = τ'`. On the diagonal the conclusion `addr(τ) = addr(τ')` reads `addr(τ) = addr(τ)`, satisfied by reflexivity of equality, so the diagonal contributes no constraint. The substantive content is off-diagonal: for any two *distinct* active tuples `τ, τ'` whose slot-address pairs match, Sh4 forces `addr(τ) = addr(τ')` — combined with R1 (AddressInjectivity, ASN-0086), the equality of addresses then collapses `τ = τ'`, contradicting the off-diagonal assumption. Read contrapositively: no two distinct active tuples in `A_K^Σ` share a slot-address pair. Subsequent appeals to "pairwise distinctness on `A_K^Σ`" mean exactly this off-diagonal content; the diagonal is dispatched once and for all by reflexivity.

*Sh4 idempotency contract (the layer-discipline contract for Sh4).* Sh4 is realized through a contract the calling layer commits to honor uniformly across every reachable state — the *Sh4 idempotency contract*, distinct from the *Emit_K routing commitment* of Scope and Substrate Scaffolding (which routes calls through `Emit_K`) and from the *FDD functional-dependency contract* (defined under FunctionalDependencyDiscipline below). Subsequent text uses *Sh4 idempotency contract* to disambiguate when more than one commitment is in scope. For each `K ∈ T_cat` with `shape(K).idem = ⊤`, on every `Emit_K(Σ, d, F, G)` call site the layer enforces the following protocol:

*Ordering with Sh-conf.* The Sh4 contract's position in the call-site gate sequence is specified by the *Gate Ordering (consolidated)* clause in the Sh-conf section above (specifically gate 3 of that sequence). The canonical-form gate (Sh-conf clauses (a)/(b)) fires first; the Sh4 contract clauses (i)–(iii) fire next; the cardinality/target-domain gates (Sh-conf clauses (c)/(d)) fire after the contract. Suppression at clause (ii) returns `⊥` without reaching the cardinality/target-domain gates; issue at clause (iii) advances to those gates. The contract presupposes canonical-form `F, G` as the precondition discharged by gate 2 — what makes every step of the contract well-defined.

(i) Before issuing the emission, the layer computes the candidate set
`C(F, G, Σ) := {τ ∈ A_K^Σ : slot_addrs(F_τ) = slot_addrs(F) ∧ slot_addrs(G_τ) = slot_addrs(G)}`
via the two-step procedure:

&nbsp;&nbsp;(i.a) Query `Observe_K(slot_addrs(F), slot_addrs(G), oper)` — a well-typed call by the ordering above: Sh-conf clauses (a)/(b) have already gated canonical-form, so `slot_addrs(F)` and `slot_addrs(G)` are finite subsets of `T` at this point in the protocol. Observe_K's semantics returns the (finite) set of active tuples whose slot coverages prefix-contain the pattern addresses — concretely, `{τ ∈ A_K^Σ : slot_addrs(F) ⊆ coverage(F_τ) ∧ slot_addrs(G) ⊆ coverage(G_τ)}`. Under Sh0/Sh1, every `τ ∈ A_K^Σ` has canonical-form slot endsets, so `coverage(F_τ) = ⋃ {{t : y ≼ t} : y ∈ slot_addrs(F_τ)}` and `slot_addrs(F) ⊆ coverage(F_τ)` iff every `x ∈ slot_addrs(F)` has some `y ∈ slot_addrs(F_τ)` with `y ≼ x`. *Contract correctness is independent of clause (d) on the new emission's F.* The contract's correctness — that the candidate set `C(F, G, Σ)` it computes equals the specified `{τ ∈ A_K^Σ : slot_addrs(F_τ) = slot_addrs(F) ∧ slot_addrs(G_τ) = slot_addrs(G)}` — does not depend on whether the new emission's F satisfies Sh-conf clause (d); it depends only on (i.a)'s Observe returning a finite over-approximation and (i.b)'s post-filter exactly testing slot-address-set equality. Even when (i.a) over-approximates by including τ whose `slot_addrs(F_τ)` strictly contains `slot_addrs(F)`, the post-filter (i.b) rejects those τ, leaving exactly `C(F, G, Σ)` regardless of any tightness property of (i.a) itself. *Over-approximation tightness — conditional on Sh-conf clause (d) holding for the new emission's F.* When Sh-conf clause (d) holds for the new emission (i.e., `slot_addrs(F) ⊆ t_F^Σ ⊆ A^Σ`, the condition that the cardinality/target-domain gates execute after this contract per the *Ordering with Sh-conf* clause above), the Observe in (i.a) tightens to *exactly* the set of τ with `slot_addrs(F_τ) ⊇ slot_addrs(F)`, by the following per-element argument. *Per-element argument.* Fix any `x ∈ slot_addrs(F)`. Under the conditional hypothesis, `x ∈ t_F^Σ ⊆ A^Σ`, so `x` is allocated. The pattern-containment hypothesis supplies some `y ∈ slot_addrs(F_τ)` with `y ≼ x`. By Sh2 applied to `τ`, `y ∈ t_F^Σ ⊆ A^Σ`, so `y` is allocated. By AllocatedAddressAntichain at the witness `y` (using `x ∈ A^Σ` and `y ≼ x`), `y = x`. So every `x ∈ slot_addrs(F)` is itself an element of `slot_addrs(F_τ)`; quantifying over `x` gives `slot_addrs(F) ⊆ slot_addrs(F_τ)`. *Multi-slot generalization (F-side and G-side, symmetric).* The same per-element argument applies independently to each `x ∈ slot_addrs(F)` regardless of `|slot_addrs(F)|` — including `c_F = *` shapes with `n ≥ 2`, where the argument fires `n` times to yield set-containment from per-element containment. Symmetric argument with G in place of F — substituting `c_G = *` for `c_F = *` and Sh3 for Sh2 at every step (witness landing: `y ∈ slot_addrs(G_τ) ⊆ t_G^Σ ⊆ A^Σ` by Sh3; AllocatedAddressAntichain firing at each `y ≼ x` with `x ∈ slot_addrs(G) ⊆ t_G^Σ ⊆ A^Σ` under the same conditional hypothesis on the G-side) — yields `slot_addrs(G) ⊆ slot_addrs(G_τ)`. The G-side argument fires `n` times at G-side cardinality `n ≥ 2` exactly as the F-side does, including for `c_G = *` shapes like BundledDirectedPair where `|slot_addrs(G)| ≥ 2` is admissible. The symmetric per-element argument is identical in structure to the F-side, differing only in side-labels — no separate paragraph is required to carry it. So under the conditional hypothesis the Observe over-approximates exact slot-set equality only by `slot_addrs(F_τ) ⊇ slot_addrs(F)` and `slot_addrs(G_τ) ⊇ slot_addrs(G)` (not by, e.g., admitting τ whose slot-address sets are disjoint from the pattern); the post-filter (i.b) then closes to exact equality. When the conditional hypothesis fails — Sh-conf clause (d) is about to reject the call at the next gate — the post-filter (i.b) is still well-defined (it tests exact equality of finite slot-address sets) and still yields exactly `C(F, G, Σ)` for the contract's clauses (ii)/(iii); the over-approximation may be looser without compromising the contract's correctness, but the contract returns `⊥` either via clause (ii) or via the next-gate Sh-conf rejection. The tightness derivation is *expository* for the (d)-conformant path; the contract's specification of `C(F, G, Σ)` is independent of it.

&nbsp;&nbsp;(i.b) Post-filter the result of (i.a): retain only τ with `slot_addrs(F_τ) = slot_addrs(F)` and `slot_addrs(G_τ) = slot_addrs(G)`. Each returned τ has canonical-slot form by Sh0/Sh1, so `slot_addrs(F_τ)` is a well-defined finite set; exact-equality checks against the finite pattern slot-address sets are decidable in finite time. The composition (i.a) ∘ (i.b) yields exactly `C(F, G, Σ)` as specified above.

(ii) If `C(F, G, Σ) ≠ ∅`, the emission is *suppressed*: no `→`-step occurs.

(iii) Only if `C(F, G, Σ) = ∅` does the layer issue `Emit_K(Σ, d, F, G)`.

The layer commits to executing clauses (i)–(iii) atomically with respect to other emitters and retractors at the same `~`-equivalence class of K — emission and retraction events at any K' with `K' ~ K` that could split (i)'s observation from (iii)'s emission must be serialized by the layer. `L_K` is `~`-class indexed (ASN-0086, `L_K^Σ = L_{K'}^Σ` whenever `K ~ K'`), so emitters at distinct-but-`~`-equivalent type indices write to the same active subset; atomicity scoped at the `~`-class is what closes the race.

*Scope: single-process substrate.* The framework's scope is restricted to single-process substrates, in which `↦`-transitions are sequential by construction — between any two transitions there is a well-defined "before" and "after" state and no third transition interleaves. Under this scope, "atomicity of clauses (i)–(iii)" reduces operationally to "within a single `Emit_K` call site, clauses (i.a), (i.b), and (iii) execute in sequence without intervening `↦`-steps from any other source." A layer satisfies this by issuing the `Observe_K` call of (i.a), the post-filter of (i.b), and (if `C = ∅`) the substrate K.λ-step of (iii) as a single uninterrupted procedure call. The "concurrent emitter or retractor" wording above refers to this within-call sequencing requirement: no other Sh4-emitter at a `~`-equivalent K may interleave a `↦`-step between (i) and (iii) of an in-progress call. Multi-process substrates — where two emitter processes might race to compute `C(F, G, Σ)` against the same state — would require a coordination protocol beyond the within-call sequentiality this framework assumes; the Open Questions section flags cross-process consistency as not addressed.

*Cross-`~`-class concurrency is benign.* Concurrent `Emit_R` retracting K-tuples while `Emit_K` is in flight (with `R ≁ K`) does not require serialization. The only mutation an `Emit_R` step applies to `A_K` is removal (a K-tuple is filtered out by `nullified(Σ)` membership); removing a tuple from `A_K` cannot violate the pairwise-slot-pair-distinctness condition, only restore it (a removed tuple is no longer a candidate witness for any pair-violation). The Sh4 atomicity scope is therefore correctly tightened to the `~`-equivalence class of K and not widened to all retractors. When `R ~ K` (K is itself the retraction relation), retraction and emission write to the same `A_K = A_R`, and the layer's atomicity at the `~`-class scope already handles the race — this is the Case D analyzed below.

*Preservation under the contract.* Sh4 holds at every reachable state under the contract, by induction on `↦*`. Fix `K ∈ T_cat` with `shape(K).idem = ⊤`.

*Stratification.* Sh4's preservation argument consumes Sh0–Sh3 (and their consumed accessor totalities, e.g., SlotAccessorTotality) as previously-established lemmas evaluated at the current state Σ — for instance, the contract specification's per-element argument in clause (i.a) cites Sh2 to land witnesses `y` in `A^Σ`. Sh4 additionally consumes the *Lemma — RetractionSelfFreshness* (stated immediately below this paragraph, before the induction's base case) at Case C's `K ~ R` sub-case and at Case D's case-description; the sub-lemma in turn consumes Sh1, Sh3, and Lemma — LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit. The stratification within Sh0–Sh3 is: Sh0 and Sh1 are each proved independently against the empty-baseline `Σ_init` (their bodies reference only the canonical-form predicate and the cardinality match, both of which Sh-conf gates directly); Sh2 then consumes Sh0 as a state-indexed lemma for the well-formedness of `slot_addrs(F)` in its body, and Sh3 consumes Sh1 symmetrically (see the *Well-formedness* clauses of Sh2 and Sh3 above). Sh4 then consumes Sh0–Sh3 as state-indexed lemmas, plus RetractionSelfFreshness at the cited sub-cases. No member of Sh0–Sh3 appears in another's inductive hypothesis — the cross-consumption is at the body's well-formedness level (Sh0/Sh1 supply `slot_addrs(·)` well-formedness for Sh2/Sh3's universal quantifier), not inside their inductive arguments. The full stratification: Sh0, Sh1 first (independent); then Sh2 (consumes Sh0 for body well-formedness), Sh3 (consumes Sh1 symmetrically); then LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit (independent of Sh0–Sh3); then RetractionSelfFreshness (consumes Sh1, Sh3, LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit); then Sh4 (consumes Sh0–Sh3 as state-indexed lemmas plus RetractionSelfFreshness at Cases C/D). No cycle: Sh4 never appears as a premise of Sh0–Sh3's proofs or of RetractionSelfFreshness's proof.

**Lemma — RetractionSelfFreshness.** Let Σ be reachable from `Σ_init` under the framework's *Emit_K routing commitment*, with R registered in `T_cat` per the framework's baseline registration requirement (Nullify Compatibility). Suppose every framework gate at an `Emit_R(Σ, d, F, G)` call site admits the call — Sh-conf clauses (a)–(d) all pass and the *Sh4 idempotency contract* clause (iii) fires — so the call proceeds to K.λ at home `d` and deposits a fresh tuple τ_new with `addr(τ_new) = a_emit(Σ, d)`, producing result state Σ'. Then:

`addr(τ_new) ∉ nullified(Σ')`

— equivalently, `τ_new ∈ A_R^{Σ'}`.

*Proof.* By Definition (nullified, ASN-0086) at Σ', `addr(τ_new) ∈ nullified(Σ') ⟺ (E (b̂, F', G') ∈ L_R^{Σ'} :: addr(τ_new) ∈ coverage(G'))`; the existential ranges over `L_R^{Σ'} = L_R^Σ ∪ {τ_new}` (R3 monotonicity on `L_R`, since this is a class-(iii) `Emit_R` step that adds exactly τ_new to `L_R`). Two witnesses must be ruled out, both by Lemma — LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit:

(i) *Self-nullification check (witness `(b̂, F', G') = τ_new`).* Sh-conf at the new emission admitted the call only because clauses (a)–(d) held; under `shape(R) = (*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)`, clauses (b)/(c)/(d) force `G_{τ_new} = {(b, δ(1, #b))}` for a unique `b ∈ A_rel^Σ`, so by PrefixSpanCoverage (ASN-0043) `coverage(G_{τ_new}) = {t : b ≼ t}`. Lemma — LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit applied at `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)` and `d := home(τ_new) ∈ dom(Σ.M)` yields `b ⋠ a_emit(Σ, d) = addr(τ_new)`, so `addr(τ_new) ∉ {t : b ≼ t} = coverage(G_{τ_new})`.

(ii) *Cross-nullification check (witness ranges over `L_R^Σ`).* For every prior `(b̂, F', G') ∈ L_R^Σ`, Sh1 at `K := R` gives `G'` canonical-slot with `|slot_addrs(G')| = 1`, and Sh3 at `K := R` gives `slot_addrs(G') ⊆ A_rel^Σ ⊆ A_rel^{Σ'}`. So `G' = {(b', δ(1, #b'))}` for a unique `b' ∈ dom(Σ.L) ⊆ dom(Σ'.L)`. Lemma — LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit applied at `b'` and the same `d = home(τ_new)` yields `b' ⋠ a_emit(Σ, d) = addr(τ_new)`, so `addr(τ_new) ∉ coverage(G')`. Quantifying over all `(b̂, F', G') ∈ L_R^Σ` discharges the cross-nullification disjunct.

Combining (i) and (ii) over the full disjunction `L_R^{Σ'} = L_R^Σ ∪ {τ_new}`: for every witness in `L_R^{Σ'}`, `addr(τ_new) ∉ coverage(G')`. Hence `addr(τ_new) ∉ nullified(Σ')`, and τ_new ∈ A_R^{Σ'} by Definition (A_K^Σ, ASN-0086). ∎

*Scope and consumption.* The Lemma's hypothesis — "every framework gate admits the call" — exhausts the conditions under which an `Emit_R` step actually deposits τ_new in `L_R^{Σ'}` (per the *Gate Ordering (consolidated)* clause in the Sh-conf section); at call sites where any gate rejects (`⊥`-return), no τ_new is produced and the Lemma's conclusion is vacuous. The Lemma is consumed at two sites in the Sh4 induction below: Case C's `K ~ R` sub-case uses part (i) alone (the τ_new produced at that step is not self-nullified, so the complementary sub-case is empty), and Case D cites the full conclusion to establish that the case-description equation `A_R^{Σ'} = (A_R^Σ ∪ {τ_new}) \ leaving` *holds as a theorem*, not as a stipulation — Case D's structural set-up presupposes τ_new joins A_R^{Σ'}, and the Lemma discharges that presupposition. Stating RetractionSelfFreshness here, before the induction begins, separates the structural establishment of "what enters A_R^{Σ'}" from the pairwise-distinctness arguments of Cases C and D on the established active subset.

*Base.* At `Σ_0 = Σ_init` (per the framework's empty-baseline assumption, *Initial-state baseline for preservation proofs* in the Initial-State Baseline section above), `L_K^{Σ_0} = A_K^{Σ_0} = ∅`; Sh4's universal is vacuous.

*Step (Case A: `A_K^{Σ'} = A_K^Σ`).* The active subset is unchanged at K. Sh4 is inherited directly. The case is *defined* by the equation `A_K^{Σ'} = A_K^Σ`, which the IH discharges without enumerating which transition classes achieve it; Case A's preservation is closed at the case-equation alone, with no further lemma consumed at the closure step. Because `A_K^{Σ'} = A_K^Σ` as sets *of triples* (not merely as sets of addresses), the triple values at both states are equal by set extensionality, so Sh4's body — quantifying over triples — yields the same Boolean at both states without any need to re-derive triple values from addresses. *Exhaustiveness of the framework's transition vocabulary.* ASN-0086 (`→ — DomExtendingTransition` Definition and the broader `↦` relation) commits to the transition vocabulary `↦ = → ∪ (↦ \ →) = (K.σ ∪ K.α ∪ K.λ) ∪ arrangement-modifying` — exhaustive of the substrate's `↦`-step taxonomy at the framework's document-level semantic scope. The framework operates within this scope (the FEBE-level operations Nelson identifies as cleanly partitioned along storage/arrangement/links plus arrangement-modifying); transition classes the full Xanadu substrate may admit at scopes outside this framework's commitment (e.g., publication-state transitions, economic transitions, BEBE topology migrations, document/version birth as distinct from K.σ document-registration) fall outside `↦`'s vocabulary as the framework consumes it, and are therefore outside Sh4's preservation scope by construction rather than by analytical gap. Within the framework's scope, the enumeration of principal transitions below is therefore *exhaustive*, not merely indicative. The classes are: all K.σ-steps and K.α-steps (which preserve `Σ.L` pointwise, hence `L_K` and `nullified` both untouched at K); K.λ-steps emitting a tuple of any type `K'` with `K' ≁ K` and `K' ≁ R` (neither `L_K` nor `nullified` extends at K); K.λ-steps emitting a tuple of type `K'` with `K' ≁ K` and `K' ~ R` *when no τ ∈ A_K^Σ is in the new R-tuple's G-coverage* (such steps extend `nullified` but fail to remove any K-tuple from `A_K`, so the case-equation still holds — the complementary sub-regime where some τ ∈ A_K^Σ *is* nullified by such a step contracts `A_K` and is routed to Case C below); and all arrangement-modifying steps in `↦ \ →` (by LinkStoreInvarianceUnderArrangement, `Σ'.L = Σ.L` pointwise hence `L_K^{Σ'} = L_K^Σ`, `L_R^{Σ'} = L_R^Σ`, and `nullified(Σ') = nullified(Σ)`). Each class satisfies the case-equation by direct inspection of the substrate transition vocabulary; together with the exhaustiveness commitment above (the framework's `↦`-vocabulary is exactly these four classes), every `↦`-step within the framework's scope that lands in Case A is among the enumerated classes. *Role of the cited expository lemmas (LinkStoreInvarianceUnderArrangement and the implicit appeal to R2 via "L_K unchanged").* The LinkStoreInvarianceUnderArrangement citation here licenses the *prior* step "every step in the enumerated arrangement-modifying transition class produces `A_K^{Σ'} = A_K^Σ`" — i.e., it establishes that arrangement steps land in Case A's case-equation; it does *not* license Case A's closure step itself (which is set-extensional inheritance from the IH at the unchanged active subset). Any implicit appeal to R2 (TupleAddressPermanence, ASN-0086) at K.σ/K.α/non-K-coverage-equivalent K.λ steps plays the same routing role: R2's "existing tuples retain their values across `→`-steps" is what underwrites those transition classes also landing in the case-equation, without being consumed at the closure. The earlier draft's disclaimer "transitions outside the enumeration but still satisfying the case-equation are equally admitted" is retracted: under the present exhaustiveness commitment, there are no `↦`-steps within the framework's scope outside the four enumerated classes, so the disclaimer's residual set is empty and the enumeration is load-bearing rather than expository.

*Step (Case B: `A_K^{Σ'} = A_K^Σ ∪ {τ_new}`, a K.λ-step at type K with `K ≁ R`).* The case is structurally restricted to `K ≁ R` rather than carrying a conditional "no concurrent nullification" qualifier: by the class-decomposition of `↦` (per ASN-0086's `→` Definition and `↦`'s broader transition relation), concurrent nullification at the same step happens only at `Emit_R` steps, since `nullified(Σ)`'s definition reads over `L_R^Σ`'s G-coverages and only `Emit_R` extends `L_R`. A non-Retraction-typed K.λ-step (i.e., the `K ≁ R` regime selected here) cannot extend `L_R^Σ` and therefore cannot expand `nullified(Σ)`, so no τ ∈ A_K^Σ leaves `A_K` at this step — concurrent nullification is structurally impossible in Case B, not a conditional precondition. The complementary `K ~ R` regime is routed to Case D below, where the step is by definition an `Emit_R`-step at the same `~`-class as K and the simultaneous addition-and-possible-contraction structure is handled via the candidate-set argument plus the structural bound `|leaving| ≤ 1`. The case-decomposition exhausts the simultaneous-effect possibilities at the K.λ class: Case B covers `K ≁ R` (no possible nullification), Case D covers `K ~ R` (possible nullification handled explicitly). By the *Emit_K routing commitment*, this K.λ-step originates as an `Emit_K` call (with K or `~`-equivalent registered type). By the *Sh4 idempotency contract* clause (iii), the emission proceeded only because `C(F, G, Σ) = ∅`. Let `τ_new` be the new tuple. Suppose, toward contradiction, that some prior `τ ∈ A_K^Σ` satisfies `(slot_addrs(F_τ), slot_addrs(G_τ)) = (slot_addrs(F_{τ_new}), slot_addrs(G_{τ_new}))`. Then by definition `τ ∈ C(F, G, Σ)`, contradicting `C(F, G, Σ) = ∅`. So no such `τ` exists, and `A_K^{Σ'}` extends with a slot-pair-unique element. The pairwise condition is preserved: existing pairs were Sh4-distinct by IH; `τ_new` shares no slot-pair with any prior active tuple.

*Step (Case C: `A_K^{Σ'} ⊆ A_K^Σ` strictly, an `Emit_R`-step nullifying one or more K-tuple addresses without adding to A_K).* Retraction filters `A_K^Σ` by `nullified(Σ)` membership but cannot introduce new K-tuples; the pairwise condition is preserved on any subset. This case fires when `K ≁ R` (so the Emit_R step's `τ_new` does not join `A_K`). The complementary `K ~ R` sub-case — where one might expect "self-retraction" of `τ_new` by the same step — is empty by Lemma — RetractionSelfFreshness (stated above, before the Base), part (i): under `K ~ R` the Lemma's self-nullification clause gives `addr(τ_new) ∉ coverage(G_{τ_new})`, so `τ_new` is never self-nullified. The `K ~ R` simultaneous-effect case where τ_new adds to A_R and one or more *prior* R-tuples leave is Case D below.

*Step (Case D: K is `~`-equivalent to R, an `Emit_R`-step that adds τ_new to A_R while potentially nullifying prior R-tuple addresses).* This is the addition-with-possible-contraction case for the retraction relation. By Lemma — RetractionSelfFreshness (stated above, before the Base), τ_new ∈ A_R^{Σ'}; combined with `nullified(·)`-membership filtering of `L_R^{Σ'}`, the post-step active subset is `A_R^{Σ'} = (A_R^Σ ∪ {τ_new}) \ leaving` where `leaving := {τ ∈ A_R^Σ : addr(τ) ∈ coverage(G_{τ_new})}` is the (possibly empty) subset of prior R-tuples whose addresses fall into the new emission's G-coverage. The case-description equation is therefore a theorem (the Lemma discharges τ_new's membership in A_R^{Σ'}; `leaving`'s definition is the standard `nullified(·)`-membership filter), not a stipulation. When `leaving = ∅` the step is operationally pure addition (`+1, 0`); when `|leaving| = 1` it is simultaneous addition-and-contraction (`+1, −1`). Both sub-cases are routed to Case D rather than Case B because Case B's "no concurrent nullification" qualifier is contingent on `K ≁ R`; under `K ~ R` even the `leaving = ∅` sub-case requires Case D's argument, which inspects the candidate-set computation against the full pre-step `A_R^Σ` rather than relying on `Σ.L` invariance under K.σ/K.α/non-K-coverage-equivalent K.λ steps.

*Structural bound on `|leaving|`.* By Sh-conf at Retraction (`c_G = 1` with `t_G = A_rel` and the canonical-slot gate), `G_{τ_new}` has the form `{(b, δ(1, #b))}` for a unique `b`, with Sh-conf clause (d) at `t_G = A_rel` forcing `{b} ⊆ A_rel^Σ`, hence `b ∈ A_rel^Σ = dom(Σ.L)` — this membership is established explicitly here from clause (d), so that the antichain application below has its `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)` precondition discharged before being invoked. By PrefixSpanCoverage (ASN-0043), `coverage(G_{τ_new}) = {t : b ≼ t}`. The set `{a ∈ dom(Σ.L) : b ≼ a}` is now characterized through two separated steps. *Step 1 — `b` lies in the set.* By Prefix reflexivity (Prefix, ASN-0034), `b ≼ b`; combined with `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)` (established just above from Sh-conf clause (d)), `b ∈ {a ∈ dom(Σ.L) : b ≼ a}`. *Step 2 — every other element of the set equals `b`.* Take any `a ∈ dom(Σ.L)` with `b ≼ a`. By R0a (FlatLinkDomain, ASN-0086), `dom(Σ.L)` is a tumbler-prefix antichain — i.e., for any pair `x, y ∈ dom(Σ.L)` with `x ≼ y`, R0a gives `x = y`. The pair `(b, a)` with both `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)` (Step 1's membership fact) and `a ∈ dom(Σ.L)` (the supposition) satisfies R0a's hypothesis at `(b, a)`; combined with `b ≼ a`, R0a forces `a = b`. *Composing Steps 1 and 2.* The set `{a ∈ dom(Σ.L) : b ≼ a}` contains `b` (by Step 1) and contains no other element (by Step 2 applied to any candidate other element), so `{a ∈ dom(Σ.L) : b ≼ a} = {b}` as sets — the equality, not merely a subset relation, follows from the conjunction of Steps 1 (membership lower bound) and 2 (extensional uniqueness upper bound). The active set `A_R^Σ` consists of triples whose `addr(·)` lies in `dom(Σ.L)`, so `leaving = {τ ∈ A_R^Σ : addr(τ) ∈ coverage(G_{τ_new})} = {τ ∈ A_R^Σ : addr(τ) ∈ {a ∈ dom(Σ.L) : b ≼ a}} = {τ ∈ A_R^Σ : addr(τ) ∈ {b}} = {τ ∈ A_R^Σ : addr(τ) = b}` (the middle substitution restricts `coverage(G_{τ_new}) = {t : b ≼ t}` to its intersection with `dom(Σ.L)`, which is `{a ∈ dom(Σ.L) : b ≼ a}` by set comprehension, then applies the just-established `{a ∈ dom(Σ.L) : b ≼ a} = {b}` identity). By R1 (AddressInjectivity, ASN-0086), at most one τ ∈ A_R^Σ has `addr(τ) = b`, so `|leaving| ≤ 1`. The Case D step is therefore at most `+1, −1`, not an arbitrary swap; the substantive content is the case structure (`+1, 0` when `leaving = ∅` per the widened Case D definition above, `+1, −1` when `leaving = {τ_old}` for one specific prior R-tuple `τ_old`), not the cardinality of `leaving`. The earlier draft's compact "By R0a... so `{a ∈ dom(Σ.L) : b ≼ a} = {b}`" understated the antecedents: R0a alone delivers the antichain implication `x ≼ y ⟹ x = y` for any `x, y ∈ dom(Σ.L)`, but the singleton equality requires both `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)` (from Sh-conf clause (d), feeding R0a's pair hypothesis at the second slot, and feeding Prefix reflexivity for the membership-of-`b` clause) and `b ≼ b` (Prefix reflexivity, populating the singleton's lower bound); without these two intermediate facts the equality would be at best a subset relation `{a ∈ dom(Σ.L) : b ≼ a} ⊆ {b}` with no guarantee the singleton has a member. The present formulation makes all three antecedents (Sh-conf clause (d), Prefix reflexivity, R0a) visible at their citation sites.

First, by the *Sh4 idempotency contract* clause (iii) — which fires uniformly across both `K ≁ R` (Case B's regime) and `K ~ R` (Case D's regime), since the contract's structural trigger is `shape(K).idem = ⊤` and per-class constancy of `shape(·)` gives `shape(K) = shape(R) = (*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)` under `K ~ R`, so the contract's `idem = ⊤` condition continues to hold — the candidate-set check `C(F_{τ_new}, G_{τ_new}, Σ) = ∅` was confirmed against the full `A_R^Σ` before the step fired (the two-step procedure (i.a) + (i.b) runs over `A_R^Σ` before the step fires, not over any post-step subset). The dependence here is on the contract itself, not on Case B's conclusion as a theorem: Case B's argument *also* leans on the contract clause (iii), but Case D needs the contract's same firing independently. So τ_new is slot-pair-distinct from every prior τ ∈ A_R^Σ, including the τ ∈ leaving (the unique `τ_old` when `|leaving| = 1`) that will subsequently exit via nullification. *Symmetry of Sh4's predicate.* Sh4's body `P(τ, τ') ≡ (slot_addrs(F_τ), slot_addrs(G_τ)) = (slot_addrs(F_{τ'}), slot_addrs(G_{τ'})) ⟹ addr(τ) = addr(τ')` is symmetric in its two operands: both the hypothesis (slot-pair equality of two endset-pair values) and the conclusion (address equality) are symmetric relations on their operands by symmetry of `=`, so `P(τ, τ_new) ⟺ P(τ_new, τ)` for every τ ∈ A_R^Σ. The off-diagonal therefore reduces to a single check across the direction; in what follows the single check "τ_new is slot-pair-distinct from every τ ∈ A_R^Σ" suffices for both ordered-pair directions `(τ_new, τ)` and `(τ, τ_new)` uniformly. Pairwise distinctness on `A_R^Σ ∪ {τ_new}` is established by the IH (which gives pairwise distinctness on A_R^Σ) together with τ_new's slot-pair distinctness from every member of A_R^Σ (the IH is the *off-diagonal* content of Sh4's universal — see the *Universal scope* clarification above — and the symmetric `(τ, τ_new)` direction closes from the same fact by symmetry of Sh4's predicate, just established; τ_new's diagonal `(τ_new, τ_new)` case is trivially satisfied by reflexivity of `addr(τ_new) = addr(τ_new)`). Second, `A_R^{Σ'} = (A_R^Σ ∪ {τ_new}) \ leaving` is a subset of the pairwise-distinct set `A_R^Σ ∪ {τ_new}`; Sh4 transfers from superset to subset by the following expansion of the universal quantifier. *Subset-closure derivation.* Let `P(τ, τ')` denote Sh4's body `(slot_addrs(F_τ), slot_addrs(G_τ)) = (slot_addrs(F_{τ'}), slot_addrs(G_{τ'})) ⟹ addr(τ) = addr(τ')`. Pairwise distinctness on a set `S ⊆ A_R^Σ ∪ {τ_new}` is `(A τ, τ' ∈ S :: P(τ, τ'))`. The hypothesis at this step is the just-established pairwise distinctness on `S := A_R^Σ ∪ {τ_new}`. For any subset `S' ⊆ S`, the Cartesian product `S'^2 = S' × S' ⊆ S × S = S^2`: every pair `(τ, τ') ∈ S'^2` is itself a pair in `S^2`, with `P` evaluated on the *same* operands (the universal's body refers only to `τ, τ'` and the per-operand projections `slot_addrs(F_·)`, `slot_addrs(G_·)`, `addr(·)`; none of these depend on which superset the pair is drawn from). Hence `(A τ, τ' ∈ S :: P(τ, τ')) ⟹ (A τ, τ' ∈ S' :: P(τ, τ'))` — every quantification site over `S'` is among the quantification sites over `S`, with the same predicate. Instantiating the implication at `S = A_R^Σ ∪ {τ_new}` and `S' = A_R^{Σ'} = (A_R^Σ ∪ {τ_new}) \ leaving ⊆ S` discharges Sh4 on `A_R^{Σ'}` from the established Sh4 on the larger pairwise-distinct set.

The induction closes. ∎

*Status.* Sh4 is a *theorem under the Sh4 idempotency contract*, not a substrate-enforced axiom. The substrate as defined by ASN-0086 does not enforce Sh4 directly: R0 (TupleAddressFreshness) explicitly permits two emissions with identical `(F, G)` to produce two distinct tuples; R1 (AddressInjectivity) keeps them distinguishable. Without the *Sh4 idempotency contract*, the substrate would admit such emissions and Sh4 would fail. The framework's Sh4 conclusion depends on the layer's protocol fidelity at this specific contract.

*Failure modes under contract violation.* If the layer breaks any of clauses (i)–(iii) at any emission site — forgetting to observe, racing with concurrent emitters or with retractors, or admitting an emission whose `C(F, G, Σ) ≠ ∅` — Sh4 may fail at the resulting state. Templates that consume Sh4 directly become undefined or multi-valued where Sh4 would have collapsed slot-pair duplicates to a single representative; per-template specifications state explicitly when a template's totality depends on Sh4. Note that the set-valued accessors `from_K`, `to_K`, `from_addrs_K`, `to_addrs_K` (DirectedPair walkthrough) consume Sh4 only weakly — they degrade from sets of slot-pair-distinct tuples to multisets when Sh4 fails — and remain well-defined as multisets.

The singleton-returning `K_target_of` accessor (DirectedPair walkthrough) requires a strictly stronger commitment, FunctionalDependencyDiscipline, presented separately: Sh4 alone is insufficient because Sh4 enforces slot-pair distinctness, not from-slot uniqueness.

*Why a layer-level contract rather than a substrate-level axiom.* Lifting Sh4 into Sh-conf as an `Emit_K`-precondition (e.g., "reject `Emit_K` when `shape(K).idem = ⊤ ∧ C(F, G, Σ) ≠ ∅`") is technically feasible but conflicts with the substrate's design intent. Type semantics in ASN-0086 are by address identity, not by content-equivalence policy; substrate-level idempotency enforcement would require the substrate to commit to a duplicate-detection semantics (set vs. multiset, exact vs. coverage-equivalent) and would couple the substrate to a particular Observe-time-of-emit transactional discipline. Keeping Sh4 at the layer separates the substrate's address-permanence guarantee from the layer's idempotency policy: layers may adopt set-semantics idempotency for some K and bag-semantics for others, with the substrate uniformly admitting all emissions and the framework's templates consuming whichever discipline the layer commits to.

*Justification of the policy.* Some predicates need yes/no semantics on tuple existence: "is `d` classified as a claim?" should not be answered by counting `(∅, {d})` tuples. `A_K` is always a set of distinct-address tuples (R1, AddressInjectivity, ASN-0086); what differs across `idem` is whether `(slot_addrs(F_τ), slot_addrs(G_τ))`-pair duplicates can persist in `A_K`. Under Sh4 (idem = ⊤), the layer contract collapses such duplicates at Emit time, so `A_K` carries at most one active tuple per slot-pair: existence-vs-count distinctions are well-defined as Boolean tests. Under idem = ⊥, multiple distinct-address tuples may share a slot-pair (e.g., two Comment tuples with identical commenter/target reflecting two distinct events); predicates that count or enumerate the slot-pair multiplicity are meaningful precisely because the active subset retains those distinctions.

*Consequences.*

(a) *Existence-vs-count distinction.* Idempotent relations support `exists_K(F, G) : Bool` predicates with stable yes/no answers under the contract. Non-idempotent relations support `count_K(...)` predicates whose value reflects the number of distinct emission events.

(b) *Re-emit-vs-fail behavior is registry-driven.* Library helpers like `emit_attribute` consult `shape(K).idem` to decide whether to short-circuit on existing match or always allocate a fresh address. The decision is mechanical from the shape, not from inspection of K's name.

(c) *Idempotency is a property of A_K, not L_K.* By R3, `L_K` always retains every emission ever made — including any duplicates that may exist if the contract was ever violated. The contract restricts what reaches `A_K`. Under correct contract enforcement, once a duplicate would be emitted, the layer suppresses it. The audit slice `L_K` retains historical state regardless: retraction-then-reemit cycles can leave multiple coverage-identical tuples in `L_K` with at most one active.


## Template Catalog (Sh5)

**Sh5 — TemplateCatalog.** For each canonical shape `Sh_canon`, the shape framework specifies a hand-curated *template family* of predicate forms applicable to every `K ∈ T_cat` with `shape(K) = Sh_canon`. Each template is parameterized by K's name (and, where noted, by layer-supplied auxiliary accessors); instantiation substitutes the name into the template body and yields a per-K predicate or accessor. The families are written by hand against the canonical shape catalog (Sh5 is META), not assembled into a single function over an explicit codomain.

*Status.* Sh5 is a META commitment about how this framework constructs and maintains its canonical shape catalog, not a mechanical-derivation theorem about all possible catalogs. The status splits into two distinct components:

(a) *META observation.* The template families exhibited in the walkthroughs below are written by hand against the canonical shape catalog. There is no procedure mapping an arbitrary shape to its template family; new shapes acquire templates by analogy with existing entries and by hand-design. *Status of per-shape uniformity (downgraded to aspiration in this draft).* The earlier draft phrased per-shape uniformity at the body-shape level ("rows with identical `(c_F, c_G, t_F, t_G, idem)` tuples agree on base templates") as a *commitment* enforced by hand-review. The present draft explicitly downgrades this from a commitment to an *aspiration*: the framework provides no mechanical procedure guaranteeing that two independent drafts registering the same shape will arrive at identical base-template bodies, and no procedural recipe (documented difference table, body-shape derivation procedure from shape components, or auditor-side review checklist) is committed in the current draft. The mechanical falsifiability claim of Sh5(b) below addresses the citation-side discipline (every cited *data symbol* falls within categories (i)–(iv) below) and does *not* extend to body-shape-level convergence — the latter is a hand-curation goal of this catalog, not a discipline the framework enforces. Concretely: a future draft proposing a divergent template body at an existing shape would not be rejected by any mechanical gate the framework supplies; the catalog author retains body-shape design freedom at every new row, and the catalog's current shape-mate convergences (DirectedPair/Resolution sharing five base templates modulo codomain shift; the two `(0, 1)` Classifier rows sharing `is_K`) are *exhibited* rather than *guaranteed*. Where downstream framework text previously referenced per-shape uniformity as a load-bearing commitment (Sh5(b)'s catalog-row-structure paragraph; the per-K opt-in registry partition; the Resolution-base-templates "mechanically generated" reading), the underlying property continues to hold *in this catalog* by hand-curation but is no longer a framework commitment that would constrain a hypothetical future catalog extension. The Sh5(b) discipline remains a falsifiable per-row check on data-symbol citations; convergence at the body-shape level is offered as a design aspiration the current catalog meets, with no enforcement mechanism beyond the catalog author's intent. Sharpening the aspiration to a procedural recipe (e.g., a documented body-shape derivation procedure keyed off shape components) is recorded as an open work item; the present draft does not undertake it.

(b) *META discipline.* This framework's catalog adheres to the rule that every catalog row's templates depend only on the following six input categories of symbols (the first four are *data symbols* gated by the literal name-citation rule; categories (v) and (vi) are non-data carve-outs that are *unrestricted* at every template body but are still enumerated explicitly so that the discipline's category set is exhaustive *for the current catalog* — every symbol cited by the current catalog's templates falls into exactly one of the six categories, with no implicit residual at the current catalog. The universal "every symbol" is *not* a closed-world theorem over arbitrary future templates; it is a verifiable property of the current catalog established by the per-row audit table below, and any future template-body symbol introduction requires the per-row minimal review checklist (steps 0–3 below) to classify each newly-introduced symbol against the six categories. A future symbol falling outside categories (i)–(vi) would *not* automatically be admitted into category (vi) or (v) by default; the checklist would route it to a rejection at step 2 (per-symbol classification), at which point the catalog author would have to either add a scaffolding clause to admit it under (iii), register an opt-in discipline export under (iv), extend the (vi) base-machinery enumeration by catalog amendment, or withdraw the candidate. The closed-world status thus reads at the *current catalog* and at *every catalog reached via the per-row checklist*; the discipline is META in that it commits the framework to a procedural gate at extension time rather than to a universal mathematical theorem over arbitrary template bodies): (i) the shape components (cardinality `c_F`, `c_G`; target-domain typing `t_F`, `t_G`; idempotency flag `idem`) *together with the slot accessors uniquely derived from them* — `from₁` (at `c_F = 1`, via SlotAccessorTotality), `to₁` (at `c_G = 1`), the partial accessors `from₁⁻` (at `c_F ∈ {1, 0|1}`) and `to₁⁻` (at `c_G ∈ {1, 0|1}`, per the *Codomain convention for partial templates*), and the set-valued slot accessors `from_K^Σ` and `to_K^Σ` (PointSlotAccessors and SetSlotAccessors Definitions). Each derived accessor is a syntactic projection off the shape registry's cardinality and target-domain components — its name, signature, and partiality reading are fixed by the shape components alone, with no additional registration required at the row. (ii) K's name; (iii) named scaffolding clauses surfaced in the *Scope and Substrate Scaffolding* list; (iv) explicitly named per-K disciplines and per-call type-index parameters registered in the row's opt-in or parametric columns; (v) *meta-operators* — logical and set-theoretic primitives, enumerated below; and (vi) *framework-internal base-machinery accessors* — substrate exports available at every row without per-row registration, enumerated below. The criterion for the *data-symbol* categories (i)–(iv) is *literal name-citation*: a data-symbol reference in a template body must either be one of the shape-component slots or one of the shape-component-derived slot accessors enumerated under (i) above, K itself, a scaffolding clause name (e.g., `chain_index`, `home(·)`, `s_L`), an accessor exported by a registered per-K discipline (e.g., `emission_order` under SingleHomeCoverageDiscipline), or a parametric type-index argument; any data symbol falling outside categories (i)–(iv) violates the discipline and the catalog rejects the addition. *Category (v) — meta-operators.* Logical and set-theoretic primitives — set comprehensions `{… : …}`, finite-set operations `∪`, `∩`, `\\`, `℘_fin(·)`, `∈`, `⊆`, `=`, quantifiers `(A …)`, `(E …)`, extremal operators (`argmax`, `argmin`, `max`, `min`) over finite ℕ-indexed sets, the Boolean connectives `∧`, `∨`, `¬`, `⟹`, `⟺`, conditional expressions, the partiality token `⊥`, and standard arithmetic on ℕ — are not data symbols and are unrestricted at every template body. Their use does not require registration. The discipline's literal name-citation rule applies only to data-symbol references (substrate accessors, scaffolding accessors, per-K-registered accessors, type-index parameters); it never restricts the logical and set-theoretic vocabulary used to *combine* those references. *Category (vi) — framework-internal base-machinery accessors.* The accessors `A_K^Σ` (the active-subset projector parameterized by K at state Σ), `addr(τ)` (R1 export from ASN-0086, returning a tuple's address), `slot_addrs(·)` (defined in *The Address-Set Projection* above, well-defined on canonical-slot endsets by Sh0/Sh1), and `δ(1, #·)` (OrdinalDisplacement, ASN-0034, the unit-depth displacement) are *substrate exports available at every catalog row without per-row registration*; their use in template bodies is unrestricted by the literal name-citation rule of (i)–(iv). The enumeration is exhaustive — any future template-body symbol claimed to qualify under (vi) must be added to this enumerated list by a catalog amendment, not by implicit admission; a symbol absent from both (i)–(v) and this (vi) enumeration falls outside the discipline and the catalog rejects its use. *Signature derivation rule (signatures only, not bodies).* Template *signatures* (input domain and codomain symbols) derive mechanically from shape components: a template's input domain and codomain symbols are read from the shape's `t_F` and `t_G` values respectively. Two registered K's at the same shape therefore inherit identical *signatures* for every base template by this rule; opt-in disciplines may refine codomains (e.g., FDD's `K_target_of` lands in `A_doc^Σ ∪ {⊥}` by adding partiality), but the underlying target-domain symbol still derives from `t_G` via category (i). The rule does *not* extend to template *bodies* — body-shape convergence at shape-mate rows is an aspiration of hand-curation, not a mechanical derivation. Template bodies *as exhibited in the current catalog* converge at shape-mate rows by hand-curation (see *Status of per-shape uniformity (downgraded to aspiration in this draft)* in Sh5(a)); the framework supplies no mechanical gate that would force a future catalog extension to register identical bodies at the same shape, so body-shape convergence at shape-mates is a property of the present catalog rather than a discipline the framework enforces. The opt-in column registers per-K disciplines (their names, their preservation contracts, and the accessor symbols they export); the parametric column registers per-call type-index arguments. Scaffolding clauses are catalog-row-independent (every row may cite them by name without per-row registration) because they are uniform substrate-layer commitments listed once in *Scope and Substrate Scaffolding*. This is a META commitment about this catalog's construction, not a theorem provable from R0…R7a plus Sh0–Sh4: a future draft proposing to add a template whose well-formedness depends on K-specific data outside (i)–(iv) (and not within (v) or (vi)) would violate the discipline, and the framework would reject the addition. The discipline is what makes Sh5's per-shape organization falsifiable: a catalog row with diverging base templates from a shape-mate row, or a template body referencing a *data* symbol outside (i)–(iv) that does not qualify under (v) or (vi), would visibly violate the discipline.

*Catalog extension is a manual review process — the acknowledged cost of Sh5's META status.* The discipline above is enforced by hand-review against each proposed catalog addition, not by a mechanical derivation procedure. There is no algorithm that takes a candidate row and certifies its templates compliant: every new row requires a human auditor to check each template body against the six input categories (i)–(vi), classifying each cited symbol and verifying that every symbol falls into one of the six (data symbols within (i)–(iv); non-data symbols within (v) or (vi)). The catalog-wide citation audit table below is the worked-out form of this review for the current catalog's eleven audit-table rows (with the layer composite `K_is_fresh` shown in a separate callout following the table to illustrate a *failed* check, since `mtime` falls outside all six categories); future extensions add new rows to that table, with each row's symbol-by-symbol classification produced by the same hand-curation process. *Mechanical falsifiability without mechanical derivation.* The discipline supports *mechanical falsification* even though it does not support mechanical *derivation*: given a candidate template body, the literal name-citation check against the six categories is a finite, decidable per-symbol classification — once an auditor (or a downstream tool) is willing to walk each symbol against the categorization rule, a violation is detectable in finite time. What is *not* mechanical is template *production*: the auditor cannot start from an arbitrary shape and have the discipline produce a template body. Sh5(a)'s "by analogy with existing entries and by hand-design" is the framing for what the auditor does instead. This split — mechanical falsification, manual derivation — is the explicit cost of Sh5's META status; the framework accepts this cost in exchange for the meta-flexibility of admitting shape-specific design choices that a purely procedural derivation rule could not capture (e.g., Retraction's deliberate set-equality F-side argument vs the alternative membership reading, documented in the Retraction walkthrough). Future drafts may sharpen the discipline into a stricter procedural recipe; the current draft does not.

*Worked check at `latest_K_for_addr`.* The Coverage walkthrough's `latest_K_for_addr` template body `argmax_{τ ∈ S_d} emission_order(τ)` references symbols that the discipline above sorts into three groups. *Data-symbol references* (subject to literal name-citation under categories (i)–(iv)): the inline `S_d = {τ ∈ A_K^Σ : to₁(τ) = d}` cites the shape-component-derived accessor `to₁` (category (i)); `emission_order(τ)`, which under SingleHomeCoverageDiscipline expands to `chain_index(addr(τ), d_K)`, cites `chain_index` (category (iii), named in the *Link sub-allocator chain-index function* scaffolding clause) and `d_K` (category (iv), registered by SingleHomeCoverageDiscipline as a per-K commitment); `K` (category (ii), via `A_K^Σ` indexing). All data-symbol references are within the four input categories. *Category (vi) — base-machinery references* (unrestricted under the enumerated framework-internal base-machinery accessors): `A_K^Σ` (the active-subset projector parameterized by K at state Σ) and `addr(τ)` (R1 export from ASN-0086). Both are in the (vi) enumeration. *Category (v) — meta-operator references* (unrestricted under the meta-operator carve-out): `argmax`, the set-comprehension braces `{… : …}`, and the membership relation `∈`. These are logical and set-theoretic primitives. Every symbol in the template body falls into one of the six categories; the template clears the discipline.

*Worked check at Retraction's `pair_K(F̂, b)`.* Retraction's `pair_K(F̂, b) ≡ (E τ ∈ A_K^Σ :: slot_addrs(F_τ) = F̂ ∧ to₁(τ) = b)` exhibits a non-standard body shape compared to DirectedPair's `pair_K` — the F-side uses `slot_addrs(F_τ)` rather than `from₁(τ)`, and the F-side argument is an address-set pattern `F̂` matched by set equality rather than an address matched by equality. We walk the discipline's category sorting at this body. *Data-symbol references* (categories (i)–(iv)): `to₁` (category (i): shape-component-derived slot accessor at `c_G = 1`, total by SlotAccessorTotality). `K` (category (ii): K's name, cited via the `A_K^Σ` indexing). No scaffolding-clause citation (category (iii)) and no opt-in-discipline accessor or parametric type-index (category (iv)) appears in the body — the body's structure is fully determined by shape components and K's name. The Sh4 idempotency contract fires at this row by `shape(R).idem = ⊤`, but the contract's clauses are gating mechanisms at the call site, not data symbols cited in the template body. *Category (vi) — base-machinery references* (unrestricted): `slot_addrs(F_τ)` (in the (vi) enumeration as a framework-internal base-machinery accessor, well-defined on canonical-slot endsets by Sh0/Sh1), `A_K^Σ` (in the (vi) enumeration). The use of `slot_addrs(F_τ)` rather than `from₁(τ)` is *forced* by the shape's `c_F = *`: SlotAccessorTotality at `c_F = 1` is what licenses `from₁` as a point accessor, but `c_F = *` admits `|slot_addrs(F_τ)|` of any cardinality, so `from₁` is undefined at this shape. The set-equality `slot_addrs(F_τ) = F̂` is the operationally meaningful Boolean test at unrestricted F-cardinality (per the *Note on `pair_K`'s set-equality F-side argument* design choice clause). *Category (v) — meta-operator references* (unrestricted): the existential `(E … :: …)`, the Boolean conjunction `∧`, the membership relation `∈`, and the set equality `=`. All standard meta-operators. *Decision.* Every data symbol cited (`to₁`, `K`) is within categories (i)–(iv); every non-data symbol cited is within category (v) or (vi). The template clears the discipline at Retraction's row. The non-standard set-equality on F is a hand-curation choice (per the same design-choice clause), not a violation of Sh5(b) — Sh5(b) restricts which data symbols may be cited, not which Boolean compositions of those symbols may form a template body.

*Worked check at Provenance's `to_K(b)`.* Provenance's `to_K(b) ≡ {τ ∈ A_K^Σ : to₁⁻(τ) = b}` exhibits the partial-G handling forced by `c_G = 0|1` — the body cites `to₁⁻` (returning `⊥` on tuples with empty G-slot) rather than `to₁` (which is undefined at this shape). We walk the discipline's category sorting at this body. *Data-symbol references* (categories (i)–(iv)): `to₁⁻` (category (i): shape-component-derived slot accessor at `c_G = 0|1`, per the PointSlotAccessors Definition — total on `L_K^Σ` but partial in the sense that its codomain is `t_G^Σ ∪ {⊥}` rather than `t_G^Σ`, per the *Codomain convention for partial templates*). `K` (category (ii): K's name, via `A_K^Σ` indexing). No scaffolding-clause citation (category (iii)) and no opt-in-discipline accessor or parametric type-index (category (iv)) appears in the body — the partial accessor `to₁⁻` is shape-component-derived at `c_G = 0|1`, not registered as an opt-in. The Sh4 contract fires at this row by `shape(K).idem = ⊤`, gating call sites but not citing data symbols in the body. *Category (vi) — base-machinery references* (unrestricted): `A_K^Σ` (in the (vi) enumeration). *Category (v) — meta-operator references* (unrestricted): the set-comprehension braces `{… : …}`, the membership relation `∈`, and equality `=`. The implicit `⊥`-filtering — "tuples with `to₁⁻(τ) = ⊥` are excluded because `⊥ ≠ b` for any `b ∈ A^Σ`", recorded as a side-note at the Provenance catalog row — is itself a meta-operator step (the inequality `⊥ ≠ b` is the standard partiality-token comparison), not a data-symbol citation. *Decision.* Every data symbol cited (`to₁⁻`, `K`) is within categories (i)–(iv); the citation of the partial-codomain accessor `to₁⁻` (rather than the total `to₁`) is *forced* by the shape's `c_G = 0|1` rather than chosen by hand. Every non-data symbol cited is within category (v) or (vi). The template clears the discipline at Provenance's row. The partial-G handling — explicit `⊥`-filtering on G-side templates — is a structural consequence of the shape's `c_G = 0|1`, not an extra-categorical citation, and the discipline accommodates it through the category-(i) inclusion of `to₁⁻` as a shape-component-derived accessor at that cardinality.

*Catalog-wide citation audit.* Beyond the `latest_K_for_addr` worked check, every other *currently-registered* catalog row's templates are verified against the same discipline. *Common category-(vi) base-machinery accessors* used uniformly across rows — `A_K^Σ`, `addr(τ)`, `slot_addrs(·)`, `δ(1, #·)` — are the enumerated framework-internal base-machinery exports of category (vi); their use is unrestricted by the literal name-citation rule of (i)–(iv). *Common category-(v) meta-operators* — the Boolean connectives, set-comprehensions, finite-set operations, quantifiers, and the partiality token `⊥` — are the meta-operator exports of category (v), also unrestricted. The table below cites the per-row template data symbols against the four data-symbol categories (i)–(iv); category-(v) meta-operators and category-(vi) base-machinery accessors are uniform across rows and omitted from the per-row cells. The audit's universal-over-rows quantifier ranges over the current catalog's eleven rows enumerated below; future extensions add to the table through the per-row checklist (steps 0–3 below), so the universal is verified *per-catalog* rather than once-and-for-all over arbitrary future rows — matching the META status of Sh5(b)'s "every symbol... no implicit residual" claim above.

*The audit table enumerates the accepted rows.* Each row's cited data symbols are classifiable under one of categories (i)–(iv) (with category-(v) meta-operators and category-(vi) base-machinery accessors omitted from per-row cells per the audit table preamble), so the row's templates clear the citation check and the catalog admits the row. To make Sh5(b)'s META falsifiability claim visibly symmetric, a separate callout following the table walks through a rejected candidate (`K_is_fresh`, citing `mtime` outside all six categories); the callout is not part of the catalog and not counted among the accepted rows. The rejected candidate is relocated to the *Layer composite: `K_is_fresh`* subsection below, where `mtime` is explicitly registered as a layer-supplied accessor and the composite re-enters scope under that registration.

*Status of the audit table — snapshot at the current catalog, with a minimal review checklist committed for extensions.* The audit table below is a *snapshot* of the catalog's eleven currently-accepted rows together with one currently-rejected candidate. The framework commits the following *minimal review checklist* — the only procedural commitment for extending the table — that a catalog author runs against each proposed new row before adding it:

0. *Shape-tuple admissibility check against R.* Before classifying any template-body symbol, compare the proposed row's registered shape tuple `(c_F, c_G, t_F, t_G, idem)` to Retraction's shape `(*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)` componentwise. *Rejection criterion.* If the proposed shape tuple is *componentwise equal* to Retraction's `(*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)`, the proposed row's representative endset `K_rep` must additionally satisfy `K_rep ~ R` (coverage-equivalence to R, decidable per the *Decidable membership* paragraph of the TypedRelationCatalog Definition).
