The empty-baseline is *required* (not merely sufficient) for FDD's preservation per the *Scope of the per-tuple-conformance relaxation* paragraph in Initial-State Baseline.

Register `K = attribute` with shape `(1, 1, A_doc, A_doc, ⊤)` (DirectedPair) and additionally register FunctionalDependencyDiscipline at K. Pre-allocate `d_parent, d_sidecar1, d_sidecar2 ∈ A_doc^{Σ_0}` and a fresh home `home_attr ∈ dom(Σ_0.M)` for `attribute` emissions (distinct from any home symbol introduced in prior walkthroughs; we use a relation-specific name to keep this walkthrough self-contained).

**Emission FDD1.** `Emit_K(Σ_0, home_attr, F_{FDD1}, G_{FDD1})` with `F_{FDD1} = {(d_parent, δ(1, #d_parent))}` (parent in from-slot) and `G_{FDD1} = {(d_sidecar1, δ(1, #d_sidecar1))}` (sidecar in to-slot).

*FDD contract clause (i):* Compute `C_fd(F_{FDD1}, Σ_0)` via the two-step procedure. (i.a) `Observe_K(slot_addrs(F_{FDD1}), ∅, oper) = Observe_K({d_parent}, ∅, oper)` returns ∅ (no prior K-tuples at the initial state). (i.b) Post-filter is vacuous on the empty result. `C_fd(F_{FDD1}, Σ_0) = ∅`.

*FDD contract clause (iii):* The candidate set is empty, so the layer issues `Emit_K(Σ_0, home_attr, F_{FDD1}, G_{FDD1})`. Sh-conf admits (canonical-slot, cardinality 1/1, `{d_parent} ⊆ A_doc^{Σ_0}`, `{d_sidecar1} ⊆ A_doc^{Σ_0}`). Result Σ_1 with τ_FDD1 at address `a_FDD1`.

*Singleton accessor evaluation at Σ_1.* `from_K(d_parent) = {τ_FDD1}` (unique element). `K_target_of(d_parent) = to₁(τ_FDD1) = d_sidecar1` (the well-defined singleton-returning accessor).

**Emission FDD2 (rejected by FDD contract clause (ii)).** Attempt `Emit_K(Σ_1, home_attr, F_{FDD2}, G_{FDD2})` with `F_{FDD2} = F_{FDD1}` (same parent) and `G_{FDD2} = {(d_sidecar2, δ(1, #d_sidecar2))}` (different sidecar — `d_sidecar2 ≠ d_sidecar1`). Note `G_{FDD2} ≠ G_{FDD1}`, so under Sh4 alone this emission would be *admitted* (slot-pair-distinct from τ_FDD1: Sh4's `C(F, G, Σ)` requires both slots to match).

*FDD contract clause (i):* Compute `C_fd(F_{FDD2}, Σ_1)`. (i.a) `Observe_K({d_parent}, ∅, oper)` returns `{τ_FDD1}` (τ_FDD1's F-coverage `{t : d_parent ≼ t}` contains d_parent). (i.b) Post-filter retains τ_FDD1 because `|slot_addrs(F_{τ_FDD1})| = |{d_parent}| = 1 = |slot_addrs(F_{FDD2})|`, forcing `slot_addrs(F_{τ_FDD1}) = {d_parent} = slot_addrs(F_{FDD2})` under the AllocatedAddressAntichain over-approximation. So `C_fd(F_{FDD2}, Σ_1) = {τ_FDD1} ≠ ∅`.

*FDD contract clause (ii):* The candidate set is non-empty, so the emission is *suppressed*. The layer returns `⊥` (per Sh-conf's extended return type). No `↦`-step occurs. State remains Σ_1; `A_K^{Σ_1} = {τ_FDD1}` unchanged.

*Singleton accessor evaluation post-suppression.* `from_K(d_parent) = {τ_FDD1}` (still unique). `K_target_of(d_parent) = d_sidecar1` (unchanged). FDD preserves single-valuedness of the singleton accessor across the duplicate-from-slot emission attempt.

**Contrast with Sh4 alone (FDD not registered).** Were the same K registered with bare DirectedPair shape (no FDD), Emission FDD2 would be admitted: Sh4's `C(F, G, Σ_1) = {τ ∈ A_K^{Σ_1} : slot_addrs(F_τ) = {d_parent} ∧ slot_addrs(G_τ) = {d_sidecar2}} = ∅` (τ_FDD1 fails the G-slot match), so suppression does not fire. The emission proceeds, producing `A_K^{Σ_2} = {τ_FDD1, τ_FDD2}` with `from_K(d_parent) = {τ_FDD1, τ_FDD2}` — a cardinality-2 set, on which a singleton-returning `K_target_of(d_parent)` would be ill-defined (it would have to choose between `d_sidecar1` and `d_sidecar2`). This is exactly the failure mode FDD prevents by restricting `C_fd` to the from-slot match.

### Sh4 emission suppression (Tuple-Classifier, idem = ⊤)

*Registered catalog for this walkthrough.* `T_cat = {endorsed_v2, R}` (closure under `~` implicit). Distinctive: `endorsed_v2` is the Tuple-Classifier-shape relation (a distinct K from the Tuple-Classifier walkthrough's `endorsed`, named `_v2` to keep its registration scope-disjoint). Baseline commitments per the *Convention for per-walkthrough registered-catalog declarations* (Initial-State Baseline). The walkthrough's working state `Σ` is reached from `Σ_init` by K.σ/K.α/K.λ steps allocating `home_endorsed_v2` and `τ_target` (the latter is a prior class-(iii) emission at some other registered K outside `T_cat`'s scope here; its specific K is not relevant to the Sh4 demonstration).

To exhibit Sh4 suppression in action (independent of FDD), register `K = endorsed_v2` with shape `(0, 1, -, A_rel, ⊤)` (Tuple-Classifier, `idem = ⊤`). Working from a state Σ where `τ_target ∈ A_rel^Σ` is a target tuple-address and `home_endorsed_v2 ∈ dom(Σ.M)` is a fresh home document for `endorsed_v2` emissions (named per-relation to keep this walkthrough's scope disjoint from prior walkthroughs that bound `home_K`).

**Emission ENDX1.** `Emit_K(Σ, home_endorsed_v2, ∅, {(τ_target, δ(1, #τ_target))})`.

*Sh4 contract clause (i):* Compute `C(F, G, Σ)` via the two-step procedure. (i.a) `Observe_K(∅, {τ_target}, oper)` — the `slot_addrs(F) = ∅` pattern is vacuously contained in any F-coverage; the `{τ_target}` G-pattern selects tuples whose G-coverage contains τ_target. Result: ∅ (no prior K-tuples). (i.b) Post-filter vacuous. `C = ∅`.

*Sh4 contract clause (iii):* `C = ∅`, so emission proceeds. Sh-conf admits. Result Σ' with new tuple σ_1 at fresh address a_σ1.

*Active subset after ENDX1.* `A_K^{Σ'} = {σ_1}`. Template: `is_K(τ_target) = true`.

**Emission ENDX2 (suppressed by Sh4 contract clause (ii)).** From Σ', attempt the *same* emission again: `Emit_K(Σ', home_endorsed_v2, ∅, {(τ_target, δ(1, #τ_target))})` — identical slot-addresses to ENDX1.

*Sh4 contract clause (i):* Compute `C(F, G, Σ')`. (i.a) `Observe_K(∅, {τ_target}, oper)` returns `{σ_1}` (σ_1's G-coverage `{t : τ_target ≼ t}` contains τ_target). (i.b) Post-filter: σ_1 has `slot_addrs(F_{σ_1}) = ∅` and `slot_addrs(G_{σ_1}) = {τ_target}`, exactly matching the patterns. So `C = {σ_1} ≠ ∅`.

*Sh4 contract clause (ii):* `C ≠ ∅`, so the emission is *suppressed*. The layer returns `⊥`. No `↦`-step. State remains Σ'. `A_K^{Σ'} = {σ_1}` unchanged — exactly *one* active tuple, not two.

*Verification of Sh4.* `|A_K^{Σ'}| = 1`. The pairwise-distinctness condition over `A_K^{Σ'}` is vacuous off-diagonal (no two distinct active tuples to consider), and the diagonal `(σ_1, σ_1)` is trivially satisfied by reflexivity. Sh4 holds. Crucially, the framework preserved Sh4 by suppressing the duplicate emission attempt; under R0 (TupleAddressFreshness, ASN-0086) alone, the substrate would have admitted ENDX2 and produced `A_K = {σ_1, σ_2}` with both targeting τ_target — violating Sh4 because the slot-address pair `(∅, {τ_target})` would witness two distinct active tuples. The *Sh4 idempotency contract* is what makes Sh4 hold.

*Contrast with `idem = ⊥`.* Were `idem = ⊥` registered for the same shape (a hypothetical NonIdempotentTupleClassifier), ENDX2 would not be suppressed: the contract clauses (i)–(iii) are conditioned on `shape(K).idem = ⊤`. The second emission would land at a fresh address, and `A_K` would carry two distinct active tuples sharing the same slot-address pair — admissible under the non-idempotent reading where each emission records a distinct event.


## Consequences

(a) *Adding a new relation generates predicates for free.* A new K with `shape(K) = DirectedPair` immediately yields `pair_K`, `from_K`, `to_K`, `from_addrs_K`, and `to_addrs_K` — no per-relation predicate code is required. The cost of a new relation is one entry in the shape registry. Layers consuming an Attribute-style or Citation-style reading further define aliases (`has_K`, `K_sidecars_of`, `cites_K`, `K_incoming`) over the canonical names; the singleton-returning `K_target_of` (and its `K_sidecar_of` alias) becomes available when the layer additionally registers FunctionalDependencyDiscipline for that K.

(b) *Composite predicates extend the catalog through the same compositional primitives.* A composite predicate combines atomic templates through Boolean operators and quantification over `T_cat`. The framework does not establish a closure theorem about these primitives — whether composition can express predicates strictly beyond what the catalog's atomic templates yield is a property of the composition language adopted, not a structural guarantee of Sh5. The design observation we record is weaker: the canonical-shape catalog is the registry's *atomic* vocabulary, and adding a structurally new pattern (e.g., a slot-cardinality combination not yet present) is handled by extending the catalog with a new canonical shape, not by composing existing relations. Layer composites (e.g., `K_is_fresh`) extend the predicate language further by bringing in external accessors like `mtime`; these compose atop the framework but are not part of it.

(c) *Shape misregistration is a structural error.* Registering a relation with the wrong shape produces predicates with wrong signatures or wrong semantics — the substrate cannot self-correct this. By Sh-conf, attempts to emit non-conformant tuples are rejected, but the rejection assumes the registered shape is the *correct* shape; if the registry is wrong, the substrate enforces the wrong constraint. Shape registration is part of the relation's contract.

(d) *The predicate language is bounded by the shape catalog.* "What the substrate can ask" is determined by the templates the shapes generate. Questions about content quality ("is this proof complete?", "is this description good?") are not expressible because no canonical shape's template generates them. Those are agent-time questions, not substrate questions.


## Properties Introduced

| Label | Type | Statement | Status |
|-------|------|-----------|--------|
| cov | DEF | Coverage projection `L_K → ℘(T) × ℘(T)` | introduced |
| cov_allocated | DEF | Allocated-coverage projection `(F, Σ) → coverage(F) ∩ A^Σ`; finite per Σ, monotone along `⊑̂` | introduced |
| canonical-slot form | DEF | Endset form `{(x, δ(1, #x)) : x ∈ X_F}` with extractable slot-address set | introduced |
| slot_addrs | DEF | Extraction `F ↦ X_F` for canonical-form F | introduced |
| AllocatedAddressAntichain | LEMMA | For every reachable Σ at a substrate-conforming layer honoring the `subspace_I(·) = E(·).1` identification, and every `x ∈ A^Σ`, `cov_allocated({(x, δ(1, #x))}, Σ) = {x}`; the layer-commitment qualifier is surfaced at the lemma statement (rather than buried in the proof preamble), since Case 3's subspace-contradiction step consumes the identification directly. The element-level character of `A^Σ` is derived (L1 + L1b for the link side, scaffolding for the content side, both resting on the same layer-commitment), so the bare `x ∈ A^Σ` hypothesis (under the qualifier) suffices | introduced |
| Sh_K | DEF | Shape: `(c_F, c_G, t_F, t_G, idem)` | introduced |
| ShapeWellFormedness | DEF | Syntactic well-formedness: four explicit implications `c_F = 0 ⟹ t_F = -`, `t_F = - ⟹ c_F = 0`, and symmetric pair for G; antecedents test the literal registry values `0` and `-` (distinct from `0|1`, `A_doc`, `A_rel`, `A`); registry admits only well-formed shapes; admits Provenance's `(1, 0|1, A, A, ⊤)` | introduced |
| match | DEF | Cardinality match predicate over `{0, 1, *, 0\|1}` | introduced |
| T_cat | DEF | Typed-relation catalog: distinguished `T_cat ⊆ T_admissible` up to `~`; lifetime-constant (fixed at `Σ_init` and unchanged across reachable states), enforcing the empty-baseline assumption of Sh0–Sh4 inductions | introduced |
| shape | DEF | Shape registry `T_cat → Shape`, per-class constant, lifetime-constant | introduced |
| conf_K^Σ | DEF | State-indexed conformance predicate; monotone along `⊑̂` | introduced |
| from_K^Σ, to_K^Σ | DEF | Total set-valued slot accessors | introduced |
| from₁, to₁ | DEF | Point-valued slot accessors (defined when c = 1) | introduced |
| from₁⁻, to₁⁻ | DEF | Optional point-valued slot accessors (defined when c = 0\|1) | introduced |
| Sh-conf | AXIOM | ShapeConformanceAxiom — Emit_K (relational-layer op) rejects unregistered types and non-conformant emissions; binds Emit_K, not K.λ; returns `⊥` on failure (extended return type `(Σ' × A_rel^{Σ'}) ∪ {⊥}`) | introduced |
| LinkAddressNotPrefixOfEmit | LEMMA | At any state Σ reachable from `Σ_init` under the *Emit_K routing commitment*, every `b ∈ dom(Σ.L)` paired with every `d ∈ dom(Σ.M)` satisfies `b ⋠ a_emit(Σ, d)`. Generalized to arbitrary link-store addresses (not just retraction-tuple G-slot witnesses) so that both consumption sites in the EffectiveWpSimplification Corollary — discharging `NoCraftedSpanReachesD(Σ, d)` for prior R-tuples and discharging the `(K ≁ R ∨ a_emit(Σ, d) ∉ coverage(G))` disjunct's `K ~ R` arm for the new emission's G — fall under one statement. Case split: per-home distinctness via the *Uniform link sub-allocator chain length* scaffolding clause + T10a.7 + T3; cross-home distinctness via L1 + L1a's home-from-zero-positions reading, with the *Document address structure* scaffolding clause supplying `zeros(d) = 2`, the *Link subspace partition* scaffolding clause's `s_L > 0` securing the first-emission `zeros = 3` count, and inline zero-count additivity over prefix decomposition (NAT-card + componentwise agreement under Prefix). | introduced |
| EffectiveWpSimplification | COROLLARY | Under the framework's *Emit_K routing commitment*, conditional on substrate reach — i.e., at any call site where the full gate stack of gates 1–4 admits, equivalently Sh-conf admits *and* every applicable per-K discipline contract (SHCD/Sh4/FDD) does not suppress — `wp_086` simplifies to `d ∈ dom(Σ.M) ∧ K ∈ T_admissible`; the effective wp at the framework's gate simplifies to `d ∈ dom(Σ.M) ∧ K ∈ T_cat ∧ conf_K^Σ(F, G) ∧ Π_K(d, F, G, Σ)` where `Π_K` is the per-K discipline non-suppression conjunct (three conditional implications, one per discipline: `K-under-SHCD ⟹ d = d_K`, `K-under-Sh4 ⟹ C(F, G, Σ) = ∅`, `K-under-FDD ⟹ C_fd(F, Σ) = ∅`, each implication vacuously true at K not under the corresponding discipline). Holds at every call site (rejected calls short-circuit to `⊥` at the failing gate without reaching the substrate, which `wp_eff = false` encodes). Placed in its own section *after* the Target Domain section (Sh3), so that Sh1 and Sh3 are both available as previously-established inputs and no forward reference enters the proof. Proof body splits into three explicit steps: Step 1 discharges `NoCraftedSpanReachesD` by citing Sh1 and Sh3 at `K := R` to pin down prior R-tuple G-endset structure at the current state (replacing the implicit Sh-conf-at-past-emission + R2 + monotonicity bridge with the named preservation theorems), then applies the Lemma at each prior R-tuple's G-slot address; Step 2 case-splits on `K ≁ R` vs `K ~ R` and, in the `K ~ R` case, applies the Lemma at the *new* emission's G-slot address (legal under the Lemma's generalization); Step 3 assembles `wp_eff` from `wp_086` and the per-gate admit conjuncts (gates 1–4), with the per-K discipline non-suppression conjunct `Π_K` capturing the gates 1, 3 admit conditions as conditional implications. The `Π_K` conjunct is necessary for sufficiency of `wp_eff` toward the named postcondition `(a, F, G) ∈ A_K^{Σ'}`: a contract-suppressed call satisfies the prior `d ∈ dom(Σ.M) ∧ K ∈ T_cat ∧ conf_K^Σ(F, G)` form but deposits no new tuple, and `Π_K` closes this gap by evaluating to false at the suppression. | introduced |
| NullifyActiveSubsetCompatibility | COROLLARY | Under the *Sh4 idempotency contract* with R registered in `T_cat`, every `Nullify(Σ, d_retr, a)` call satisfying ASN-0086's P0/P1/P2 preconditions delivers the active-subset content of ASN-0086's Nullify postcondition — (i) single-tuple scope `{t : a ≼ t} ∩ A_rel^{Σ_target} = {a}` and (ii) `a ∈ nullified(Σ_target)` stable under R6a — whether the contract's clause (iii) admits a fresh `(Σ', _)` (with `Σ_target = Σ'`) or clause (ii) suppresses to `⊥` (with `Σ_target = Σ`). Case-split on the firing contract clause; Case A by ASN-0086's substrate-level argument under R0a + R6a; Case B from `a ∈ nullified(Σ)` derived through the candidate-set witness `τ_prior ∈ L_R^Σ` plus Definition (nullified, ASN-0086) and R6a. Audit-slice multiplicity is *not* preserved and the framework records this loss as a deliberate set-semantics commitment of the `shape(R) = (*, 1, A, A_rel, ⊤)` registration with `idem = ⊤`. | introduced |
| SubstrateConsumerActiveSubsetCompatibility | LEMMA | General framework-level companion to NullifyActiveSubsetCompatibility: characterizes the complete set of ASN-0086 surfaces compatible with Sh-conf's `⊥`-extension. A surface `S` destructuring `Emit_K`'s return tuple with postcondition `φ_S(Σ_target)` over the relational-layer state is *active-subset-content-preserving* iff `φ_S` satisfies (α) substrate-level success path discharge at `Σ_target := Σ'` via ASN-0086's standard argument + the framework's preservation theorems, and (β) layer-level rejection path discharge at `Σ_target := Σ` via (β.i) a prior-tuple witness or (β.ii) vacuous satisfaction. Consumed by every row of the *Per-consumer compatibility commitments* table: NullifyActiveSubsetCompatibility is the load-bearing instance at Row 1 (Nullify alias); Row 2 (direct relational-layer Emit_K calls) inherits compatibility via this Lemma; Row 3 (future ASN-0086 composites) qualifies when its `φ_S` satisfies the relational-layer-state-only condition; Rows 4–5 (Observe_K, K.λ) fall outside the destructure-Emit_K-return scope and apply vacuously. The Lemma records the framework's *compatibility envelope* as a load-bearing scope statement: surfaces outside the envelope require explicit per-surface compatibility commitments. | introduced |
| Sh0 | LEMMA | FromSlotCanonicalAndCardinalityFixed — proof covers both `→` and `↦ \ →`, via the *Emit_K routing commitment* | introduced |
| Sh1 | LEMMA | ToSlotCanonicalAndCardinalityFixed — G-side analog of Sh0; proof exhibits Sh-conf clauses (b) and (c) at the substituted sites | introduced |
| Sh2 | LEMMA | FromSlotTargetRestricted — `slot_addrs(F) ⊆ t_F^Σ` on every tuple | introduced |
| Sh3 | LEMMA | ToSlotTargetRestricted — symmetric to Sh2; commutes with retraction (`A_K ⊆ L_K`) | introduced |
| SlotAccessorTotality | LEMMA | When `c = 1`, the point accessor is a total function | introduced |
| Sh4 | LEMMA | IdempotencyDiscipline — at-most-one active duplicate when `idem = ⊤`; theorem under the *Sh4 idempotency contract* (single-process substrate scope: atomicity of clauses (i)–(iii) reduces to within-call sequentiality between `Observe_K` and the substrate K.λ-step); universal scope clarified (off-diagonal substance, diagonal trivial); proof covers A/B/C plus Case D (K=R simultaneous addition and contraction, with the structural bound `|leaving| ≤ 1` derived from R0a + R1 + PrefixSpanCoverage making the simultaneous step at most `+1, −1`); Case A simplified to rely on the case-equation `A_K^{Σ'} = A_K^Σ` alone, with the principal-transitions enumeration retained as expository orientation rather than a load-bearing case analysis | introduced |
| Sh5 | META | TemplateCatalog — hand-curated per-shape template families; status split into META observation (no procedure derives templates from arbitrary shapes) and META discipline (templates depend only on shape + K's name + named accessors); base/opt-in/parametric criterion formalized | introduced |
| SingleHomeCoverageDiscipline | DEF | Per-K opt-in extension at NonIdempotentDirectedPair securing `emission_order`; structurally eligible at any K with `shape(K) = (1, 1, A_doc, A_doc, ⊥)` plus the per-K registration, with no semantic-role precondition; allows non-K relations at the same home as long as K is single-home; realized through the *single-home commitment* layer-discipline contract | introduced |
| single-home commitment | DEF | The layer-discipline contract realizing SingleHomeCoverageDiscipline. Single-step protocol: rejects `Emit_K(Σ, d, F, G)` outright when `d ≠ d_K`; admits when `d = d_K`. No Observe step required (home check is literal-equality against a per-K registration constant); atomicity trivial. Preservation theorem establishes the homed-set commitment `(A τ ∈ L_K^Σ :: home(addr(τ)) = d_K)` inductively, with Case A/B/C structure analogous to Sh4 and FDD; companion property `S_d ⊆ {chain elements at d_K}` follows for every `d ∈ A_doc^Σ` | introduced |
| FunctionalDependencyDiscipline | DEF | Per-K commitment (strictly stronger than Sh4) for DirectedPair: at most one active tuple per from-slot value; secures the singleton-returning `K_target_of` accessor | introduced |
| Sh4 idempotency contract | DEF | The layer-discipline contract realizing Sh4. Observe-then-Emit protocol clauses (i)–(iii) the layer commits to for idempotent K; clause (i) uses the two-step procedure (i.a) finite-argument `Observe_K(slot_addrs(F), slot_addrs(G), oper)` over-approximation + (i.b) exact-slot-equality post-filter; atomically scoped at the `~`-equivalence class of K | introduced |
| FDD functional-dependency contract | DEF | The layer-discipline contract realizing FunctionalDependencyDiscipline. Observe-then-Emit protocol with from-slot-only candidate set `C_fd`; same two-step procedure as the *Sh4 idempotency contract*; same atomicity scope as Sh4; structurally exclusive with SingleHomeCoverageDiscipline by `idem` flag | introduced |

(Earlier drafts called this *content-side scaffolding*; the union spans both link-side, content-side, and document-side properties, so the name has been retired in favor of the substrate-conforming-layer reading.) | introduced |

Subsequent prose cites this commitment by name to disambiguate from the four other framework commitments | introduced |


## Open Questions

Each item is tagged with one of three kinds:
- **[design choice]** — an unresolved design decision the framework has not yet made;
- **[refinement candidate]** — a possible elaboration of the catalog or interface, not load-bearing for the current framework;
- **[scope boundary]** — a structural limitation the framework commits to; further work would extend the framework's scope rather than fill in a gap.

Items in the third category are listed here, not in a separate Scope Limitations section, because the boundary is identifiable per item and grouping by kind helps the reader sort what is open work from what is settled.

- **[refinement candidate]** Should `(0, 0)` shapes be admitted? A relation with `c_F = c_G = 0` would be a single-tuple existence flag whose only role is "this event happened" without any from/to attribution; whether the substrate has any such relations is unclear, and the slot accessors degenerate to constants on it.
- **[design choice]** Provenance's `c_G = 0|1` mixes shapes — should it be split into two distinct canonical shapes (Provenance-with-target and Provenance-attribution-only), each generating separate templates? The current formulation requires the optional accessor `to₁⁻` to handle both cases in a single template.
- **[refinement candidate]** Is idempotency recoverable from cardinality plus target-domain alone, or is it an independent axis? Empirically the canonical catalog has both `idem = ⊤` (DirectedPair) and `idem = ⊥` (Coverage, Comment) at `(1, 1, A_doc, A_doc, _)`, suggesting independence.
- **[design choice]** Should the per-K opt-in registry — currently FunctionalDependencyDiscipline and SingleHomeCoverageDiscipline — be promoted to a sixth shape-tuple component, so that registrations with and without the discipline become structurally distinct rows rather than the same row with different opt-in flags? The current catalog encodes them as opt-in extensions atop a five-component shape; a sixth component would make the shape registry exhaustive but inflate the canonical-shape space considerably.
- **[design choice]** The shape constraint `slot_addrs(F) ⊆ t_F^Σ` requires slot addresses to be already-allocated at emission time. This precludes shape-conformant emissions whose slot addresses are *ghost* (currently outside `A^Σ`, possibly to be allocated later). L9 (TypeGhostPermission, ASN-0043) permits ghost spans in endsets; the shape framework restricts this to *non-slot* uses only. Whether future shape families should admit ghost-targeting slot semantics — and under what state-dependent conformance rule — is an open design question.
- **[refinement candidate]** Do *composite shapes* (relations whose F or G is itself constrained by another relation's content) require a new restriction axis, or do they decompose into existing primitives plus auxiliary predicates expressible in the current template language?
- **[scope boundary]** What guarantees the shape registry stays consistent across processes? Lifetime constancy is asserted as a substrate-level commitment within a single process; cross-process consistency (e.g., concurrent shape re-registration in a distributed substrate) is not addressed. Relatedly, the *Sh4 idempotency contract* and the *FDD functional-dependency contract* are both *committed* to single-process substrates by design — their atomicity premise reduces to within-call sequentiality between `Observe_K` and the substrate K.λ-step within a single `Emit_K` call (see the Sh4 contract's *Scope: single-process substrate* clause). This is a framework-scope commitment, not a topic awaiting future investigation: porting the framework to a multi-process substrate with racing Sh4-emitters at coverage-equivalent K's would require a coordination protocol (e.g., distributed lock at the `~`-equivalence class scope) outside the current framework. Characterizing the minimum protocol that preserves Sh4 in the multi-process setting would *extend* the framework's scope; this item is listed here to flag the boundary, not as an unresolved internal question.


## Appendix: Local NAT Primitives

This appendix discharges the two ℕ-level primitives the framework cites — NAT-card and NAT-sub — from the foundation's NAT axioms (NAT-closure, NAT-order, NAT-discrete, NAT-addcompat, NAT-wellorder) plus two background ℕ-arithmetic facts and three Peano-core supplements. The material is routed here rather than to the body of the document because the framework's substantive content is the shape discipline; readers tracking the shape framework's argument may treat NAT-card and NAT-sub as named cardinality and partial-subtraction primitives without consulting their derivations.

*Background ℕ-arithmetic facts beyond the listed foundation axioms.* The NAT-sub derivation below invokes two facts about ℕ-arithmetic — *ℕ-commutativity of addition* (`m + n = n + m`) and *ℕ-associativity of addition* (`(m + n) + p = m + (n + p)`) — that are not literally enumerated among the foundation's listed NAT axioms (NAT-closure, NAT-order, NAT-discrete, NAT-addcompat, NAT-wellorder). We derive both here from the listed axioms supplemented by three additional Peano-core clauses: (Peano-rec), which defines the recursive behavior of `+` on its successor argument; (Peano-zero-least), which pins `0` as the least element of ℕ; and (Peano-pred), which asserts that every non-zero `m ∈ ℕ` has a predecessor (i.e., successor is surjective on positives). NAT-closure asserts that binary addition is total (`m + n ∈ ℕ`) but does not axiomatize its computational behavior, so the recursion clause is the minimum gap-bridge for ℕ-commutativity and ℕ-associativity; the listed axioms also do not force `0` to be the least element of ℕ (NAT-discrete and NAT-order are satisfied by, e.g., ℤ), so (Peano-zero-least) is the minimum gap-bridge for the NAT-sub base case; and the listed axioms — NAT-discrete (which only restricts values strictly between `m` and `m + 1`) and NAT-order (a strict total order) — jointly do *not* force the successor map `n ↦ n + 1` to be surjective on the positives, so (Peano-pred) is the minimum gap-bridge for NAT-sub's *Case B*, which constructs `m'` such that `m = m' + 1` when `m ≥ 1`.

*(Peano-rec) Successor-recursion for binary addition.* `m + (n + 1) = (m + n) + 1` for all `m, n ∈ ℕ`. This is the standard Peano clause that defines binary addition recursively on its second argument; it is one of three added axioms in this appendix and is standard in any Peano-style ℕ. The framework cites it as a Peano-core upstream supplement to NAT-closure; a future draft may extend the foundation to enumerate (Peano-rec) explicitly.

*(Peano-zero-least) Zero is the least element of ℕ.* `(A n ∈ ℕ :: 0 ≤ n)`. The listed NAT axioms (NAT-closure, NAT-order, NAT-discrete, NAT-addcompat, NAT-wellorder) do not uniquely pin ℕ as the non-negative integers with `0` as least element — they are equally satisfied by, e.g., the carrier `ℕ_a := {a} ∪ ℕ` where `a` is a new symbol not in ℕ, equipped with the order extension `a < 0 < 1 < 2 < ...` (so `a` is the unique element of `ℕ_a` strictly below `0` and is the global minimum) and addition extended by `0 + a := a` (NAT-closure's additive identity at `n := a`), `a + 1 := 0` (so NAT-discrete's clause `m ≤ n < m + 1 ⟹ n = m` at `m := a` reads `a ≤ n < 0`, satisfied only by `n = a` since the extension places `a` immediately below `0` with no intermediate element), `a + (n + 1) := (a + n) + 1` for `n ≥ 1` (the (Peano-rec) clause propagated from `a + 1 = 0` to all positive arguments — equivalently, `a + n = n − 1` for `n ≥ 1`), and `a + 0 := a` (right-identity at the new element). NAT-wellorder is satisfied: every non-empty `S ⊆ ℕ_a` either contains `a` (whence `a` is the least element by the order extension) or is a non-empty subset of ℕ (whence its least exists by the standard well-ordering of ℕ). NAT-addcompat is satisfied because the addition extension preserves order-compatibility (verifiable case-by-case on whether the operands include `a` or lie entirely in ℕ). Note that the integers ℤ — although they satisfy NAT-closure, NAT-order, NAT-discrete, and NAT-addcompat — do *not* satisfy NAT-wellorder (the subset `{0, −1, −2, …} ⊆ ℤ` is non-empty but admits no least element under `<`), so ℤ is *not* a valid model exhibiting (Peano-zero-least)-independence; the `ℕ_a` example above is. Under `ℕ_a`, `0` is *not* the least element of the carrier — `a < 0` — so (Peano-zero-least) fails. This model exhibits that the listed axioms admit carriers in which `0` is not at the bottom; (Peano-zero-least) is exactly the supplement that excludes such carriers. We therefore admit (Peano-zero-least) as the second added Peano-core axiom of this appendix, alongside (Peano-rec). The clause is standard in any Peano-style ℕ; a future draft may extend the foundation to enumerate (Peano-zero-least) explicitly. NAT-sub's *Base* case below cites (Peano-zero-least) to conclude `n = 0` from `n ≤ 0`: under (Peano-zero-least), `n ≥ 0` for every `n ∈ ℕ`, so `n ≤ 0` combined with NAT-order's `≤` defining clause `m ≤ n ⟺ m < n ∨ m = n` forces `n < 0 ∨ n = 0`; (Peano-zero-least) excludes the strict alternative (it would contradict `0 ≤ n` by NAT-order's irreflexivity composed with transitivity), leaving `n = 0`.

*(Peano-pred) Predecessor existence on positives.* `(A m ∈ ℕ : m ≥ 1 :: (E m' ∈ ℕ :: m = m' + 1))`. The listed NAT axioms (NAT-closure, NAT-order, NAT-discrete, NAT-addcompat, NAT-wellorder), together with (Peano-zero-least), do not jointly entail that every positive `m ∈ ℕ` lies in the image of the successor map `n ↦ n + 1`. *Why the listed axioms are insufficient.* NAT-discrete's contrapositive (`m < n ⟹ m + 1 ≤ n`) bounds positions between strictly-ordered ℕ-elements but does not *construct* a predecessor: given `m ≥ 1`, NAT-discrete's contrapositive at `0, m` gives `0 + 1 = 1 ≤ m`, which after NAT-closure's additive identity reduces to `1 ≤ m` — a non-strict order relation between `1` and `m`, not the existential `(E m' :: m = m' + 1)`. NAT-order supplies trichotomy and transitivity but no construction of intermediate elements. NAT-wellorder supplies least-element on non-empty subsets and underwrites induction; an inductive derivation of (Peano-pred) is possible via strong induction on the predicate `P(m) := m = 0 ∨ (E m' :: m = m' + 1)` (P(0) holds trivially by the first disjunct; if P(n) holds, then P(n + 1) holds with witness `m' := n` — but this argument *itself uses the successor's surjectivity at the inductive step*: the step shows P(n + 1) by exhibiting `m' = n` such that `(m') + 1 = n + 1`, which presupposes that successor is well-defined on `n`, not that arbitrary positives admit a predecessor). A non-circular inductive derivation would require an external well-founded measure independent of the successor structure — the framework does not commit to one, and the standard Peano formulation supplies the surjectivity-on-positives axiom directly. We therefore admit (Peano-pred) as the third added Peano-core axiom of this appendix, alongside (Peano-rec) and (Peano-zero-least). The clause is standard in any Peano-style ℕ — equivalent to "every `m ∈ ℕ` is either `0` or a successor", which is one of Peano's original five postulates; a future draft may extend the foundation to enumerate (Peano-pred) explicitly. *Uniqueness of the predecessor.* The witness `m'` is unique: if `m' + 1 = m'' + 1 = m`, then by successor injectivity (derived in this appendix from NAT-order axioms — see *Successor injectivity from NAT axioms* below), `m' = m''`. *Strict-decrease property used by NAT-sub.* The predecessor `m'` satisfies `m' < m`: from `m = m' + 1` and NAT-addcompat's strict-successor inequality `m' < m' + 1 = m`, NAT-order's irreflexivity-and-trichotomy yield `m' < m`. This is what licenses NAT-sub's invocation of the IH at `m' < m` in *Case B*. *Order-conformance with the case hypothesis.* When NAT-sub's *Case B* additionally hypothesizes `n < m`, the predecessor `m'` satisfies `n ≤ m'`: from `n < m = m' + 1`, NAT-discrete's contrapositive at `n, m' + 1` gives `n + 1 ≤ m' + 1`; by successor injectivity (applied to the inequality `n + 1 ≤ m' + 1`'s case-equality branch, or via NAT-addcompat's order-compatibility of addition reversed at the `+ 1` argument) reduces this to `n ≤ m'`. The two consequences — strict-decrease `m' < m` and order-conformance `n ≤ m'` — together discharge NAT-sub Case B's invocation of the IH at the predecessor.

*Derivation of an auxiliary bundle: successor injectivity, right-identity, and successor-distributivity.* Three intermediate lemmas required for the commutativity proof — *successor injectivity* (`(A a, b ∈ ℕ :: a + 1 = b + 1 ⟹ a = b)`), *right-identity* (`(A n ∈ ℕ :: n + 0 = n)`), and *successor-distributivity-on-first-argument* (`(A m, n ∈ ℕ :: (m + 1) + n = (m + n) + 1)`) — are derived in sequence. Right-identity's inductive step does *not* close from (Peano-rec) and the induction hypothesis alone: (Peano-rec) at substitution `(m, n) := (n + 1, 0)` gives `(n + 1) + (0 + 1) = ((n + 1) + 0) + 1`, which simplifies via NAT-closure's `0 + 1 = 1` (the `n := 1` instance of the additive identity) to `(n + 1) + 1 = ((n + 1) + 0) + 1`; cancelling `+ 1` to extract `n + 1 = (n + 1) + 0` requires successor injectivity, which must be supplied separately. We therefore derive successor injectivity first from the listed NAT axioms, then use it to close right-identity, then derive successor-distributivity from (Peano-rec) and right-identity.

*Successor injectivity from NAT axioms.* `(A a, b ∈ ℕ :: a + 1 = b + 1 ⟹ a = b)`. By NAT-order's trichotomy on `a, b ∈ ℕ`, exactly one of `a < b`, `a = b`, `b < a` holds; we rule out the strict cases. Suppose `a < b` (the case `b < a` is symmetric). NAT-discrete's contrapositive yields `a + 1 ≤ b`: derivation — assume `¬(a + 1 ≤ b)`; NAT-order's trichotomy on `a + 1, b` combined with NAT-order's defining clause `m ≤ n ⟺ m < n ∨ m = n` forces `b < a + 1` (the alternatives `a + 1 < b` and `a + 1 = b` each contradict the assumption); combined with `a ≤ b` (from `a < b` via the same defining clause), we obtain `a ≤ b < a + 1`; NAT-discrete forces `b = a`, contradicting `a < b` by NAT-order's irreflexivity. So `a + 1 ≤ b`. By NAT-addcompat's strict-successor inequality at `n := b`, `b < b + 1`. Composing `a + 1 ≤ b` and `b < b + 1` (NAT-order's defining clause unfolds `a + 1 ≤ b` to `a + 1 < b ∨ a + 1 = b`; in the first sub-case NAT-order's transitivity of `<` with `b < b + 1` gives `a + 1 < b + 1`; in the second sub-case substitution gives `a + 1 = b < b + 1`, hence `a + 1 < b + 1`) yields `a + 1 < b + 1`, contradicting `a + 1 = b + 1` by NAT-order's irreflexivity. The strict cases excluded, trichotomy forces `a = b`. ∎

*Right-identity from successor injectivity and (Peano-rec).* `(A n ∈ ℕ :: n + 0 = n)`. By induction on `n` over NAT-wellorder. *Base* (`n = 0`): NAT-closure's additive identity at the `n := 0` instance gives `0 + 0 = 0`. ✓ *Step* (`n → n + 1`): show `(n + 1) + 0 = n + 1`. (Peano-rec) at substitution `(m, n) := (n + 1, 0)` gives `(n + 1) + (0 + 1) = ((n + 1) + 0) + 1`; NAT-closure's `0 + 1 = 1` (the `n := 1` instance of the additive identity) simplifies the LHS to `(n + 1) + 1`, yielding `(n + 1) + 1 = ((n + 1) + 0) + 1`. Successor injectivity at `a := n + 1, b := (n + 1) + 0` yields `n + 1 = (n + 1) + 0`, i.e., `(n + 1) + 0 = n + 1`. ✓ (The IH `n + 0 = n` is not consumed by the step; it closes from successor injectivity and (Peano-rec) directly. The induction is structural — NAT-wellorder requires that the step *be made* at each `n`, not that the IH be load-bearing — so right-identity is established uniformly over all `n ∈ ℕ`.)

*Successor-distributivity from (Peano-rec) and right-identity.* `(A m, n ∈ ℕ :: (m + 1) + n = (m + n) + 1)`. By induction on `n` over NAT-wellorder. *Base* (`n = 0`): `(m + 1) + 0 = m + 1` (right-identity at `m + 1`, established above) `= (m + 0) + 1` (right-identity at `m`, substituted right-to-left under `+ 1` via reflexivity of `=`). ✓ *Step* (`n → n + 1`): assume `(m + 1) + n = (m + n) + 1` (IH). Then `(m + 1) + (n + 1) = ((m + 1) + n) + 1` (Peano-rec at substitution `(m + 1, n)`) `= ((m + n) + 1) + 1` (IH applied under `+ 1`) `= (m + (n + 1)) + 1` (Peano-rec at substitution `(m, n)`, applied right-to-left). ✓ Universally quantifying over `m` closes the step.

*Derivation of ℕ-commutativity.* By induction on `m` over NAT-wellorder. Base (`m = 0`): `0 + n = n` (NAT-closure additive identity); `n + 0 = n` (right-identity, established above); so `0 + n = n + 0`. Step (`m → m + 1`): assume `m + n = n + m`. Then `(m + 1) + n = (m + n) + 1` (successor-distributivity, established above) `= (n + m) + 1` (IH) `= n + (m + 1)` (Peano-rec at `n`). ✓ Universally quantifying over `n` closes the step. ∎

*Derivation of ℕ-associativity.* By induction on `p` over NAT-wellorder. Base (`p = 0`): `(m + n) + 0 = m + n` (right-identity) `= m + (n + 0)` (right-identity applied inside, then reflexivity). Step (`p → p + 1`): assume `(m + n) + p = m + (n + p)`. Then `(m + n) + (p + 1) = ((m + n) + p) + 1` (Peano-rec) `= (m + (n + p)) + 1` (IH) `= m + ((n + p) + 1)` (Peano-rec, applied right-to-left) `= m + (n + (p + 1))` (Peano-rec, again applied right-to-left, at `n`). ✓ Universally quantifying over `m, n` closes the step. ∎

NAT-sub's Case A invokes ℕ-commutativity (now derived) to bridge `0 + m = m` to `m + 0 = m`; Case B and the strict-monotonicity-of-addition step invoke ℕ-associativity (now derived) — specifically the case `(n + p_1) + 1 = n + (p_1 + 1)`, which is also a direct application of (Peano-rec). Downstream proofs that consume NAT-sub inherit the (Peano-rec) dependency transitively through the appendix.

*NAT-card derivation.* For any finite `S ⊆ ℕ`, a strictly-increasing enumeration `f : {1, …, k} → ℕ` with `image(f) = S` is constructed. *External well-founded measure.* "Finite `S ⊆ ℕ`" carries by hypothesis a witness `N ∈ ℕ` such that `S ⊆ {0, 1, …, N}` (the upper bound is part of the meaning of "finite subset of ℕ" at this framework's level; equivalently, NAT-wellorder applied to the set of upper bounds `{m ∈ ℕ : (A x ∈ S :: x ≤ m)}` — non-empty by the finiteness witness — supplies a least upper bound). The construction proceeds by strong induction on `N`, with the IH being "for every `S' ⊆ ℕ` with `S' ⊆ {0, …, N'}` and `N' < N`, the construction terminates and produces a unique strictly-increasing enumeration of `S'." The induction parameter `N` is well-founded by NAT-order's strict total order on ℕ (descending chains terminate by NAT-wellorder); it is external to the construction, so neither `|·|` nor `k` enters the termination measure. *Base.* `N = 0`: `S ⊆ {0}`, so `S ∈ {∅, {0}}`. For `S = ∅`, take `k = 0` and the empty enumeration; for `S = {0}`, take `k = 1` and `f(1) = 0`. *Step.* `N ≥ 1`: case-split on `N ∈ S`. *Case A — `N ∉ S`.* `S ⊆ {0, …, N − 1}`, so the IH at `N − 1 < N` supplies a unique strictly-increasing enumeration of `S` directly. *Case B — `N ∈ S`.* The residual `S' := S \ {N}` satisfies `S' ⊆ {0, …, N − 1}`, so the IH at `N − 1 < N` supplies a unique strictly-increasing `f' : {1, …, k − 1} → ℕ` with `image(f') = S'`. Append `N` at position `k`: `f(i) := f'(i)` for `1 ≤ i ≤ k − 1` and `f(k) := N`. Strictness at the seam: `f(k − 1) ∈ S' ⊆ {0, …, N − 1}`, so `f(k − 1) ≤ N − 1 < N = f(k)` by NAT-order. The pair `(k, f)` is unique: any strictly-increasing enumeration must place the largest element at the last position (NAT-order's strict total order forces this), so `f(k) = max(S) = N` in Case B and the prefix is determined by the IH. Define `|S| := k`.

*Additivity over disjoint unions — induction on `|S₁ ∪ S₂|`.* For disjoint finite `S₁, S₂ ⊆ ℕ`, `|S₁ ∪ S₂| = |S₁| + |S₂|`. *Why a trichotomy-on-`(max(S₁), min(S₂))` argument does not suffice.* An earlier draft attempted to derive additivity by routing through NAT-order's trichotomy applied to `(max(S₁), min(S₂))`: disjointness rules out equality, leaving "one of `max(S₁) < min(S₂)` or `max(S₁) > min(S₂)` holds" and re-framing the latter as `max(S₂) < min(S₁)` to enable a "concatenate `f₁` then `f₂`" construction. The re-framing is unsound — `max(S₁) > min(S₂)` does *not* entail `max(S₂) < min(S₁)`; the negation of the conjunction of these two inequalities is the *interleaved* case, where `max(S₁) > min(S₂)` and `max(S₂) > min(S₁)` simultaneously hold (e.g., `S₁ = {2, 7}`, `S₂ = {3, 5}` gives `max(S₁) = 7 > 3 = min(S₂)` and `max(S₂) = 5 > 2 = min(S₁)`, so neither concatenation produces a strictly-increasing enumeration). The trichotomy argument therefore covers only the non-interleaved sub-case (one set entirely below the other), not the general disjoint finite ℕ-subset pair. The derivation below replaces the trichotomy-on-extremes route with a maximum-extraction induction on `|S₁ ∪ S₂|` that covers interleaved configurations uniformly. *Derivation.* By strong induction on `|S₁ ∪ S₂|` over NAT-wellorder (the induction parameter `|S₁ ∪ S₂| ∈ ℕ` is well-defined by NAT-card's enumeration on the finite set `S₁ ∪ S₂`, which exists by the same finiteness witness that names `S₁` and `S₂` as finite ℕ-subsets — `S₁ ∪ S₂ ⊆ {0, ..., max(N_1, N_2)}` where `N_1, N_2` are the upper-bound witnesses for `S₁, S₂`; descending chains in `|S₁ ∪ S₂|` terminate by NAT-wellorder on ℕ). *Base* (`|S₁ ∪ S₂| = 0`): NAT-card's enumeration of the empty set has length 0, so `S₁ ∪ S₂ = ∅`, hence `S₁ = S₂ = ∅` (both are subsets of the empty set), hence `|S₁| = |S₂| = 0`, hence `|S₁ ∪ S₂| = 0 = 0 + 0 = |S₁| + |S₂|`. ✓ *Step* (`|S₁ ∪ S₂| ≥ 1`): NAT-card applied to `S₁ ∪ S₂` produces a strictly-increasing enumeration `f : {1, …, k} → ℕ` with `image(f) = S₁ ∪ S₂` and `k = |S₁ ∪ S₂| ≥ 1`. Let `m := f(k)`; by NAT-card's uniqueness clause "any strictly-increasing enumeration must place the largest element at the last position", `m = max(S₁ ∪ S₂)` is well-defined. By disjointness `S₁ ∩ S₂ = ∅`, exactly one of `m ∈ S₁`, `m ∈ S₂` holds (the disjunction is exhaustive because `m ∈ S₁ ∪ S₂`, and disjointness excludes the conjunctive case `m ∈ S₁ ∧ m ∈ S₂`). The two sub-cases are symmetric in side-labels; we walk Sub-case A and indicate the symmetric Sub-case B at the end. *Sub-case A* (`m ∈ S₁`): set `S₁' := S₁ \ {m}`. Three observations close the inductive step. *(α) Disjointness of `S₁'` and `S₂`.* `S₁' ⊆ S₁` and `S₁ ∩ S₂ = ∅` give `S₁' ∩ S₂ ⊆ S₁ ∩ S₂ = ∅`, hence `S₁' ∩ S₂ = ∅`. *(β) Set identity `S₁' ∪ S₂ = (S₁ ∪ S₂) \ {m}`.* By disjointness, `m ∈ S₁ ⟹ m ∉ S₂`, so `m ∉ S₁' ∪ S₂` (since `m ∉ S₁'` by construction and `m ∉ S₂` by disjointness). Conversely, any element of `(S₁ ∪ S₂) \ {m}` lies in either `S₁ \ {m} = S₁'` or `S₂`, hence in `S₁' ∪ S₂`. The two inclusions give the set identity. *(γ) Length-decrement-by-one in NAT-card's enumeration.* Stripping the largest element from a strictly-increasing enumeration produces the strictly-increasing enumeration of the residual: NAT-card's enumeration of `S₁ ∪ S₂` is `f : {1, …, k} → ℕ` with `f(k) = m`; restricting to `f|_{1..k − 1}` is strictly-increasing with image `image(f) \ {m} = (S₁ ∪ S₂) \ {m}`. By NAT-card's uniqueness, the residual's enumeration length is exactly `k − 1`, giving `|(S₁ ∪ S₂) \ {m}| = k − 1 = |S₁ ∪ S₂| − 1`. Composing with (β), `|S₁' ∪ S₂| = |S₁ ∪ S₂| − 1`. The same length-decrement-by-one argument applied at `S₁` (whose enumeration also places `m` last by NAT-card's uniqueness — `m = max(S₁ ∪ S₂) ≥ max(S₁)` and `m ∈ S₁` together force `m = max(S₁)`, so `S₁`'s enumeration places `m` at its last position) yields `|S₁'| = |S₁| − 1`. *Closing the step.* The IH at `|S₁' ∪ S₂| < |S₁ ∪ S₂|` (strict inequality by (γ)) gives `|S₁' ∪ S₂| = |S₁'| + |S₂|`. NAT-card's appending construction at `S₁` — appending `m` at position `k` to `S₁'`'s enumeration produces `S₁`'s enumeration — yields the additive identity `|S₁'| + 1 = |S₁|` directly, the additive restatement of (γ)'s enumeration-length fact, used here to avoid NAT-sub (which is derived later in the Appendix). Composing via ℕ-associativity, ℕ-commutativity (both derived in the Appendix above), and this additive identity: `|S₁ ∪ S₂| = |S₁' ∪ S₂| + 1 = (|S₁'| + |S₂|) + 1 = |S₁'| + (|S₂| + 1) = |S₁'| + (1 + |S₂|) = (|S₁'| + 1) + |S₂| = |S₁| + |S₂|`. ✓ *Sub-case B* (`m ∈ S₂`): identical with `S₁` and `S₂` exchanged at every step — (α) becomes `S₁ ∩ S₂' = ∅` for `S₂' := S₂ \ {m}`; (β) becomes `S₁ ∪ S₂' = (S₁ ∪ S₂) \ {m}` with `m ∉ S₁` from disjointness; (γ) becomes `|S₁ ∪ S₂'| = |S₁ ∪ S₂| − 1` and `|S₂'| = |S₂| − 1`; the IH yields `|S₁ ∪ S₂'| = |S₁| + |S₂'|`; composing yields `|S₁ ∪ S₂| = |S₁| + |S₂|`. ✓ The induction closes; `|S₁ ∪ S₂| = |S₁| + |S₂|` holds for every disjoint finite `S₁, S₂ ⊆ ℕ`, including the interleaved configurations the trichotomy-on-extremes route did not cover. *Worked interleaved instance — `S₁ = {2, 7}`, `S₂ = {3, 5}`.* The configuration that breaks the trichotomy-on-extremes route discharges cleanly under the induction. `S₁ ∪ S₂ = {2, 3, 5, 7}` with NAT-card enumeration `(2, 3, 5, 7)` of length 4; `m = max(S₁ ∪ S₂) = 7 ∈ S₁`, so Sub-case A fires with `S₁' = {2}`, `S₂ = {3, 5}`. The IH applies at `|S₁' ∪ S₂| = |{2, 3, 5}| = 3 < 4`; at this level `S₁' ∪ S₂ = {2, 3, 5}` with `m' = max = 5 ∈ S₂`, so Sub-case B fires with `S₂'' = {3}`, `S₁' = {2}`. The IH applies at `|S₁' ∪ S₂''| = |{2, 3}| = 2 < 3`; at this level `m'' = max({2, 3}) = 3 ∈ S₂''`, so Sub-case B fires again with `S₂''' = ∅`, `S₁' = {2}`. The IH applies at `|S₁' ∪ S₂'''| = |{2}| = 1 < 2`; at this level `m''' = max({2}) = 2 ∈ S₁'`, so Sub-case A fires with `S₁'' = ∅`, `S₂''' = ∅`. The base case at `|S₁'' ∪ S₂'''| = |∅| = 0` returns `0 = 0 + 0`. Unwinding the recursion stack — `0 → 1 → 2 → 3 → 4` at the union-cardinality measure — yields `|S₁ ∪ S₂| = 4 = 2 + 2 = |S₁| + |S₂|`. The interleaving — the alternation between `m ∈ S₁` and `m ∈ S₂` at successive recursion levels (Sub-case A at level 0, Sub-case B at levels 1 and 2, Sub-case A at level 3) — is dispatched by the case-split at each step rather than by an ordering on extremes. *Subset-with-equal-cardinality identity.* The standard consequence — if `T ⊆ S` finite and `|T| = |S|`, then `T = S` — follows from the now-general additivity: if `T ⊂ S` strictly, the partition `S = T ⊔ (S \ T)` is disjoint by definition of set-difference, and `S \ T ≠ ∅` by strictness of the inclusion. The induction-based additivity above (which makes no `(max, min)`-ordering assumption on the two sides of the partition) gives `|S| = |T| + |S \ T|`; from `S \ T ≠ ∅`, NAT-card's enumeration of a non-empty finite ℕ-subset has length `≥ 1`, so `|S \ T| ≥ 1`; ℕ-arithmetic (NAT-addcompat strict-successor inequality plus order-compatibility of addition) gives `|T| + |S \ T| ≥ |T| + 1 > |T|`. Hence `|S| > |T|`, contradicting `|T| = |S|`. The partition `S = T ⊔ (S \ T)` may interleave `T` and `S \ T` arbitrarily in ℕ-position (e.g., `S = {1, 3, 5}` with `T = {3}` gives `S \ T = {1, 5}` interleaving `T = {3}` in ℕ); the induction-based additivity handles every such configuration uniformly via the `m ∈ T` vs `m ∈ S \ T` case-split at each step.

*NAT-sub derivation.* For `m, n ∈ ℕ` with `n ≤ m`, the set `T_{m, n} := {p ∈ ℕ : n + p = m}` is shown to be a singleton; define `m − n` as the unique element. *External well-founded measure for existence.* The induction parameter is `m` itself (not `m − n`, which would be circular). The construction proceeds by strong induction on `m`, with the IH being "for every `m' ∈ ℕ` with `m' < m` and every `n' ≤ m'`, there exists a unique `p' ∈ ℕ` with `n' + p' = m'`." *Existence.* By strong induction on `m`. *Base.* `m = 0`: by `n ≤ m = 0` and (Peano-zero-least) — `0 ≤ n` for every `n ∈ ℕ` — combined with NAT-order on ℕ, `n = 0` (per the derivation in the (Peano-zero-least) clause above: `n ≤ 0` and `0 ≤ n` jointly force `n = 0` by NAT-order's `≤` defining clause plus irreflexivity-via-transitivity); then `p = 0` satisfies `n + p = 0 + 0 = 0 = m` via NAT-closure's additive identity. *Step.* `m ≥ 1`: case-split on `n = m` versus `n < m`. *Case A — `n = m`.* `p = 0` satisfies `n + p = m + 0 = m` via *ℕ-commutativity* combined with NAT-closure's additive identity (`m + 0 = 0 + m = m`). *Case B — `n < m`.* By (Peano-pred) applied at `m ≥ 1` (the Step's case hypothesis), `m` has a unique predecessor `m' ∈ ℕ` with `m = m' + 1`; the strict-decrease consequence `m' < m` and the order-conformance consequence `n ≤ m'` (both derived in the (Peano-pred) clause above from the case hypothesis `n < m`) license the IH invocation. The IH at `m' < m` supplies a unique `p' ∈ ℕ` with `n + p' = m'`. Then `p := p' + 1` satisfies `n + p = n + (p' + 1) = (n + p') + 1 = m' + 1 = m` (using *ℕ-associativity* to step from `n + (p' + 1)` to `(n + p') + 1`).

*Uniqueness — strict-monotonicity-of-addition.* The uniqueness step rests on `p_1 < p_2 ⟹ n + p_1 < n + p_2`, which is *not* a direct consequence of NAT-addcompat alone (NAT-addcompat supplies the non-strict version `q ≤ r ⟹ n + q ≤ n + r`). Derivation: from `p_1 < p_2` and NAT-discrete's contrapositive (`m < n ⟹ m + 1 ≤ n`), obtain `p_1 + 1 ≤ p_2`. Apply NAT-addcompat at `q := p_1 + 1, r := p_2`: `n + (p_1 + 1) ≤ n + p_2`. By *ℕ-associativity*, `n + (p_1 + 1) = (n + p_1) + 1`, so `(n + p_1) + 1 ≤ n + p_2`. By NAT-addcompat's strict-successor inequality (`n + p_1 < (n + p_1) + 1`), composing yields `n + p_1 < n + p_2` via the standard `a < b ∧ b ≤ c ⟹ a < c` (NAT-order's trichotomy + transitivity). *Uniqueness applied.* If `n + p₁ = n + p₂ = m`, NAT-order's trichotomy on `p₁, p₂` rules out the strict cases (each contradicts the derived strict-monotonicity together with NAT-order's irreflexivity); trichotomy then forces `p₁ = p₂`. Define `m − n := p`.
